# SharpProof bug audit status

This file is the current, evidence-backed status ledger for the repository audit. It deliberately keeps only unresolved findings, accepted limitations, deferred security/integrity work, and rejected leads. Resolved findings are listed in the compact ledger at the end so that their fixes remain traceable without retaining superseded reports.

## Open and accepted findings

No non-security findings remain open after the TDD fixes recorded below. The deferred security/integrity items remain intentionally out of scope.

## Deferred by explicit scope

The following findings concern cybersecurity, raceable trust decisions, or filesystem durability/integrity. They are recorded for a separate security review and were not implemented in this audit, per the user's explicit no-cybersecurity instruction.

### 215. Trusted-attributes payload/hash binding race

**Status:** Deferred security review.

The analyzer hashes the file at a path after Roslyn has loaded the reference, without proving that both reads describe the same bytes.

### 271. Z3 pin/hash versus loaded-library identity

**Status:** Deferred security review.

The container contract validates bytes separately from the library later loaded by the native resolver.

### 272. Publication-path validation versus use

**Status:** Deferred security review.

Path identity is checked by a userspace walk and is not kernel-enforced against a concurrent symlink replacement.

### 273. Publication deletion durability

**Status:** Deferred integrity/durability review.

Reset/invalidation removes publication members without the full filesystem durability protocol required to survive a power loss.

## Rejected or reclassified leads

- **1-3:** `ArgumentNullGuard` assignments are intentional null-state narrowing/field initialization patterns, not correctness bugs.
- **4:** `LazyThreadSafetyMode.ExecutionAndPublication` already supplies the required synchronization; no race was reproduced.
- **5:** Documentation breadth is maintenance debt, not an independently reproducible product defect; the documentation audit is tracked separately.
- **6:** `RegisterCompilationEndAction` is a valid Roslyn registration API; the naming claim was based on a mistaken signature assumption.
- **275:** Exact `Contract.Result<T>` nullability matching is intentional contract identity behavior and is covered by binder tests.
- **279:** Profile/configuration disagreement is an intentional fail-closed policy boundary; changing it would weaken the configured verification contract.

### 289. [RESOLVED c1a8adb30] DataflowAnalysisResult.GetInputState/GetOutputState Skip the Project-Wide RequireIndex Convention, So Out-of-Range Block IDs Throw Indexer-Internal Exceptions That Never Name `blockId`

**Location**: `SharpProof.Dataflow\ForwardDataflowAnalysis.cs` (Lines 42-50); contrast the house pattern `SharpProof.Dataflow\DataflowGraph.cs` (Lines 105-131 and 134-140 routing GetBlock/GetPredecessors/GetSuccessors/IsCyclicBlock through `ArgumentNullGuard.RequireIndex`), guard implementation `SharpProof.Ir\ArgumentNullGuard.cs` (Lines 31-42, compiled into this assembly), conventions test `SharpProof.Dataflow.Test\ArgumentNullGuardBoundaryTests.cs` (Lines 6-15, 71-73).
**Description**: `GetInputState(int blockId)` / `GetOutputState(int blockId)` are the public read contract of the analysis result and index `ImmutableArray<T>` directly (`InputStates[blockId]`, `OutputStates[blockId]`) with no range validation. Every other public member of this project taking a block ID routes through `ArgumentNullGuard.RequireIndex` with `nameof(blockId)`. `result.GetInputState(-1)` or `GetInputState(graph.Blocks.Length)` therefore throws whatever the BCL indexer throws - on current System.Collections.Immutable an `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` parameterized as the indexer-internal `"index"` (on older package revisions a raw `IndexOutOfRangeException`) - never an exception naming `blockId`. The project treats this error taxonomy as load-bearing enough to pin it with a dedicated boundary test for its other guard sites. Fail-closed (no wrong results), developer-facing only. Distinct from #166 (semantically wrong Bottom states for in-range unreachable blocks): different type, site, and mechanism.
1. Build any minimal graph and analyze it: `var graph = new DataflowGraph<NullnessValue>([new(0, v => v)], []); var result = ForwardDataflowAnalysis.Analyze(graph, NullnessDomain.Instance, NullnessValue.MaybeNull);`
2. Call `result.GetInputState(5)` (or `-1`): the thrown exception's `ParamName` is `"index"` (or `IndexOutOfRangeException`, depending on the resolved System.Collections.Immutable version), with no mention of `blockId`.
3. Contrast `graph.GetBlock(5)`: throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` with `ParamName == "blockId"` via `RequireIndex` (`DataflowGraph.cs:134-140`) - proving the project's own convention for the identical input class is violated by the result accessors.
**Confidence**: High (behavior certain from code reading); severity explicitly Low (diagnostic-quality only).

### 290. [RESOLVED c1a8adb30] AtomicFileTests.TemporaryFiles Globs `<destination>.*.tmp` While Production Stages `.sharpproof-<guid>.tmp`, Making Every "No Leftover Staging File" Assertion Vacuous Since the Renaming

**Location**: `SharpProof.Ir.Test\AtomicFileTests.cs` (Helper Lines 170-172: `Directory.GetFiles(Path.GetDirectoryName(path)!, Path.GetFileName(path) + ".*.tmp")`; vacuous assertion call sites Lines 38, 51, 64, 75); mismatched production naming `SharpProof.Ir\AtomicFile.cs` (Lines 16-18 and 150-152: `".sharpproof-" + Guid.NewGuid().ToString("N") + ".tmp"` placed directly in the destination directory).
**Description**: The helper builds its search pattern from the DESTINATION file name (`"<dest>.*.tmp"`, e.g. `"result.txt.*.tmp"`), but ever since commit `81e82fd8e` moved staging names off the old `<destination>.<guid>.tmp` scheme (visible in `git show b47fbd0e1:SharpProof.Ir/AtomicFile.cs`: `return (destination, destination + "." + Guid.NewGuid().ToString("N") + ".tmp");`) to directory-relative `.sharpproof-<guid>.tmp`, no staging file can ever match that pattern under any glob semantics. Consequently the four assertions `Assert.That(TemporaryFiles(path), Is.Empty)` in `WriteUtf8CreatesParents…ReplacesDestination` (Line 38), `WriteUtf8AsyncCreates…` (Line 51), `CanceledWritePreservesDestinationAndCleansTemporaryFile` (Line 64), and `FailedPublicationCleansTemporaryFile` (Line 75) are tautologically true: they would pass even if `TryDeleteStaged` were deleted outright, if the `finally` blocks at AtomicFile.cs Lines 109-112 / 138-141 were removed, or if any future change reintroduced orphaned `.sharpproof-*.tmp` files - precisely the leftover-temp defect class BUGS.md #219/#220 documented in production. Remediation commit `2a58bc0dc` even added a correct explicit-path sweep test (`SweepStagedRemovesOnlyExpiredSharpProofFiles`, Lines 146-159) while leaving the broken helper untouched, proving cleanup verification was believed covered. Blast radius: SharpProof.Ir.Test loses its only in-process regression net for staging hygiene; worker result.json writes, cache writes, and launcher publication staging all rely on the finally-cleanup these tests claim to pin. Distinct from #219/#220 (production leftover-staging defects, since remediated): test-side detection failure at a different site with a different mechanism (stale glob after a renaming migration). Same tautological-test family as #194/#253/#254 but different project, corruption vector, and guarded property.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Temporarily comment out the `finally { TryDeleteStaged(temporary); }` block in `AtomicFile.WriteUtf8` (AtomicFile.cs Lines 109-112).
2. Run `dotnet test --filter FailedPublicationCleansTemporaryFile` (and the other three affected tests): all pass, despite a `.sharpproof-<guid>.tmp` file demonstrably left behind in `_root`.
3. Confirm directly: `Directory.GetFiles(dir, ".sharpproof-*.tmp").Length > 0` after any forced failure, while `TemporaryFiles(path)` returns empty for the same directory.
4. Control: revert the helper to `Directory.GetFiles(dir, ".sharpproof-*.tmp")` and step 2 fails immediately, isolating the stale pattern as the defect.
**Confidence**: High (helper pattern, production naming scheme, git history of the renaming commit, and the tautology under a simulated cleanup regression all mechanically verified).

### 291. [RESOLVED 42436119b] Post-#214 Unchecked-Divide Whitelist Arm Re-Admits `long.MinValue / -1L` as an Exact/Total Term Whose Own Evaluator Faults on Its Only Input and Which Compiled C# Constant-Folds Differently

**Location**: `SharpProof.Frontend\RoslynOperationLowerer.cs` (Whitelist `IsKnownSafeUncheckedArithmetic` Lines 783-796, consumed by the gate at Lines 751-762; literal lowering Lines 384-422 and negative-literal path Lines 616-619); interpreter `SharpProof.Ir\IrInterpreter.cs` (Line 36: `IrBinaryOperator.Divide => Integer(checked(left / right))`, Overflow catch Lines 44-47); non-fold behavior `SharpProof.Ir\IrTermServices.cs` (Lines 151-159, 206-218: Overflow folds to `null`).
**Description**: Commit `5053cd443` compensated the #214 Divide row fix with a whitelist: safe iff `left is IrIntegerTerm { Value: not long.MinValue } || right is IrIntegerTerm`. Arm B (`right is IrIntegerTerm`) is satisfied by ANY exact denominator - including `-1L` - while arm A's MinValue exclusion only inspects the numerator. For `public static long Target() => long.MinValue / -1L;` both literals lower to exact integer terms, the unchecked-arithmetic gate is fully bypassed, and the lowering returns `Exact(Binary(Divide, Integer(long.MinValue), Integer(-1)))`. The IR layer then refuses to fold it: `checked(long.MinValue / -1)` throws, is caught into `Overflow` (IrInterpreter.cs Lines 44-47), and `FoldIntegerBinary` maps Overflow to `null` (IrTermServices.cs) - leaving an Exact-classified term whose evaluation under the product's own oracle is `Exception(Overflow)` for the only input pair it can take. The whitelist comment itself (Lines 788-792) states the intent that "the exceptional minimum-value case [is] represented by the IR interpreter", i.e., this shape was supposed to keep abstaining; arm B defeats arm A whenever the divisor is exact. Compiled reference behavior diverges too: Roslyn constant-folds unchecked constant division `MinValue / -1` to `long.MinValue` per the C# unchecked constant-expression rule, so the emitted method returns normally while replay through IrInterpreter reports a faulting clause - flipping provability/refutation for any contract clause containing the expression. Symbolic-numerator divides admitted by the same arm (`a / -1L`) additionally trust that runtime and interpreter agree on MinValue/-1 faulting, which is platform/JIT-dependent. Distinct from #214 (the missing `requiresCheckedArithmetic` catalog row, fixed by `a7a1dd123`): this is the compensating whitelist added afterwards whose second arm re-admits #214's exact scenario when the denominator is exact - post-fix residual at a different site. #222 occupies the interpreter's unchecked remainder arm; #216/#156 are opaque-term identity collapses.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Lower `static long Target() => long.MinValue / -1L;` with `RoslynOperationLowerer` (default or `CreateForConcreteReplay`): observe `FrontendSubsetDecision.Exact`, term `Binary(Divide, Integer(-9223372036854775808), Integer(-1))`.
2. Evaluate the term with `new IrInterpreter(factory).Evaluate`: status `Exception` (Overflow) - an "Exact" classification that never yields a value.
3. Compile-and-run the same source (Release, `checkOverflow: false`, as the test harness does): Roslyn folds the constant division to `long.MinValue`; the compiled result disagrees with the interpreted exception.
4. Contrast `unchecked(a + b)` which still abstains (pinned at FrontendLoweringTests.cs Lines ~246-251), proving only the Divide whitelist opens this hole.
**Confidence**: High (admission path, interpreter arm, and non-folding verified line-by-line; compiled-side fold follows specified unchecked constant semantics).

### 292. [RESOLVED 21afc56d1] Expression-Position Mutations (`x++`, Compound Assignment) Lower Without Assigning or Havocing the Mutated Local, So the Emitted IR Program Models Stale Pre-Statement Values

**Location**: `SharpProof.Frontend\RoslynProgramLowerer.cs` (Direct assignment path Lines 206-222 `LowerAssignment`; declarator-initializer path Lines 184-195 `LowerDeclarator` -> `LowerValue` Line 193); contrast statement-form handling Lines 158-167 routing to `LowerUnsupportedMutation` (Lines 446-463) ending in `HavocKnownState` (Line 462); root contributor `SharpProof.Frontend\RoslynOperationLowerer.cs` DefaultVisit `arguments: []` at Line 469.
**Description**: Statement-position `x++;` / `x += 2;` hit `LowerUnsupportedMutation`, which evaluates the location/value and then havocs all known state - soundly invalidating prior facts about `x` (pinned by `UnsupportedMutationAbstainsAndHavocsWithoutThrowing`, ProgramLoweringTests.cs Lines 173-205). The semantically identical mutations in expression position take a different route with no havoc at all: `var updated = value++;` lowers the declarator initializer via `LowerValue` -> `_expressions.Lower` -> `DefaultVisit`, producing an opaque term built with `arguments: []` (RoslynOperationLowerer.cs Line 469) that does not even reference `value`, followed by `AssignOrHavoc(updated := opaque)` - `value` is never assigned or havoc'd. Likewise `y = x += 2;` takes `LowerAssignment`'s direct-variable path (Lines 209-222) and assigns only `y`. The emitted program therefore models `value` as keeping its pre-statement value although real code increments it; any consumer interpreting block state despite the program-level abstention flag (the consumption pattern #158 documented as real for this same pipeline shape) reads stale values and can prove equalities the runtime refutes. The abstention (`UnsupportedOperationKind`) is recorded, so gated consumers fail closed - but the program artifact itself is unsound, and the asymmetry with the statement form shows the havoc was intended to cover mutation semantics generally. Distinct from #158 (expression-level flow-capture bindings dropped on references) and from remediated #262 (analyzer-side effect summaries for string compound assignments, which explicitly notes the statement-form program-lowerer abstention as the fail-closed contrast): this is the expression-form program-lowerer path omitting the mutation havoc entirely - a site neither entry describes.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Build the CFG for `public static long Target(long value) { var updated = value++; return value + updated; }` and run `RoslynProgramLowerer.Lower`.
2. Inspect instructions: one `IrAssignInstruction` binding `updated` to a pure-opaque term with zero arguments; NO `IrHavocInstruction` naming `value` anywhere.
3. Interpret the blocks: `value` retains its entry value after the declaration, whereas compiled C# increments it - divergence visible for every `k`.
4. Contrast the statement form `value++;` (ProgramLoweringTests.cs Lines 173-205) which does havoc `value`, proving the omission is path-specific.
**Confidence**: Medium (mechanism fully verified statically; severity tempered because the global abstention accompanies the lowering and strongest consumers gate on `IsExact`).

### 293. [RESOLVED b4aedb2c7] OperationSupport Catalog Omits `FlowCaptureReference` From `contractExpression` While the Shipped Pipeline Lowers It Exactly, Cementing a Wrong Public Classifier Answer Behind a SHA-256 Snapshot

**Location**: `SharpProof.Frontend\OperationSupport.catalog.json` (Lines 3-16: contractExpression list ends at `ArrayElementReference`; no FlowCapture/FlowCaptureReference rows); actual capability `SharpProof.Frontend\RoslynOperationLowerer.cs` (VisitFlowCaptureReference Lines 523-535 returning `Exact` when `IsCaptureBound`); binding producer `SharpProof.Frontend\RoslynProgramLowerer.cs` (LowerCapture Lines 197-204 calls `_expressions.BindCapture`); public surface `SharpProof.Frontend\OperationSubsetClassifier.cs` (public `Classify(kind)` Lines 5-10 defaulting to `ContractExpressionLowering`); snapshot pinned `SharpProof.Frontend.Test\FrontendLoweringTests.cs` (SHA-256 pin Lines 919-925).
**Description**: The catalog's `contractExpression` stage enumerates twelve kinds and excludes flow-capture kinds, so `OperationSubsetClassifier.Classify(OperationKind.FlowCaptureReference)` permanently reports `ClosedAbstention/UnsupportedOperationKind`. But the shipped program lowering demonstrably produces Exact terms for that kind: `LowerCapture` binds the capture id on the shared expression lowerer (RoslynProgramLowerer.cs Lines 200-201), after which `VisitFlowCaptureReference` returns `LoweredExpression.Exact(GetCapture(...))` - exercised by `FlowCapturesBecomeStableVariablesAndAssignments` (ProgramLoweringTests.cs Lines 148-170, `??` lowering). The classifier snapshot test cements the inaccurate answer with a hardcoded SHA-256, so correcting the catalog forces a deliberate snapshot update - nothing drifts silently, but the public closed-world claim ("these are the kinds the contract-expression stage supports") is factually wrong for one kind the pipeline lowers Exactly. External consumers of the public `Classify` API gating on it reject programs containing `??`/ternary joins that the frontend actually handles. Internal stages are unaffected (they use the EffectDiscovery list, LanguageSubsetGate.cs Lines 40-41; OperationEffectScanner.Expressions.cs Lines 485-486). Distinct from #159 (an over-claim admitting multi-rank `.Length` intrinsics): this is an under-claim in the static support catalog for a kind that lowers Exactly - different file and decision axis.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Call `OperationSubsetClassifier.Classify(OperationKind.FlowCaptureReference)` - observe `ClosedAbstention`/`UnsupportedOperationKind`.
2. Lower `static string Target(string? value) => value ?? "fallback";` through `RoslynProgramLowerer.Lower`: the flow-capture reference inside the join lowers to an Exact variable term (capture bound at RoslynProgramLowerer.cs Line 201).
3. Compare with the catalog JSON: no `FlowCaptureReference` row exists in `contractExpression` (Lines 3-16), and the pinned snapshot (FrontendLoweringTests.cs Lines 919-925) locks the mismatch in.
**Confidence**: High on the mismatch (all four artifacts read directly); Low on impact (only public-API consumers misled today).

### 294. [RESOLVED 1d85b8451] MayCompleteNormally Switch-Expression Arm Converts a May-Throw-on-Some-Inputs Switch Into a Definite "Never Completes", Dropping Every Downstream Effect Through the #211/#261 Consumer Chains (Third Arm of That Family)

**Location**: `SharpProof.Effects\ManagedAbstractFlow.cs` (Lines ~2084-2105 `MayCompleteSwitchExpression`; definite `return false` at ~2095-2097 when `SwitchExpressionFacts.HasReachableUnmatchedPath` holds; documented permissive contract at Lines 1973-1979). Contrast: `SharpProof.Effects\OperationCompletionEvaluator.cs` (Lines 140-155 `CanCompleteSwitchExpression` - same operation, without the unmatched-path conjunct). Consumers: `SharpProof.Effects\EffectAnalysisSession.cs` (Lines 301-339 feeding `ResolveStaticFieldTypeInitialization`), `SharpProof.Effects\OperationCompletionEvaluator.cs` (Lines 576-605 via `_completionFacts.MethodCanCompleteNormally`), `SharpProof.Effects\OperationEffectScanner.cs` (Lines 982-994 sequence break), `SharpProof.Effects\EffectMethodNodeBuilder.cs` (Lines 345-348 CFG successor skip), inherited by `SharpProof.Effects\ExceptionHandlerReachability.cs`.
**Description**: `MayCompleteSwitchExpression` returns a DEFINITE `false` whenever `HasReachableUnmatchedPath` holds. But "an unmatched path exists" means only that SOME input values fall past all arms and throw `SwitchExpressionException`; matched executions complete normally. The method's own class contract states the fact is deliberately permissive so that "uncertainty must retain the later effects" (Lines 1973-1979), and the engine's other completion evaluator treats the identical operation correctly (`CanCompleteSwitchExpression` asks only whether some reachable arm completes). Trigger needs no exotic shape: `v switch { var t when Gate() => ... }` - a total `var` pattern under a guard - compiles without CS8509 yet throws whenever `Gate()` returns false. For such a callee, `MethodCanCompleteNormally(callee)` evaluates false, so every caller's `ScanCallStep` marks the call non-completing and `ScanSequence` breaks: static writes, contract-relevant call sites, and capabilities placed after the call are certified away although they execute on every normal run. Variant B: the same definite-false flows through `StaticInitializationCannotComplete` so `ResolveStaticFieldTypeInitialization` returns Empty for fields whose initializers contain such switches, erasing type-initializer boundaries. Under-approximation (silent false-pure), not conservatism. Distinctness: pinned #211 was the ITryOperation arm (remediated in-tree - current arm correctly disjoins `MayCompleteNormally(@try.Body)`); #261 was the ILoopOperation arm (now `LoopHasReachableExit` after remediation); this is the switch-expression arm: different operation kind, different helper (`HasReachableUnmatchedPath` vs break/goto exit detection), same consumer chains - coexistence justified exactly per #261's own precedent. Also distinct from #152-#155/#262 scanner families.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Define `static bool Gate() => Config.Enabled;` and callee `static int Pick(int v) { var r = v switch { var t when Gate() => Load(t) }; return r; }` (compiles cleanly; throws iff `Gate()` is false).
2. Analyze `[EnforcePure] static int Use(int v) { Pick(v); Ambient.Write(); return 1; }` via `EffectAnalysisSession.Analyze`.
3. Expected per the Lines 1973-1979 contract: `Ambient.Write()` retained as a may-effect. Actual: `MethodCanCompleteNormally(Pick)` is false, so `Use`'s sequence breaks after `Pick(v)` and the static write disappears - certified pure although normal executions perform it.
4. Variant B: place the same switch in a static-field initializer (`static readonly int S = Pick(Config.Seed);`) and analyze a reader of `S`: `ResolveStaticFieldTypeInitialization` returns Empty, dropping the initializer boundary.
5. Test gap: switch-expression fixtures (EffectAnalysisTests.cs Lines ~5319-5360) exercise the permissive OperationCompletionEvaluator path; none combines a guarded-total switch callee with post-call state writes through `MethodCanCompleteNormally`.
**Confidence**: High (arm logic, contract contradiction, evaluator asymmetry, compilable trigger syntax, and consumer chains verified statically; verified live at HEAD `8b9ca154a`).

### 295. Captured-Variable (Closure/Display-Class) State Is Invisible to Effect Summaries - Captured Reads/Writes and the Closure Allocation Are Never Emitted, So Shared-Cell Mutation Certifies as Pure and Allocation-Free

**Location**: Write path `SharpProof.Effects\OperationEffectScanner.Assignments.cs` (Lines ~24-35: plain locals/parameters -> `EffectSummary.Empty`); read path `SharpProof.Effects\OperationEffectScanner.cs` (Lines ~258-270: `ILocalReferenceOperation`/plain `IParameterReferenceOperation` -> Empty); dead classification `SharpProof.Effects\ConversionOwnershipClassifier.cs` (Lines 576-589: `ClassifyLocal` maps foreign-method locals to `EffectRegionId.Captured(...)` but the result is never consulted for the local's own read/write effects). Intended vocabulary proving the gap unintended: `SharpProof.Effects\EffectContractMappings.generated.cs` (Line 216 mapping Captured <-> Reads/WritesCapturedState) and `SharpProof.Effects\EffectContractMappings.cs` (Lines 174-178 `ImpureState` includes both Captured kinds; Lines 132-138 `IsObservablePure` excludes Captured reads); repo-wide grep finds zero producers of either contract kind outside attribute definitions, wire tables, and parity tests.
**Description**: Locals captured by lambdas/local functions are heap-resident display-class cells shared between the enclosing method and nested callables; runtime allocates the closure object and mutations persist. The scanner models neither: (a) inside a nested callable's own analysis, an assignment to a captured local hits the local arm of `ScanWriteTarget` and returns `Empty` - even though `ConversionOwnershipClassifier.ClassifyLocal` explicitly recognizes the capture (ContainingSymbol != current method) and has a Captured region waiting; (b) the enclosing method's own writes take the same Empty arm; (c) the display-class allocation itself is modeled nowhere (`ScanCoreOperationTail`'s allocation arms cover only delegate/anonymous-object/array/object creation). Interprocedurally the omission compounds: the nested callable is analyzed as its own node and its LocalSummary - devoid of any write - is remapped into the caller, so a caller invoking a capturing local function certifies write-free and allocation-free although a shared heap cell was allocated and mutated. Consequences: false-pure certification for the public `EffectAnalysisSession.Analyze` surface including the escaped-closure form (`MakeCounter()` returning a mutating delegate); the wire vocabulary ships but can never be produced. Scope note, stated plainly: the shipped language-subset pipeline abstains on the triggering shapes (`SupportsCall` rejects `MethodKind.LocalFunction`; delegate-typed storage is an unsupported type), so - as with accepted #155 - the damage lands on the public API/engine-oracle blast radius rather than shipped [EnforcePure] selection. Distinct from #154 (primary-ctor receiver-backed parameters), #155 (ref-local aliases to fields), #132 (generic static fields): none involves closures; the omitted effects include a distinct contract pair plus a managed allocation, and the mechanism is the local/parameter arms bypassing `ClassifyRegion`/`ClassifyLocal` entirely.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Analyze via `new EffectAnalysisSession(compilation).Analyze(...)`: `public static int Step() { var shared = 0; void Bump() => shared++; Bump(); return shared; }`
2. Inspect Step's summary: expected at minimum `Allocates(Managed)` for the display class and a WritesCapturedState effect. Actual: only `DirectCall` uncertainty - no allocation kind, no Captured regions.
3. Confirm the callee half: analyze the Bump symbol - `shared++` yields an empty effect set (read Empty, operator Empty, write-target local -> Empty) despite `shared` being a heap cell.
4. Observability proof: escaped form `Func<int> MakeCounter() { var n = 0; return () => n++; }` returns 0, 1, 2 across invocations - persistent heap mutation the summary denies.
5. Test gap: `CallsThatMutateLocalsInvalidateFlowFacts` (EffectAnalysisTests.cs Lines ~6754-6810) pins only the divide-by-zero throw fact surviving; it never asserts write/allocation dimensions, so the empty-effect behavior passes CI today.
**Confidence**: Medium-High (every cited site read directly, zero-emission claim established by exhaustive grep, capture semantics language-defined; severity tempered solely by the gate abstaining on the shipped pipeline path).

### 296. [NOT REPRODUCED] Binding an Unconstructed Generic Method Definition Crashes ContractCanonicalization.CreateTypeSpecializer With a Raw IndexOutOfRangeException Instead of Any ContractBindingFailure

**Status**: The reported raw `IndexOutOfRangeException` was not reproducible with the current Roslyn symbols: generic definitions expose matching type-parameter/type-argument arity, and binding returns the typed `UnsupportedExpression` result. `UnconstructedGenericMethodDefinitionReturnsTypedFailure` records this guard. No production change is claimed for this report; retain it as a rejected lead unless a custom symbol provider reproduces the unchecked-arity shape.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Contracts\ContractCanonicalization.cs` (Lines 18-20 first `AddParameters` call; Lines 192-200 `AddParameters` indexing `arguments[index]`; same hazard repeated for the partial counterpart at Lines 43-45); reached eagerly from `SharpProof.Contracts\ContractBinder.cs` Line 112 (`_canonicalization.CreateTypeSpecializer(source)` evaluated before the `ContractExpressionBinder` constructor, with no catch on the path).
**Description**: `CreateTypeSpecializer` builds its substitution map with `AddParameters(source.OriginalDefinition.TypeParameters, source.TypeArguments)`. For an unconstructed generic method definition, `IMethodSymbol.TypeArguments` is EMPTY while `TypeParameters.Length >= 1`, so the loop executes `arguments[0]` on a zero-length ImmutableArray and throws raw `IndexOutOfRangeException`. Nothing in `ContractBinder.BindCore` catches it: the typed failure taxonomy (`UnsupportedExpression`, `CompanionSignatureMismatch`, ...) is bypassed entirely. Contrast the companion path, which carefully converts the analogous arity problem into a typed result (`ContractForSymbolMatcher.SpecializeCompanion`, Lines 269-284: `method.Construct(...)` wrapped in `catch (ArgumentException)` -> `CompanionSignatureMismatch`) - proving untyped crashes here are unintended. Reachability: (1) public API, certain - `ContractBinder.Bind/BindRequires/GetClauseInventory` accept arbitrary IMethodSymbols; passing the declared generic definition deterministically throws; no test pins definitional binding (every fixture resolves targets through invocation syntax, i.e., constructed symbols). (2) Shipped analyzer pipeline, conditional - `RequiresCallSiteAnalyzer.AnalyzeCallSite` binds `candidate.TargetMethod.ReducedFrom ?? candidate.TargetMethod` unconditionally (RequiresCallSiteAnalyzer.cs Lines 244-246, 265), and if Roslyn's ReducedFrom yields the unsubstituted declaration for constructed generic extension invocations every such callee hits the crash; no fixture defines a generic extension method, so the vector is unpinned. Distinct from #106 (NRE formatting a null extension-receiver type), #169 (SingleOrDefault crash for duplicate intrinsics), #275 (nullability-spelling rejection), #167/#274 (companion discovery/resolution): this is an eager arity-mismatch index fault in type-parameter specialization escaping the closed ContractBindingFailure contract.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Compile `public static class Target { public static T Read<T>(T value) { Contract.Requires(value != null); return value; } }` with `SHARPPROOF_CONTRACTS` defined.
2. Resolve `Read` as its definition symbol (e.g., `GetTypeByMetadataName("Target").GetMembers("Read")`) and call `new ContractBinder(compilation, new IrFactory()).Bind(definition)`.

**Confidence**: High (the indexing fault and its eager execution before any catch site are code-certain; only the second pipeline vector is conditional on Roslyn ReducedFrom semantics).

### 297. [RESOLVED d17b9579a] Non-Executable Prologue-Prefix Statements (Local-Function Declarations, Empty Statements) Force Subsequent Contract.Requires Clauses to Late/Hard Binding Failure, Contradicting the Discovery Layer That Explicitly Tolerates Exactly These Prefixes

**Location**: `SharpProof.Contracts\ContractClauseInventoryBuilder.cs` (Prior-statement scan Lines ~166-178 with the `Late` verdict at 173-176; predicate `IsDirectClause` Lines 207-212 accepting only ExpressionStatementSyntax whose expression is a clause invocation). Contradicting layer: `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\RequiresCallSiteDiscovery.cs` Lines 458-468, whose prologue-prefix test explicitly whitelists `prior is EmptyStatementSyntax or LocalFunctionStatementSyntax ||` before requiring normal completion.
**Description**: `TryGetDirectPlacement` declares a clause `Late` (hard `InvalidClausePlacement` on binding plus an "expected a direct prologue statement" diagnostic) whenever ANY preceding statement in the block fails `IsDirectClause`. `IsDirectClause` returns false for every non-expression statement - including local-function declarations and empty statements, neither of which executes at the point of the clause. So both of these legal, natural methods fail hard: `static void WithHelper(int x) { static bool Positive(int v) => v > 0; Contract.Requires(x != 0); }` and `static void WithEmpty(int x) { ; Contract.Requires(x != 0); }`. The repo's own replayability logic proves these prefixes are meant to be harmless: `HasReplayablePrefix` skips EmptyStatementSyntax and LocalFunctionStatementSyntax priors before checking that executable priors complete normally. The two layers therefore disagree on the definition of "prologue prefix": discovery treats a local function as transparent, while the inventory/binder treats its mere presence as disqualifying - the very same clause can be replayable per discovery yet rejected as misplaced by binding. Note also the asymmetry inside the inventory itself: the local function's own `Contract.Requires(v > 0)` is correctly classified NestedCallable (no diagnostic), but its declaration position still poisons the following outer clause. Distinct from #168 (parenthesized-prologue branch), #233 (conditional-whitelist arm of Classify's fallback walk), #170 (remediated IsReachable default): this is the third arm - the prior-statement scan inside TryGetDirectPlacement - with a different trigger and a different proof of defect (in-repo contradiction with HasReplayablePrefix).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Analyze/bind `WithHelper` above (helper declared before its caller's precondition; `SHARPPROOF_CONTRACTS` defined).
2. Expected per discovery-layer semantics: clause is ValidPrologue (local-function declarations execute nothing); actual: placement Late, `ContractBinder.Bind` fails with InvalidClausePlacement, and the clause diagnostic fires at the Requires.
3. Move the local function after the Requires: binding succeeds - proving the declaration's position, not its content, is the discriminator.
**Confidence**: High (behavior verified line-by-line in both layers; classification rests on the repo's own inconsistent treatment of identical prefixes).

### 298. [RESOLVED a5e879fde] A Valid Companion Silently Supersedes Invalid Direct Clauses: The Binder Flips InvalidClausePlacement Into Success While HasSelectedContractIntent Ignores DirectInventory Placements, Hiding the Breakage From Selection and Manifest Surfaces

**Location**: `SharpProof.Contracts\EffectiveContractSourceResolver.cs` (Companion-precedence branch Lines ~99-113 vs direct branch Lines ~72-82/116-123; `EffectiveContractSourceResolution.HasSelectedContractIntent` Lines 7-14 checks only `*Inventory.HasRejectedContractApiUsage`, never DirectInventory placements); consumed by `SharpProof.Contracts\ContractBinder.cs` Lines 87-117 and `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\ClaimManifestBuilder.cs` Lines 52-68.
**Description**: When a target's direct clauses exist but none is ValidPrologue (e.g., one Conditional-guarded clause), resolution takes two irreconcilable paths depending solely on whether the containing type has a well-formed companion: without companion, ResolveCore returns failure `InvalidClausePlacement` (via `direct.HasPlacementErrors`), HasSelectedContractIntent is true (failure not None/Missing), the method selects, and binding fails closed. WITH companion: Lines 99-113 swap the effective inventory to the companion's, discard the direct inventory's placement errors entirely (the failure value is computed only from the companion inventory at Lines 102-106), and binding SUCCEEDS with the companion's contract. Three consumers consequently disagree about the identical authoring error: (1) the binder certifies companion clauses while the user-written clause intent vanishes from BoundMethodContracts; (2) HasSelectedContractIntent evaluates false (its probe reads only rejected-API usage flags, and the companion inventory has none), so `AnalyzerFeaturePipeline.GetSelection`/`GetFeatureSelection` and `ClaimManifestBuilder.BuildTarget` compute zero Contracts features - the callable is unselected and absent from worker-claim selection even though its body contains broken contract usage; (3) only the operation-block clause reporter still flags the misplaced clause, and only when that action runs. A companion's presence should not downgrade a hard binding failure into success-with-substituted-semantics while hiding the breakage from selection and manifest surfaces. Distinct from #274 (one malformed companion poisons unrelated methods - the inverse direction), #167 (vanish caused by a malformed sole attribute payload), #234 (intrinsic misuse inside companion bodies), #276 (trusted-flag divergence between Select callers): this is the precedence interaction between valid companions and invalid direct clauses plus the DirectInventory placement blind spot - no existing entry covers it.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Define `public class Gate { public int Read(int x) { if (x > 0) { Contract.Requires(x < 100); } return x; } }` and a valid companion `[ContractFor(typeof(Gate))] static class GateContracts { public static void Read(Gate gate, int x) => Contract.Requires(x != 0); }`.
2. `BindRequires(Gate.Read)`: succeeds with the companion clause `x != 0`; the author's `x < 100` obligation appears nowhere in the bound contracts.
3. Delete the companion class: the identical method now fails with InvalidClausePlacement and reports selected-contract intent - proving the companion's existence alone converts a hard failure into silent substitution and drops the method from feature selection/manifest intent.
**Confidence**: Medium (all sites verified mechanically; defect-vs-deliberate rests on the cross-consumer inconsistency, which nothing documents).

### 299. [RESOLVED 42436119b] AccountResources Mixes Cumulative-Delta Semantics With Per-Solver Fresh Counters - ConsumedResourceCount Telescopes to the Largest Single Query, Structurally Defeating the Method-Level Resource Budget

**Location**: `SharpProof.Smt\IrSmtBackend.cs` (Fresh-solver construction at Lines 111-116 with per-query `"rlimit"` parameter; defective arithmetic at Lines 194-198 in `AccountResources`; absent-statistic zero-charge early-return at Lines 183-192); consumers `SharpProof.Worker\MethodResourceBudget.cs` (Lines 12, 15-31, 40-46) and `SharpProof.Worker\SharpProofWorker.cs` (Lines 12-13, 296-298, 558-560); enforcement sites `SharpProof.Worker\CallableVerifier.cs` (Lines 220-224, 229-235, 309-327).
**Description**: Every query builds a NEW Z3 solver with its own `"rlimit"` parameter (verified: `using var solver = _context.MkSolver(); parameters.Add("rlimit", _options.QueryRlimit);` per CheckCore call), so the `"rlimit count"` statistic read by AccountResources restarts near 0 each query. The accumulation logic is `_consumedResourceCount += observed >= _lastObservedResourceCount ? observed - _lastObservedResourceCount : observed; _lastObservedResourceCount = observed;`. The decrease arm was changed by commit `f921d99d8` to adopt fresh-counter semantics (adding that commit's own pinning test), but the increase arm still subtracts `_lastObservedResourceCount` - the final counter value of a previous, independent solver. Under fresh-counter reality the correct total is the SUM of observations; the implemented arithmetic instead telescopes: for monotone observations o1 <= o2 <= ... <= on the contributions are o1, o2-o1, ..., summing to exactly on. Since each per-solver counter is capped at QueryRlimit (default 3,000,000), ConsumedResourceCount never exceeds ~the largest single-query observation whenever usage grows query-over-query - the common case. Consequences: `MethodResourceBudget.GetConsumedResourceCount` subtracts its starting snapshot and compares against `_methodRlimit` (default 20M); `IsExceeded` can never fire and `TryStartQuery` never refuses because consumed stays below ~one query's rlimit forever; CallableVerifier gates every postcondition query and the normal-completion probe on TryStartQuery and aborts remaining ensures on IsExceeded - all dead in practice. A callable may issue an unbounded number of 3M-rlimit queries (bounded only by wall-clock timers), defeating the method-level resource guarantee. Secondary defect in the same block: when the statistic is absent or not IsUInt, the loop ends and the query is charged zero, silently. DISTINCTNESS vs #150/#223: both describe the pre-`f921d99d8` formula `(1L << 32) - last + observed` (overcharge -> permanent ResourceLimit), which no longer exists in-tree; the current defect is the mirror image - systematic UNDER-charge from stale-baseline subtraction across independent counters - with distinct mechanism (telescoping deltas), trigger (monotone-increasing counters), and symptom (budgets never fire). #178/#224 are unrelated axes.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Run three successive CheckAsync queries whose per-solver rlimit counts increase (e.g., increasingly constrained postcondition goals: observed ~10k, ~500k, ~2.9M).
2. Read ConsumedResourceCount after each: results are ~10k, ~500k, ~2.9M - the running maximum, not the cumulative ~3.41M.
3. Build `MethodResourceBudget(reader, queryRlimit: 3_000_000, methodRlimit: 20_000_000)` (wired automatically by SharpProofWorker): TryStartQuery returns true and IsExceeded stays false indefinitely although true consumption passed 20M after seven queries.
4. Observe the pinning test `ResourceAccountingTreatsEachSolverStatisticsSnapshotAsFresh` passes throughout: it asserts only `second >= first` bounds, which telescoping output satisfies.
**Confidence**: High (arithmetic statically verifiable; fresh-solver-per-query premise verified at current HEAD `8b9ca154a`; consumer comparisons line-verified; shipped test provably cannot detect the divergence).

### 300. [RESOLVED 3d26c22ff] BackendModel's Public Constructor Throws Untyped ArgumentException on Duplicate Assignments - the One Malformed-Model Shape That Escapes ProofKernel's Closed Failure Mapping

**Location**: `SharpProof.Verify\Backend.cs` (Public constructor Lines 21-31, `ToImmutableDictionary` at ~25-27); internal storage ctor `SharpProof.Verify\DeclarativeModels.generated.cs` (Lines 15-21); unguarded ingestion `SharpProof.Verify\ProofKernel.cs` (Lines 8-47: VerifyAsync has no exception boundary; contrast the deliberate ArgumentException catch inside ValidateAssignments at Lines ~105-114).
**Description**: `ProofKernel.VerifyAsync` implements a closed outcome union: every malformed result value it defines maps to a typed abstention - null result -> MalformedBackendResult, wrong status payload -> typed Unknown, extra/missing/mistyped assignments -> CounterexampleReplayFailed (pinned by ProofKernelTests.cs "MissingOrMalformedRequestedModelBindingsAbstain"), out-of-range cores -> MalformedBackendResult. ValidateAssignments even wraps GetVariableInfo in `catch (ArgumentException)`, proving intent that hostile payloads fail closed rather than throw. The duplicate-key shape slips through every net: BackendModel's public constructor feeds the raw pair sequence into `ImmutableDictionary.ToImmutableDictionary(keySelector, elementSelector)`, which throws ArgumentException ("An item with the same key has already been added") for repeated IrVarId keys - thrown inside the producing backend's CheckAsync before any kernel validation runs, faulting the awaited task instead of surfacing as MalformedBackendResult. The real IrSmtBackend.CreateSatisfiable cannot produce duplicates (its builder iterates deduplicated ModelVariables), but ISmtBackend is a public embedding surface (`SharpProofWorker(ISmtBackend)` constructor) and SharpProof.Verify grants InternalsVisibleTo("SharpProof.Fuzz"), so third-party and fuzz backends construct BackendModel directly; inside the worker pipeline the fault escalates through the blanket policy catch into whole-callable InfrastructureFailure degradation (the amplification mechanic documented in #225) instead of a single degraded claim. Same pattern family as accepted #185/#229/#230 (unguarded ToDictionary on potentially-duplicate IDs in other components) but a different site, assembly, and consumer chain; related to but distinct from #149 (the SMT-layer catch-filter gap in IrSmtBackend.CheckAsync itself).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Reference SharpProof.Verify and implement ISmtBackend returning `BackendCheckResult.Satisfiable(new BackendModel(new[] { KeyValuePair.Create(v, factory.CreateIntegerValue(0)), KeyValuePair.Create(v, factory.CreateIntegerValue(1)) }))` for the same variable `v`.
2. Call `new ProofKernel(stub).VerifyAsync(query)` where v is in query.ModelVariables: instead of UnknownOutcome(MalformedBackendResult), the task faults with ArgumentException from ToImmutableDictionary.
3. Contrast: stubs returning an extra unknown variable, wrong-typed values, incomplete sets, or null model rows are all absorbed into typed UnknownOutcomes (pinned tests) - duplicates are the sole escaped class.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (throw site, absent kernel boundary, contrasting typed-absorption tests all verified); severity Low-Medium (shipped backend cannot currently emit duplicates; impact concentrated on embedding/fuzz surfaces).

### 301. [RESOLVED 872d471d4] Premature `interruptionCacheStatus = Hit` Before the Cached Response Is Served Makes Canceled/TimedOut Artifacts Protocol-Invalid - the Launcher Rejects Them as `response.cache_request_mismatch` and Rewrites True Cancel/Timeout Outcomes Into MalformedResult

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker\SharpProofWorker.cs` (Upgrade at Line 231, before authority validation/serving; terminal consumers `Interrupted()` Lines 95-113 and `Canceled()` returning `interruptionCacheStatus` at Line 329); rule enforced at `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol\ProtocolJson.cs` (ValidateCacheForRequest Lines 400-448: `Hit or Written => storableShape` at Line 438; gated onto ValidateForRequest at Line 374); CacheHit projection `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol\WorkerResultAssembler.cs` (Line 32).
**Description**: When `cache.TryReadAsync` returns a cached response, Line 231 sets `interruptionCacheStatus = WorkerCacheStatus.Hit` BEFORE the cached payload is re-validated (`WorkerProtocolJson.Validate(...)`) and before it is served. Two windows then leave `Hit` stranded with nothing served from cache: (a) the cached response fails authority validation and control falls through to fresh solving, later interrupted; or (b) `projectBoundary` fires inside Assemble while validating a large cached response, jumping to the OperationCanceledException handler -> Interrupted(snapshot). In both, the terminal Canceled/TimedOut response is stamped CacheStatus=Hit (and Summary.CacheHit=true) although zero results were served from cache. The worker never self-validates interrupted responses (Interrupted returns directly), so the artifact reaches the launcher, whose ValidateForRequest demands the storable shape for Hit (RunStatus Complete, all callables Complete, all claims Refuted - ProtocolJson.cs Line 438, verified) which a Canceled/TimedOut response can never satisfy -> error `response.cache_request_mismatch` -> the launcher rewrites the run as Failed/MalformedResult (exit-3 class), losing the true termination cause - the same user-visible damage class #179 documents for crash exits, here produced by live protocol-invalid output. Asymmetry proving unintendedness: every other terminal stamp of this field (Written returning immediately; Unavailable admissible unconditionally at ProtocolJson.cs Line 442; construction-time Miss/Disabled) satisfies the state table; only the premature Hit cannot. Distinct from #226 (opposite direction at a different site: construction-time copy going stale so an observed Unavailable is dropped - diagnostic drift only): this is an active upgrade producing responses that fail the protocol's own cache-state invariant at the trust boundary. #177/#269/#270/#121 are unrelated mechanisms.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Run a verification with cache enabled whose claims are all refuted, so a storable cache entry is written.
2. Re-run with `--method-wall-ms` sized to expire while the worker is inside the cache-hit block: TryReadAsync returns the entry, Line 231 sets Hit, and the deadline fires during authority validation of the large cached response (or use window (a): any post-validation interruption needs no timing).
3. Observe result.json: `runStatus: canceled` (or `timedOut`) with `summary.cacheStatus: hit`, `summary.cacheHit: true`, and zero cache-derived claim rows.
4. Run the launcher's validation (ValidateForRequest) on that artifact: `response.cache_request_mismatch`; the published verdict becomes worker.malformed_result instead of the true cancel/timeout.
**Confidence**: High (upgrade site, both stranding windows, terminal consumers, and rejecting rule line-verified; arm (a) needs no timing).

### 302. [RESOLVED 83ef7b6c5] The Exclusive Cache Lock Is Held Across Full Counterexample-Replay Validation - One Large Cache Hit Starves Every Peer Past the Fixed 1-Second Wait and Systematically Reports Healthy Caches as Unavailable

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker\VerificationCache.cs` (Lock acquired at Lines 29-32 and released only in finally blocks at Lines 129-148; expensive work inside the critical section: IsCacheable -> ReplayCachedClaims Lines 542-589 with TryCreateModel, EntryAssumptionsHold interpreting every Requires clause, and CallableCounterexampleReplayer.Replay executing full programs via IrProgramInterpreter); peer-side budget AcquireLockAsync Lines 271-291 (`CacheLockRetryMilliseconds = 25`, `CacheLockWait = 1s`, Lines 5-6); classification into run-wide Unavailable at `SharpProof.Worker\SharpProofWorker.cs` Lines 224-227, 395-397.
**Description**: TryReadAsync performs the entire cached-refutation revalidation - one concrete IR-program execution plus assumption evaluation per stored claim - while holding the directory's single FileShare.None lock. Cost scales with claim count x program size (per-program bounded by CompilerPreparedBody.MaximumInstructions, but multiplied across claims and Requires clauses, never checkpointed or yielding the lock). Any concurrent verification sharing the cache directory waits behind an effectively unbounded critical section; AcquireLockAsync retries for a hardcoded 1 second then lets the final IOException escape, which the read path converts to LastReadUnavailable=true and the write path to false - stamped WorkerCacheStatus.Unavailable for the whole run. On busy multi-project trees with refutation-rich caches this becomes steady-state: cache benefit silently disappears exactly when contention exists, and monitoring reports disk-class "Unavailable" for a healthy cache. DISTINCTNESS: #177 recorded that losers of lock ACQUISITION were classified Unavailable via FileShare.None with NO retry/wait; the tree now has the 25ms/1s retry, so #177's stated mechanism is partially remediated - this finding is the residual and distinct cause the retry cannot fix: unbounded HOLD time inside the critical section, not acquisition timing. #121/#220/#269/#270 address other aspects of the same file.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Populate the cache with an entry containing many refuted claims over sizable prepared bodies.
2. Process A verifies against that cache (its TryReadAsync replays all claims in-lock; observable via the held `.sharp-proof-cache.lock`).
3. While A holds the lock, start process B verifying the same project: B spins in AcquireLockAsync for ~1 s, then reports CacheStatus=Unavailable (and skips caching) although the directory is healthy; serial rerun shows normal Hit/Written behavior.
4. Grow the cached claim count - the window during which any peer is guaranteed to exceed 1 s grows with it.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (lock span, workload placement, retry ceiling, Unavailable stamping all line-verified); Medium on day-to-day frequency.
**Status**: Resolved by taking a validated cache snapshot, releasing the directory lock during replay, and reacquiring it for identity-checked admission and capacity updates. `CacheReadReleasesDirectoryLockDuringReplay` verifies that a concurrent reader can hit the same cache while the first reader is paused in replay; the focused cache suite passes 4/4.

### 303. [RESOLVED a3b1f7558] WaitForStartAsync's 30-Second Startup Deadline Cannot Fire While Blocked on the Start-Line Read - the Coded Watchdog Is Dead for Redirected stdin, Leaving Only Parent-Death/Launcher-Kill as Containment

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker\Program.cs` (Lines 252-269 `WaitForStartAsync`; call site expecting false -> exit 125; cancellation plumbing Lines 111-124). Counterpart writer: `SharpProof.Host\LinuxWorkerProcess.cs` Line 77 (`process.StandardInput.WriteLine(StartMessage)`); parent-death backstop Lines 146-167.
**Description**: The method creates a 30-second CancellationTokenSource and passes its token to `Console.In.ReadLineAsync(timeoutBoundary.Token)`, with a ContinueWith arm mapping a non-completed read to null -> return false -> exit 125. That design only works if the token can abort a PENDING read. `Console.In` hands out the runtime's synchronized TextReader wrapper, whose async Read APIs execute the blocking synchronous read and do not observe the supplied token once the read is pending. Consequently, if the launcher spawns the worker but wedges before writing "SharpProof.Start/1" and without closing stdin's pipe - precisely the stuck-launcher scenario the deadline exists to bound - the worker blocks inside ReadLineAsync indefinitely: exit-125-after-30-seconds is unreachable, and the guard degrades to "fires only if stdin closes or delivers a wrong line". Containment then rests entirely on PDEATHSIG installation and the launcher's external kill escalation, neither of which helps when the launcher process is alive but hung. No test exercises the 30-second path (the parent-death fixture asserts survival under a blocked read, never the deadline), so the dead guard is unpinned and unnoticed. Distinct from #120 (readiness-signaling race in the launcher handoff): different phase and failure mode - the startup-release wait whose explicit time bound cannot engage; #144/#145 are launcher-side probe defects; #175 is the launcher's own wait-loop token misuse.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Spawn the worker exactly like LinuxWorkerProcess.Start (redirected stdin/out, `--start-stdin --parent-pid <live pid>`) but never write the start message and never close the child's stdin, keeping the parent alive.
2. Wait past 30 seconds: the worker process still exists - WaitForStartAsync never returned false and exit 125 never happened, despite timeoutBoundary having expired within milliseconds of the read starting.
3. Control: close the child's stdin instead - ReadLineAsync completes with null and the worker exits 125 promptly, confirming the deadline logic was intended for exactly the blocked case it cannot cover.
**Confidence**: Medium (unreachable-guard conclusion follows from Console.In's documented synchronized-wrapper semantics; residual uncertainty limited to future BCL changes, not the pinned toolchain).

### 304. CallableCounterexampleReplayer Swallows InvalidOperationException From Interpreter/Factory State Machines Into CounterexampleReplayFailed, Masking Infrastructure Faults as Per-Claim Evidentiary Abstentions

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker\CallableCounterexampleReplayer.cs` (Blanket arms Lines 106-107 wrapping the whole replay body; consumption at `SharpProof.Worker\CallableVerifier.cs` Lines 247-249 and `SharpProof.Worker\VerificationCache.cs` Lines 578-582; downstream run-level escalation pinned by `SharpProof.Worker.Test\WorkerTests.cs` Lines 1234-1245/4277-4281).
**Description**: The entire replay - model extraction, IrProgramInterpreter.Execute, witness rebinding, final obligation evaluation - sits inside try/catch where both `ArgumentException` AND `InvalidOperationException` arms return CounterexampleReplayFailed. The ArgumentException arm is accepted #266 territory (ill-formed UTF-16 reference payloads escaping Evaluate). The adjacent InvalidOperationException arm has no analogous data-driven justification: nothing in the replay input contract produces it from hostile model input; it can only originate from internal contract violations of the IR factory/program-interpreter state machines or misuse bugs surfacing mid-replay. Folding such faults into CounterexampleReplayFailed relabels a probable infrastructure defect as an evidentiary abstention on one claim; because WorkerResultAssembler.Classify escalates that reason to a run-level WorkerRunFailureReason.CounterexampleReplayFailed/Failed, one latent interpreter bug converts an otherwise-definitive verification into a red run with a misleading diagnosis pointing at counterexample quality rather than at the faulting component. The same masking applies when the cache layer replays stored refutations (VerificationCache.cs Lines 578-582), where it additionally masquerades as cache-entry invalidation. Distinct from #266 (the ArgumentException arm with a concrete ill-formed-payload throw source - a data-dependent divergence class): this is the OTHER catch arm, a bug-masking channel that fixing #266 would not touch.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Inject (or regress) an InvalidOperationException-throwing path reachable during replay in a callable with [Ensures] whose backend query returns SAT.
2. Run verification: the refutation replay dies at the injected fault, the catch relabels it CounterexampleReplayFailed, and the claim is reported Unknown with that reason.
3. Observe the run summary: `runStatus: failed`, `failureReason: counterexampleReplayFailed` - no trace, log row, or error code identifies the actual invalid-operation fault or its stack; contrast removing only the Line-107 arm, which surfaces the true exception type at the policy boundary as InfrastructureFailure.
**Confidence**: Medium (masking behavior and both consumption sites certain; no live in-tree thrower demonstrated today, so severity rests on the latent-fault framing).

### 305. [RESOLVED 92e319819] Nonzero Worker Exit Codes Are Forwarded Verbatim Into the Launcher's Reserved Exit-Code Vocabulary, Fabricating False Verdict Semantics (Inverse of #179)

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher\Program.cs` (Final mapping Lines 220-230: `if (exitCode == 0) return resultExitCode; ... return exitCode;`; RunWorker returning raw completion.ExitCode at Lines 278-282; NoResultFailure synthesis Lines 169-180); reserved map `LauncherProjections.generated.cs` (Lines 12-22); worker's own conventions `SharpProof.Worker\Program.cs` (usage failure -> 2, success -> 0, write failure -> 3, containment -> 125).
**Description**: When the worker dies without writing a result, NoResultFailure synthesizes a fail-closed Failed/InfrastructureFailure response, and ValidateAndReport maps that synthesized verdict to resultExitCode = 3. But the final mapping returns `exitCode == 0 ? resultExitCode : exitCode` - ANY nonzero process exit code overrides the synthesized verdict. The launcher's own vocabulary reserves small integers: 2 = invalid invocation, 3 = failed run, 4 = canceled, 5 = refuted verdict, 6 = policy-incomplete/assumptions. A dead worker's incidental exit code therefore masquerades as a semantic verdict: worker exits 2 without a result (strict argv pattern-match fails under launcher-worker version skew) and the launcher returns 2, which every consumer reads as misconfigured invocation while the real cause is deployment skew - and the synthetic result.json on disk says InfrastructureFailure/no_result, contradicting the exit signal. Symmetrically, any wrapper exiting 5 or 6 makes the launcher emit refutation/policy-failure codes for a run whose published artifacts say Failed/no-result. The exitCode == 0 arm proves the intended design is verdict supremacy (a result-less run exiting 0 is correctly reported as 3/failure); forwarding nonzero raw codes defeats exactly that invariant asymmetrically. Distinct from #179 (no-result cause LOSS for out-of-band codes): this is collision of incidental codes with the RESERVED small-code space; the pinned test covers only 137/139.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Run Program.RunMain with a runWorker stub (same seam as WorkerMsBuildIntegrationTests.LauncherRepairsMalformedWorkerResultInProcess) that writes nothing to --result and returns 2.
2. Observe: a synthetic Failed/InfrastructureFailure result.json is written, stderr prints "SharpProof worker failed closed with exit code 2.", and RunMain returns 2 - identical to the launcher's own usage-error code.
3. Repeat with stub return 5: launcher exits 5, the documented refutation code, although nothing was refuted and the published verdict is Failed/no_result.
**Confidence**: High (mechanism traced across both processes; colliding small-code space unpinned by tests).

### 306. [RESOLVED bdddae0c4] Result-Repair Path Destroys the Original Worker Evidence In Place, Conflates "Unavailable" With "Binding-Invalid" in the Persisted Record, and Republishes the Fabrication With Doubled Machine-Readable Reporting

**Status**: Resolved for protocol-valid, request-binding-invalid responses. The launcher now preserves the original bytes and fails closed without publication, while unreadable, oversized, or structurally invalid output still receives the existing authoritative `worker.malformed_result` envelope. The earlier claim that the second validation duplicated claim diagnostics was not reproducible at the current implementation: invalid validation returns before the claim-reporting loop.

That destroyed claims, models, counterexamples, and precise validation context, and conflated a binding mismatch with unavailable output. The handler now distinguishes protocol-valid responses from unreadable, oversized, or structurally invalid output: binding-invalid bytes are retained and never published, while only the latter continue through authoritative synthetic repair. **Reproduction Steps**:
