2. Flip one character of its "inputHash" value; re-run the launcher with identical arguments.
3. The fixed launcher exits 3, reports the precise `response.input_mismatch`, preserves result.json byte-for-byte, emits no `worker.malformed_result`, and publishes nothing. A `not-json` result remains covered by `LauncherRepairsMalformedWorkerResultInProcess` and still receives the synthetic malformed envelope.
**Confidence**: High; the focused launcher regression suite passes 2/2, including the byte-preservation and reserved-exit normalization case plus the existing malformed-output repair.

### 309. [RESOLVED e0c690937, 1bd817fda] Compact Wire Form Erases the Only Carriers of Assumption Used Bits - Every Oversized Response With a Used Assumption Is Rejected by the Launcher as Malformed, and Validation Runs on Different Bytes Than Publication

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol\ProtocolJson.cs` (Compaction nulling claim rows at Lines 639-661, esp. `result.Assumptions = null` when the owning callable declares non-empty assumptions; summary recomputation Lines ~759-776 with SummaryAssumptionsMatch tuple compare; effect-evidence tuple derived from `(value.Assumptions ?? [])` at Lines ~607-620); producers setting Used=true exclusively on claim rows `SharpProof.Worker\CallableClaimResultAssembler.cs` (Lines 65-70) and `EffectClaimResultAssembler.cs` (Line 76); rules forcing all other rows to Used==false `ProtocolModel.generated.cs` (Lines 854, 869); publish path `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher\Program.cs` (validate deserialized object graph, then publish re-serialized compacted bytes).
**Description**: `WorkerAssumptionEvidence.Used == true` is legal ONLY on WorkerClaimResult.Assumptions rows - the manifest rule and callable-result rule force every manifest/callable row to Used == false, and production assemblers set Used = true only when building claim rows. SerializeResponse's size fallback CompactClaimAssumptions replaces exactly those rows with null whenever the owning callable declares non-empty assumptions - i.e., it targets precisely the rows carrying the protocol's only Used information. Three downstream consequences: (1) Summary divergence (always fires when any assumption was used): published Summary.Assumptions was computed pre-compaction with Used=k>0; the launcher deserializes the compact bytes and ValidateSummary recomputes SummarizeAssumptions from callable rows (all false) plus claim rows (now null), yielding Used=0; SummaryAssumptionsMatch tuple-compare fails -> error `summary.assumptions` -> ValidateAndReport exits 3 and rewrites the run as worker.malformed_result. (2) Effect-evidence divergence (trusted/proven effect claims): hasTrustedProvenance/trustedProvenanceUsed compute from `(value.Assumptions ?? [])`; a compacted row is null so both are false, and the mandatory Proven/TrustedCompleteBoundary tuple can never match -> `response.effect_evidence`. (3) Validation/publication skew: the launcher validates the deserialized graph but PublishOutputs re-validates the UNCOMPACTED in-memory response and only then serializes with compaction - the verdict is computed on bytes that were never validated; the BuildTask re-validation re-parses the published compact bytes and would independently trip symptom 1. The worker cannot notice: its self-validation runs before compaction. The compact-form test pins only Used == false fixtures via plain Validate, leaving the used-assumption path unpinned. Distinct from #230 (duplicate-key ArgumentException in CompactClaimAssumptions, GroupBy-guarded) and remediated #231 (authority rejecting the null marker): this is the protocol's OWN summary/effect checks consuming null-derived facts.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Replicate the OversizedAssumptionExpansionUsesAValidatedCompactClaimAssumptions fixture (>16 MiB uncompressed), but mark one claim row's UserAssume assumption Used = true (exactly what CallableClaimResultAssembler produces for a proof core citing Contract.Assume).
2. SerializeResponse compacts; observe the published JSON has `"assumptions":null` on that row while `summary.assumptions.used == 1`.
3. ValidateForRequest (launcher path) reports `summary.assumptions`; add a Proven/TrustedCompleteBoundary effect claim and `response.effect_evidence` appears as well. The launcher exits 3 and labels the run MalformedResult.
**Confidence**: High (sole-carrier rule, compaction target selection, pre/post-compaction recomputation, launcher wiring, worker validation ordering, and test blind spot verified statically across both assemblies).

### 310. [RESOLVED 3b448037c] The 16 MiB Protocol Size Invariant Stops at WorkerProtocolJson - the Shipped MSBuild Consumer Buffers Published Protocol Files With File.ReadAllBytes and No Cap, Turning an Enlarged Publication Into an Unhandled Out-of-Memory Task Crash

**Location**: Invariant `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol\ProtocolJson.cs` (MaximumJsonBytes enforced at every entry point; pinned by BoundedUtf8FileReaderRejectsOversizedAndInvalidFiles); violating consumer `SharpProof.BuildTasks\ValidatePublishedVerificationResult.cs` (Lines 53-54, 72-74, 80-81: `DecodeUtf8(File.ReadAllBytes(...))` with no size limit; catch filters at Lines 133/186 excluding OutOfMemoryException).
**Description**: MaximumJsonBytes is enforced at every WorkerProtocolJson entry point, but the BuildTask that re-validates published request.json/result.json/invocation results reads whole files with File.ReadAllBytes before any protocol call. A publication enlarged past the limit (torn publisher output, disk corruption, or any actor with write access to the obj-tree publication paths that #242/#272 establish as realistic) is fully buffered - multi-GiB allocations end in OutOfMemoryException, which the task's filter deliberately omits, so the task faults unhandled instead of taking its designed fail-closed path (Log.LogError + TryInvalidateRejectedPublication). The launcher's equivalent read is capped and fully caught, proving the intended contract; the consumer half of the handshake simply doesn't implement it. Secondary asymmetry in the same family: SerializeRequest performs no size check while SerializeResponse does - harmless today because the worker's reader caps, but the protocol's own producer API can mint unrepresentable requests. Distinct from #172 (ProtocolJson's own reader TOCTOU/cancellation, since bounded by the fixed-size buffer): different component with NO check at all, and a different failure class (OOM escaping a catch filter vs slow growth).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Run a verified build with publications enabled; replace `obj/.../SharpProof/result.json` with a multi-GiB file (e.g., `truncate -s 4G`).
2. Rebuild: ValidatePublishedVerificationResult.Execute calls File.ReadAllBytes(resultPath) and buffers 4 GiB before any parse.
3. On a constrained agent the process hits OOM; the exception is outside the catch filter, so the task crashes the build unhandled instead of logging the invalid-publication error and invalidating the set.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (cap absence, catch-filter contents, launcher contrast all read directly); Medium-Low practical frequency.

### 311. Evidence-Authority Validation Is Structurally Unreachable Through the Public ValidateForRequest API - External Embedders Silently Receive Strictly Weaker Tamper-Detection Than the Shipped Launcher

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol\ProtocolJson.cs` - public overload (hardwires no authority; ValidateResponse called without evidenceAuthority) vs internal overload requiring IWorkerResponseEvidenceAuthority; authority hook consumed only inside ValidateResponse when non-null; interface `ResponseEvidenceAuthority.cs` is internal.
**Description**: After the evidence-authority tier was added (the layer binding response rows to the compiler artifact, shown essential on the compact wire form by the #231 saga), the only signatures able to supply it are internal. The public `WorkerProtocolJson.ValidateForRequest(response, requestHash, inputHash, manifest, request, versions[, grace])` always validates with no authority, so the authority-derived checks are skipped entirely. Every non-launcher embedder of the documented public API silently receives strictly weaker validation with no compile-time signal, while the shipped launcher uses the internal overload. The result is a two-tier trust boundary inside one public type: identical inputs yield IsValid == true publicly and IsValid == false internally (e.g., a response whose assumption rows disagree with the artifact). Either the authority parameter belongs on the public surface or the asymmetry should be documented; today it is neither. Distinct from #231 (which documented the launcher passing the authority and the authority rejecting the compact marker, since fixed): this is the accessibility split itself, not a rejection bug; not #185 (a different assembly's validator).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. From a test project referencing only public APIs, craft an otherwise-valid response whose effect-claim rows contradict the compiler artifact.
2. Call the public WorkerProtocolJson.ValidateForRequest(...) - result is valid; no public path can enable the authority machinery.
3. Run the launcher's internal-overload validation on the same input - authority-derived errors appear. Same assembly, same input, two verdicts.
**Confidence**: Medium (accessibility split and null-threading are verified facts; defect-vs-deliberate-internal-hardening inferred from the launcher being the sole entitled consumer).

### 313. statfs Magic Switch Classifies Every Unlisted Filesystem as "local" - Network/Cluster Filesystems (ceph, lustre, 9p, virtiofs, afs) Bypass the Publication Denylist Because the Name-Based Layer Is Only Reachable When statfs Fails

**Location**: `SharpProof.Host\LinuxPathIdentity.cs` (Denylist set Lines 52-56; primary classifier FindVisibleFileSystemType Lines ~1277-1306 with the four-entry magic switch and `_ => "local"` default; mountinfo fallback FindFileSystemType Lines ~1240-1275 consulted only when StatFs fails; gate RequireLocalPath Lines 137-154).
**Description**: RequireLocalPath rejects publication paths on unsupported filesystems, but the primary classifier maps statfs f_type through a four-entry switch: nfs, cifs, smb3, fuse, `_ => "local"`. Every magic number outside the table - including genuine network/cluster filesystems whose durability, locking, and consistency semantics motivated the guard: ceph, lustre, 9p, virtiofs (Docker Desktop/macOS shared mounts), afs - is silently certified "local" and admitted. The name-based layer can only ever see strings produced by the mountinfo-text fallback, i.e., only when StatFs fails; when statfs succeeds - the normal case - the richer name-based check is unreachable, so the two layers never compose: the magic table is the whole defense and it whitelists by default. Consequence: verifying onto a ceph/lustre/9p-backed output tree publishes verdicts, SARIF, and ownership markers onto infrastructure whose flock/fsync/rename semantics the entire publication mutual-exclusion design assumes but cannot get, with zero diagnostic - the opposite of the explicit "'X' filesystem" rejection the code promises at Line 150. Distinct from #243 (casefold fail-open logic, which merely mentions fs-type denial as a sibling check) and #272/#273 (validation-vs-use gap and deletion durability): no entry audits the completeness of the statfs switch or the fallback-only reachability of the denylist names.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Mount a cephfs (or any FS with a magic outside the four-entry table) at /mnt/verify - `stat -f -c %t /mnt/verify` shows e.g. 0xc36400.
2. Invoke LinuxPathIdentity.RequireLocalPath("/mnt/verify/proj/obj/result.json").
3. Observe acceptance: FindVisibleFileSystemType hits the default arm, UnsupportedRemoteFileSystems.Contains("local") is false, and the path is returned canonical; contrast an NFS mount, which is rejected with the explicit filesystem message.
**Confidence**: Medium (switch contents, default-arm admission, and fallback-only reachability verified by inspection; severity depends on deployments publishing onto non-table filesystems - common on shared CI homes).

### 315. Natural Worker Exit Never Sweeps the Session/Process Group - Orphaned Worker Descendants Survive the Success Path While Every Failure Path Group-Kills

**Location**: `SharpProof.Host\LinuxWorkerProcess.cs` (Exited return Lines 140-143; Dispose early-out at ~Line 180 skipping Terminate when process.HasExited; contrast Terminate ending in KillProcessGroup(process.Id) at Line 240 and TerminateNow at Lines 257-267; session creation via /usr/bin/setsid Lines 34, 57).
**Description**: Workers start under setsid, so the worker PID is its own session and process-group leader - exactly the identity kill(-pid, SIGKILL) relies on. That sweep runs on every abnormal path: Terminate ends with it for both the timeout arm and Dispose's live-process branch, and even Start's launch-failure finally calls TerminateNow, which group-kills after re-checking exit state. But the SUCCESS path performs no group accounting at all: WaitForExit returns Exited straight from Lines 140-143 without touching the group, and Dispose skips termination entirely when process.HasExited. Any descendant the worker leaked before exiting cleanly - a spawned helper that ignored cancellation, a double-forked daemon, a hung child of Z3/toolchain tooling - is reparented to init and runs indefinitely, with no signal ever sent to -workerPid. The asymmetry with TerminateNow (which deliberately sweeps even a dead leader's group) shows the invariant "no worker-group member outlives the handle" was intended to hold on all paths; the exited branches just never implement it. Grandchildren do not inherit the worker's PR_SET_PDEATHSIG (cleared across fork; EnterChildBoundaryRequired installs it only in the direct child), so nothing else reaps them. Distinct from #173 (Dispose race/handle-leak - its synchronization issues remediated in current code; that entry is about lock-free access, not which paths invoke the group kill): #115/#175/#144-#145/#240/#241 are different components or windows; this is the Host-side launcher-support class performing ZERO group action after clean completion.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Launch a stub worker via LinuxWorkerProcess.Start whose Main spawns `sleep 600` detached and then exits 0 without waiting.
2. Call WaitForExit: completion is Exited/0; Dispose takes the HasExited fast path and issues no kill(-pgid).
3. `ps -eo pid,pgid,cmd` in the container: the sleep persists indefinitely under the worker's old pgid. Perform the same run but hang the worker past terminationStart instead: Terminate group-kills the descendant, proving the sweep exists everywhere except the success path.
**Confidence**: Medium (code asymmetry fully verified; classification depends on intent - containment policy says leaked workers must not outlive the run, but no doc pins the success-path case).

### 316. [RESOLVED 477cd1fa2] BindPublicationSet Crash-Orphaned `<id>.set.<guid>.tmp` Temp Markers Are Never Swept by Anything

**Location**: `SharpProof.Host\LinuxPathIdentity.cs` (Temp creation Lines ~897-909: `item.MarkerPath + "." + Guid.NewGuid().ToString("N") + ".tmp"`; in-process cleanup only in the finally at Lines 920-930; torn-recovery TryRemoveTornPublicationMarker Lines 1033-1043 handles only exact `.set` paths); sweeper `SharpProof.Ir\AtomicFile.cs` SweepStaged glob `".sharpproof-*"` temp names (call sites AtomicFile.cs Lines ~13/149 and VerificationCache.cs Line 295 never pointed at `.sharpproof-publication` directories).
**Description**: Marker commits stage to `<sha256>.set.<guid>.tmp` inside each `.sharpproof-publication/` directory, flush durably, then rename to the final `.set`. The deleting finally executes only in-process: SIGKILL/OOM/power loss between temp creation and File.Delete leaves the orphan permanently. No recovery layer knows this name: TryRemoveTornPublicationMarker inspects only exact .set paths and well-formed content; AtomicFile.SweepStaged - the repo's only temp sweeper - globs `.sharpproof-*.tmp`, which neither matches the `<64hex>.set.<guid>.tmp` shape nor ever runs against the publication-metadata directory. Each hard-killed bind attempt therefore leaks one durable temp next to the ownership markers forever. Hygiene-class like accepted #219, but a distinct producer/pattern/site that the existing sweepers demonstrably miss; distinct from #242 (torn COMMITTED .set markers) and #220 (cache-dir staging accounting).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Start a build that enters BindPublicationSet and `kill -9` the MSBuild node while the temp marker exists (between creation and rename).
2. Inspect `obj/**/.sharpproof-publication/`: a `<hex>.set.<guid>.tmp` file remains.
3. Re-run builds repeatedly (and trigger the AtomicFile sweep paths): the orphan is never removed - SweepStaged's glob does not match it and is never pointed at the metadata directory; only manual deletion cleans it.
**Confidence**: High (producer pattern, in-process-only cleanup, non-matching sweeper glob, and sweeper call-site directories all verified); severity low.
**Status**: Resolved by sweeping marker-scoped temporary files while the publication lock is held and synchronizing the metadata directory. The orphan-marker recovery regression passes.

### 317. Per-Invocation GUID Leaks Through CompilerVisibleProperty Into GeneratedMSBuildEditorConfig.editorconfig, Defeating Incremental Compilation for Every Verification-Enabled Project

**Location**: `SharpProof.Verifier\buildTransitive\SharpProof.Verifier.targets` (GUID mint at Line ~126: `_SharpProofInvocationId Condition="'$(_SharpProofInvocationId)' == ''"` -> NewGuid; `_SharpProofExpectedInvocationDirectory`/`_SharpProofInvocationDirectory` = runs/<guid> at Lines ~128/131; `_SharpProofCompilerManifestPath` = Combine(invocation dir, 'compiler-manifest.json') at Line 146; hook at Line 92); `SharpProof.Verifier\buildTransitive\SharpProof.Verifier.props` (Line 33: `<CompilerVisibleProperty Include="_SharpProofCompilerManifestPath" />`); consumer proof `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\FinalCompilationCollector.cs` (Line 7 reads analyzer-config key `build_property._SharpProofCompilerManifestPath`); SDK chain: Microsoft.Managed.Core.targets writes CompilerVisibleProperty values into `$(IntermediateOutputPath)$(MSBuildProjectName).GeneratedMSBuildEditorConfig.editorconfig` and adds it to @(EditorConfigFiles); Microsoft.CSharp.Core.targets lists @(EditorConfigFiles) among CoreCompile Inputs and passes them to Csc.
**Description**: Every build that runs _SharpProofInitializeVerify mints a fresh _SharpProofInvocationId GUID (condition `'== ''` means new per project evaluation per msbuild invocation) and derives _SharpProofCompilerManifestPath under runs/<guid>/ - that property is registered as compiler-visible and read back by the collector through the generated editorconfig, proving the value travels there. Because the manifest path embeds a fresh GUID on every invocation, the generated editorconfig content differs on every build, the write-always target rewrites it, its timestamp bumps, and CoreCompile's Inputs check always fails: csc, all analyzers/generators, and then the entire verifier pipeline (launcher + worker + Z3) rerun on every no-op `dotnet build` of any project with SharpProofVerify=true (strict profile makes that opt-out-free via AnalyzerConsumer.props/portable targets). Secondary effect: during design-time builds the setting PropertyGroup condition excludes DesignTimeBuild, so the property flips empty and back around every F5, churning the same file. A stable path already exists ($(_SharpProofVerifyDirectory), computed identically without the invocation id). Verified mechanically against both repo files and installed SDK 9.0.317 targets.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. In the canonical container create a consumer project with strict profile plus both SharpProof packages; build twice with zero source changes.
2. After build 2, observe obj/Debug/net8.0/<Project>.GeneratedMSBuildEditorConfig.editorconfig contains a DIFFERENT build_property._sharpproofcompilermanifestpath (.../runs/<new-guid>/compiler-manifest.json) than after build 1.
3. Observe CoreCompile/Csc re-executed on build 2 (-v:n shows no "Skipping target CoreCompile") followed by a fresh launcher spawn although no input changed. Delete only the GUID from that one line and rebuild: the file stops changing and CoreCompile skips again, isolating the GUID as the cause.
**Confidence**: High (each link verified statically; blast radius is all verification-enabled projects).

### 318. Retained Cleanup Anchors Invoke Environment.FailFast Asynchronously After RunVerifier Has Returned - Killing Reused MSBuild Nodes (and Unrelated Concurrent Builds) After the Task Reported Its Result

**Location**: `SharpProof.BuildTasks\RunVerifier.cs` (Callback construction ~Lines 434-449: authenticationFailure null ONLY when terminal cause == Canceled; retention-deadline arm and receipt-recheck arm inside ObserveCleanupAnchorAsync ~Lines 813-855 invoking anchor.AuthenticationFailure; overflow-eviction invoke ~Lines 805-807; sink HandleContainmentAuthenticationFailure -> Environment.FailFast at Lines ~720-728; deferral gate Lines 366-385); supervisor exit-without-receipt semantics `SharpProof.BuildTasks\VerifierProcessSupervisor.cs` Lines 172-182 (return 125 with no receipt when cleanup incomplete or the protected launcher outlives its grace).
**Description**: When a run terminates by timeout/output-limit while the supervisor cannot finish within its bounded window, Execute retains a cleanup anchor whose authenticationFailure callback is non-null for every terminal cause except Canceled. The anchor lives on background tasks (ObserveCleanupAnchorAsync): if the armed supervisor later exits without an authenticated cleanup receipt (exactly when a descendant could not be proven dead or the launcher needed >1 s to notice its killed worker), or if the 30 s retention deadline expires, the anchor invokes the callback, which calls Environment.FailFast. This executes AFTER Execute() has returned ExitCode=124/-1 and MSBuild has raised its own error and moved on: on node-reuse hosts the process dies asynchronously seconds-to-minutes later, aborting whatever else that node is doing (parallel project builds, subsequent scheduled requests), with a failfast dump naming neither the original timeout nor the wedged descendant. Notably, CleanupRetryBudgetMilliseconds bounds what used to hang the node (#241) into precisely these no-receipt 125 exits - converting the old livelock into this post-hoc FailFast. DISTINCTNESS vs #240: #240 acknowledges only the INLINE/synchronous missing-receipt FailFast during Execute; the residual defect reported here is the asynchronous, post-task anchor callback crashing nodes outside any task boundary - distinct site (ObserveCleanupAnchorAsync/RetainCleanupAnchor vs RequireSupervisorCleanupReceipt), timing, and trigger.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Run a verified build sized so a callable times out while the launcher needs >1 s after worker death to publish (or block a worker in D-state so the supervisor's bounded cleanup completes=false).
2. Let the wall budget expire: RunVerifier logs timeout, sets ExitCode=124, the targets raise their error, and the build finishes failing normally - but the pipes were open at decision time, so the anchor was retained with a non-null callback.
3. Within <=30 s the anchor observes the supervisor's receipt-less 125 exit (or hits the retention deadline) and Environment.FailFast terminates the MSBuild node after completion - observable as abrupt node death while other work runs on it; replacing the callback body with logging removes the crash, proving the anchor path fired.
**Confidence**: High (every cited branch read; trigger chain statically complete; frequency depends on launcher/descendant slow-fail timing that CI load routinely produces).

### 319. [RESOLVED e36ffcd63] Pre-Compile Invalidation Pass Runs Before _SharpProofActiveCacheDirectory Exists - Cache-Alias Screening Is Silently Inert Exactly Where Stale Publications Are Deleted Before the Compiler Can See Them

**Location**: `SharpProof.Verifier\buildTransitive\SharpProof.Verifier.targets` (Early pass hook Line 45 `BeforeTargets="ResolveReferences"` passing CachePath="$(_SharpProofActiveCacheDirectory)"; the only assignment of that property is inside _SharpProofInitializeVerify whose target hooks BeforeTargets="GenerateMSBuildEditorConfigFileShouldRun;GenerateMSBuildEditorConfigFile" at Line 92 - chained just before CoreCompile, long after ResolveReferences); inert-guard consequence `SharpProof.BuildTasks\InvalidatePublishedResult.cs` (Lines 161-163 blank -> null; Lines 183-194 aliasesCache skipped entirely when resolvedCachePath == null).
**Description**: The two invalidation passes execute in the inverse order of their guard completeness. The early pass deletes marker-owned published members before ResolveReferences - i.e., before compilation can observe them, its raison d'etre - yet at that moment _SharpProofActiveCacheDirectory has never been set (it is created only inside _SharpProofInitializeVerify). CachePath therefore evaluates to empty string, Present() drops it, and the entire aliasesCache conflict screen (cache directory vs publication members/markers/input paths/worker tree, plus the paired "output, input, cache, and worker paths must be distinct" error) does not run in this pass. For a misconfiguration where a custom SharpProofVerifyCacheDirectory contains or overlaps a publication path, the pre-compile pass deletes publications from inside the user-designated cache tree with no diagnostic; only the second pass (inside _SharpProofInitializeVerify, which has the cache path) fails the build afterwards. The build still fails closed overall, but the guard layer is dead code in the one pass whose deletions precede compilation, and deletion happens before the configured rejection can prevent it. Distinct from #227 (stale publications left by failed runs), #242/#243 (torn markers/casefold fail-open in LinuxPathIdentity), #256/#257 (escaping/TFM partitioning in the same targets file): this is a target-ordering/condition gap in the invalidation sequence itself.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Consumer project with the Verifier package, verify enabled, and `-p:SharpProofVerifyCacheDirectory=$(IntermediateOutputPath)SharpProof` (cache root = the directory holding request.json/result.json/compiler-manifest.json).
2. Build once successfully so publications and markers exist; touch a source; build again with binary logging (-bl).
3. In the log, the pre-ResolveReferences invalidation executes with CachePath="" and reports no cache error while deleting publication members; the identical task invocation later carries the cache path and raises the alias error - proving the screening existed but was absent in the earlier, pre-compilation pass.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (target hook order confirmed against installed SDK sources; property lifetime read directly); Low-Medium severity (second pass restores fail-closed before compilation).

**Status**: Resolved by computing the effective active cache path in the pre-`ResolveReferences` target before invoking `InvalidatePublishedResult`. The integration regression confirms a cache alias fails before invalidation and preserves the existing request, result, and manifest bytes.

### 320. Per-Tree Line-Map Digest Is Recomputed From Scratch for Every Location x Tree Pair - O(Locations x Trees x Source-Lines) JSON Re-Serialization Inside the Compiler, Uncancelable

**Location**: `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact\CompilerSourceLocationAuthority.cs` Lines 20-51 (HasValidLineMap recomputing the digest at Line ~26 before any cheap check); fan-out Lines 53-108 (HasValidLocationGeometry calls HasValidLineMap BEFORE geometry tests; FindUniqueTree scanning every tree per location); digest itself `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact\CompilationFingerprint.cs` Lines 17-30 (ComputeLineMapSha256 JSON-serializes the entire entry array per call); callers `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\CompilerManifestArtifactProducer.cs` Lines 180-200/234-245; effect-replay repetition in CompilerManifestArtifact.cs Lines ~790-836 and CompilationFingerprint ValidateShape re-validating every tree at least twice per artifact.
**Description**: HasValidLineMap never trusts the stored LineMapSha256; every invocation re-serializes the tree's complete line map to UTF-8 JSON and SHA-256s it. Because FindUniqueTree must prove uniqueness, it evaluates HasValidLocationGeometry - and therefore the full digest - for EVERY candidate tree for EVERY bound location (callables + claims + compiler-error diagnostics + effect-replay events). Total work is Theta(Locations x Trees x TotalSourceLines) serialized bytes, all on the compiler thread during the compilation-end action. None of these loops consult a CancellationToken (token checks exist only in CaptureTree/TryGet), so a build cancel cannot interrupt them. There is no memoization despite the digest being immutable per snapshot. A mid-size verified project (~300 callables+claims, 20 trees, 200k total source lines) performs billions of entry-serializations before MSBuild sees a diagnostic - multi-minute CPU-bound stalls that look like a hung csc/VBCSCompiler node. DISTINCTNESS: #277 is unbounded recursion depth (StackOverflow) in CompilerRelationalSummaryProvider; here depth is capped everywhere and the defect is quadratic recomputation volume with no cancellation. #182/#183 are path-normalization/hash-input correctness - these digests compute correctly, just catastrophically often. #232 is ambiguity aborting emission.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Enable verification on a project with ~4 machine-generated files of ~50k lines each (~200k total lines across 20 trees) and ~300 selected callables/claims carrying locations.
2. Build: CompilerManifestArtifactProducer drives 300 x FindUniqueTree; each of the 300x20 pairs re-serializes a 50k-entry line map. Observe a multi-minute CPU-bound stall inside the analyzer with no diagnostic progress; cancelling the build does not interrupt the loops.
3. Instrument ComputeLineMapSha256: call count approximates Locations x Trees (+ per-replay-event duplicates), each call hashing megabytes.
**Confidence**: High (every site quoted; complexity mechanical; absence of memoization/cancellation verified across all callers).

### 321. Line-Map Evidence Stores One Row Per Source Line With the Absolute Mapped Path Repeated on Every Row - Manifest Size Scales O(total source lines) Against the Fixed 16 MiB Budget, Deterministically Killing Whole Projects With Opaque SP0049

**Location**: `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\CompilerCompilationCapture.cs` Lines 118-158 (CaptureTree: one CompilerSourceLineMapEntry per text line via text.Lines.Select, MappedPath resolving to the full absolute physical path per row); model serialization `CompilerCompilationModel.generated.cs` Lines 73-88 (no omission); hard cap `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact\CompilerManifestArtifact.cs` (Serialize throws when byte count > MaximumBytes = WorkerProtocolJson.MaximumJsonBytes = 16 MiB); failure conversion `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\FinalCompilationCollector.cs` Lines 41-51 (blanket catch -> single manifest-failure diagnostic -> SP0049). The worker cannot waive the payload: HasValidLineMap requires LineMap non-empty.
**Description**: Every line of every syntax tree contributes a row of roughly 130-200 bytes including property names and the repeated absolute mappedPath string. The 16 MiB manifest budget therefore saturates at roughly 10^5 total source lines across the compilation - routinely exceeded by generator output (EF scaffolding, parser tables, designer files), even in a single file. Past the cliff, Serialize throws "The compiler manifest exceeds the worker input byte limit.", the collector's blanket catch collapses the entire manifest into one diagnostic naming neither file nor cause, and the build dies with SP0049 ("did not receive the required final compiler manifest"). The evidence could be encoded as mapped segments (runs of consecutive lines sharing one mapped path/delta) instead of per-line materialization; nothing in the format requires per-line rows. Distinct from #232 (ambiguous #line geometry) and #279 (profile handshake mismatch) - different triggers of the same opaque SP0049 symptom; from #182 (path-equality abort) and #183 (hash reproducibility): here paths hash fine but blow a fixed byte budget by construction. No existing entry addresses manifest payload scaling.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Add one generator-emitted (or plain) .cs file with ~150k lines to a SharpProofVerify=true project whose checkout path is ~55 characters.
2. Build: estimated line-map payload ~150k x ~140 B = ~20 MB > 16 MiB; CompilerManifestArtifactJson.Serialize throws at the cap check.
3. Observe the build fail with exactly one CompilerManifestFailureRule surfaced as SP0049; shrink the file below ~90k lines and the identical project builds green, isolating size as the sole variable.
**Confidence**: High mechanism (row schema, per-row absolute path, cap constant, conversion chain all read directly); Medium-High overall.

### 322. [RESOLVED f2d3e5882] Collector IL-Summary Translator Supports Overflow-Checked Int64 Arithmetic But Rejects Plain Unchecked Int64 Add/Sub/Mul - the Default Compiled Form of Long Math Systematically Abstains

**Location**: `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\CompilerImplementationIlSummaryLowerer.cs` - Translator.Arithmetic Lines 1058-1126: the checked/Div/Rem branch accepts both Int32 and Int64 (TryIntegerRange explicitly handles System_Int64), while unchecked Add/Sub/Mul fall through to `if (left.SpecialType != SpecialType.System_Int32) { return false; }`; Int32 gets WrapInt32 emulation; Int64 gets nothing. Supporting intent evidence: ldc.i8 decoded, Int64 comparisons/equality fully supported, Int64 parameters/results admitted by IsCandidate.
**Description**: C# compiles by default in unchecked context, so a static scalar method in a referenced assembly doing ordinary long addition emits add (not add.ovf) - which Arithmetic rejects, aborting the entire translation with reason UnsupportedIl (and the false return never sets a specific reason). Any caller lowerer hits MissingCallSummary -> the referencing callable fails PrepareBody with UnsupportedBody, degrading all of its claims. Net effect: the COMMON form of Int64 arithmetic is unsupported while the rarer checked{} form works - an inversion strongly indicating oversight rather than policy (a WrapInt64 mirroring WrapInt32 modulo 2^64 is expressible with a subtract-before-compare restructure). Fail-closed abstentions (never wrong verdicts) but systematic coverage loss for BCL-style scalar helpers using long arithmetic. Distinct from #214 (frontend Divide admission row), #222 (interpreter unchecked remainder arm), #221 (SMT proof-path assertion), #151 (encoder/replay string matrix): different components and mechanisms; this is the collector's IL-translator operand-type gate.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Compile `class Lib { public static long Inc(long x) => x + 1L; }` to a DLL (default unchecked context) and reference it from a verified project calling Inc inside an annotated callable.
2. At collection, TryBuild translates ldarg.0/ldarg.1/add: Arithmetic reaches the Int32-only gate with SpecialType Int64 -> returns false -> translation aborted with reason UnsupportedIl.
3. Observe the callable degraded (UnsupportedBody -> claims Unknown) although the sibling `checked { x + 1L; }` variant (emitting add.ovf) lowers successfully - proving Int64 arithmetic support exists and only the unchecked opcode arms are gated off.
**Confidence**: Medium-High (code path verified end-to-end; classification rests on the Int32-vs-Int64 and checked-vs-unchecked asymmetries, both factual in the cited lines).

**Resolution**: Unchecked Int64 add, subtract, and multiply now lower through bounded modulo-2^64 IR arithmetic. Boundary and mixed-sign cases are covered by `WorkerTests.ImplementationIlScalarOpcodeMatrixBuildsExactSummaries`; checked operations and Int32 wrapping retain their existing paths.

### 323. [RESOLVED efea3ff04] Top-Level SummaryEvidence Canonicality Gate Orders by Pipe-Concatenated Ordinal Key While Its Producer Sorts Tuple-Wise - Latent Whole-Manifest-Rejection Twin of the Remediated #268

**Location**: Consumer gate `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact\CompilationFingerprint.cs` Lines 217-258 (ValidSummaryEvidence: concatenated key `(int)Origin + "|" + CallIdentity + "|" + EvidenceIdentity + "|" + EvidenceSha256` at ~Lines 245-246, strict-increase requirement via StringComparer.Ordinal.Compare(previous, key) >= 0 at ~Lines 247-252); producer ordering `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\CompilerRelationalSummaryProvider.cs` Lines 40-45 (OrderBy Origin .ThenBy CallIdentity .ThenBy EvidenceIdentity .ThenBy Sha - lexicographic TUPLE order), emitted verbatim by BuildSummaryEvidence. Enforcement fires at emission too (Producer.Create -> Json.Validate -> ValidateShape).
**Description**: '|' is 0x7C and outranks letters/digits, so tuple order and concatenated-key order diverge whenever one field value is a proper prefix of another field value in an adjacent row. Today the divergence is masked by a structural invariant: _authorities holds at most one authority per normalized method symbol, and CreateAuthority pins CallIdentity to the method's documentation-comment ID which always terminates in ')' - so no prefix relations occur among adjacent sorted rows. But the two orderings are DIFFERENT contracts: the producer guarantees tuple order, the validator demands concat-ordinal order, and nothing links them. Should pack identities ever share a prefix (pack id versioning like foo@1 / foo@10 is prefix-related!) or identities loosen beyond doc-ID shapes, adjacent legitimate rows invert the ordinal comparison and ValidSummaryEvidence returns false - throwing inside Producer.Create itself and collapsing the whole project into SP0049 exactly like #321's chain. Stricter failure mode than remediated #268: there the mismatch surfaces worker-side at decode; here it would break the BUILD. DISTINCTNESS vs #268: that occupied CompilerLoweredArtifact.ValidDependencyEvidence (per-summary-call DependencyEvidence arrays, fixed by commit 0c95dad38 to compare independent fields); this is the TOP-LEVEL compilation.SummaryEvidence gate with a different producer sort site, different data, and build-breaking consequence - untouched by the #268 fix.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Unit-level (deterministic today): evaluate StringComparer.Ordinal.Compare("2|X.Foo@1|shaA", "2|X.Foo@10|shaB") - the tuple-sorted pair yields a positive ordinal comparison, tripping the strict-increase guard.
2. Construct two spec-pack summaries for methods whose pack ids are prefix-related (requires extending PackIdentities), run the collector: SummaryEvidenceAuthorities emits tuple-correct rows; ValidateShape at emission throws and the build reports only the manifest-failure diagnostic/SP0049.
3. Contrast: reorder the same two rows so the concatenated keys ascend and validation passes - proving ordering-contract mismatch, not row content, is the fault.
**Confidence**: High on the mechanism (both comparators and emission-time enforcement verified line-by-line); Low on present-day reachability given the one-authority-per-method masking invariant.

### 326. [RESOLVED 03e09edbd] SPMETA009 Registers Only Binary String Concatenation - Compound-Assignment (`text += " => "`) and string.Concat(...) Synthesize Banned C# Expression Text Unreported

**Location**: `SharpProof.Meta.Analyzers\SharpProofSoundnessAnalyzer.cs` (Registration Lines 81-105: fragment analysis reachable only via OperationKind.BinaryOperator -> AnalyzeCSharpExpressionText, which requires IBinaryOperation { OperatorKind: Add } with System_String type; ALL assignment kinds route exclusively to CacheSoundnessRules.AnalyzeAssignment; invocations route to AnalyzeInvocation whose only content checks are forbidden-API/semantic-equals/cache writes - no expression-fragment arm). Test gap: the meta test project pins interpolated strings and plain `"(" + name + ") is not null"` concatenation only.

The operation-level implementation covers exactly two shapes: binary + and interpolations with holes. Roslyn lowers `text += " => ";` to ICompoundAssignmentOperation (never reaching the binary scanner) and `string.Concat(prefix, " && ", suffix)` to IInvocationOperation (reaching only unrelated logic), so both idioms synthesize banned expression text with zero diagnostics while the semantically identical `text = text + " => "` reports Error severity. This mirrors the #246 registration-gap class on a different rule and detector: fixing #246 (done in-tree - assignments now registered for cache analysis) did not and would not route those operations to AnalyzeCSharpExpressionText. Distinct from #187's recorded SPMETA009 hole (constant-only interpolated strings escaping while + was flagged, also apparently remediated): this is the compound-assignment and invocation-form escape - a different operation-kind registration gap.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. In a soundness-critical project analyze: `static string Key(string name) { var text = name; text += " is null"; return text; }` -> zero SPMETA009 diagnostics.
2. Change the middle statement to `text = text + " is null";` -> Error-severity SPMETA009 fires, isolating the registration asymmetry.
3. Variant: `return string.Concat(name, " == ");` -> likewise silent; the invocation reaches only AnalyzeInvocation's unrelated checks.
**Confidence**: High (registration list, cast targets, and method bodies read in full; test corpus exhaustively lacks both shapes).

### 331. [RESOLVED 9c902dbde] Early-Throw Paths of Invoke-SharpProofGateEvidence.ps1 Never Unlink the Previous Evidence File - a Stale Green performance.json Survives Failed Reruns on Persistent Checkouts

**Status**: Resolved by resolving the contained output and deleting the prior envelope, raw gate output, and stderr sidecars before source preflight. The standalone-gate architecture regression pins the cleanup path.

**Location**: `scripts\Invoke-SharpProofGateEvidence.ps1` (Pre-write throws at Lines 22-24 commit bind, 27-29 dirty worktree, 47-49 gate build failed, 53-56 missing exe/pdb; the ONLY write of $resolvedOutput is Lines 171-175 after full processing; exhaustive grep finds no Remove-Item/Delete of the output anywhere). Callers bind a fixed path: Invoke-SharpProofContainer.ps1 (pr-gates and performance cases -> artifacts/ci/performance.json); CI uploads it if:always(); compose.yaml bind-mounts ./artifacts and entrypoint.sh symlinks artifacts outside the disposable clone, making persistence structural.
**Description**: The script writes its output only at the very end. Every pre-flight throw (dirty tree, failed Release build of SharpProof.Gates, missing freshly-built binaries) aborts before the write, leaving any previously written evidence file untouched - typically the previous run's passed = true envelope. On the persistent container checkout (the documented operating mode), a later failing rerun therefore leaves artifacts/ci/performance.json claiming success for a tree that just failed; presence-based consumers (artifact upload-and-inspect flows like ci.yml's always() uploads, dashboards, receipt-style automation) attribute the old green verdict to the new run unless they revalidate SourceCommit/executable hashes inside the envelope. The repo's own hygiene standard proves intent otherwise: Test-SharpProofPilots.ps1 explicitly removes stale prior reports, a step entirely absent here. Mitigating factor noted honestly: the envelope embeds SourceCommit + executable hashes, so careful joiners can detect staleness - but nothing requires them to, and the CI upload path attaches whatever bytes exist. Distinct from #227 (stale launcher publications via SharpProof.Worker.Launcher/MSBuild validator compensation): different producer, artifact chain, and window.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. In a container checkout, run `docker compose run --rm tooling performance` successfully; observe artifacts/ci/performance.json with "passed": true.
2. Introduce a condition that fails the script's internal Release rebuild of SharpProof.Gates and rerun.
3. The script throws mid-flight; observe the step fails but artifacts/ci/performance.json still contains the previous run's passed envelope bound to the old commit/hashes.
4. Contrast: the sibling pilot flow deletes its stale report under the same circumstances.
**Confidence**: High mechanism (script read end-to-end; no deletion path exists; persistence verified through compose mount and entrypoint symlink); Medium severity because hash-carrying envelopes allow diligent consumers to detect staleness.

### 332. [RESOLVED 4640d06f3] Gate Exit-Code Taxonomy Conflates Infrastructure Crash With Threshold Failure (Both Exit 1) and Discards Completed Phase Results When `all` Crashes Mid-Run

**Status**: Resolved by giving gate threshold, usage, infrastructure, and partial-result outcomes distinct process codes. The `all` command now emits a structured partial envelope when either phase throws, preserving a completed corpus result when performance fails; focused `ProgramTests` cover both phase-failure paths. Threshold failures retain exit code 1 and successful runs retain exit code 0.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Gates\Program.cs` - failure exits returning 0/1 per phase (~Lines 45, 59, 87, 95); usage exit 2; crash path catch-all printing the exception and returning 1; `all` runs corpus then performance and serializes the combined object only AFTER BOTH complete.
**Description**: Three distinct outcomes share exit code 1: (a) a genuine measured-budget failure with structured Failures in the printed JSON; (b) an infrastructure crash - malformed contract.json, analyzer exception mid-corpus, PackageBuildSdkPin JsonException (#329), or a non-source-bound build hitting CreateStandaloneEnvelope - where no JSON is printed at all; and (c) partial completion, because `all` serializes only at the end: if corpus succeeds and performance throws, the already-computed corpus result (minutes of measurement, snapshot verdicts) is discarded unprinted and the process exits 1. Automation keyed on the exit code - the only signal guaranteed to exist - cannot distinguish "advisory overhead regressed past threshold" (revert the perf-affecting commit) from "the harness itself broke" (fix infra), and there is no distinct code for crash/partial even though the binary demonstrates a three-level taxonomy (0/1/2). For a toolchain whose launcher elsewhere maps typed failure reasons instead of raw exits, the gate's own boundary loses exactly that information. Distinct from #146 (missing source-binding envelope under `all` - orthogonal identity concern): even with envelopes added, crash-vs-failure-vs-partial remain indistinguishable at the process boundary; #179 covers the worker launcher, a different component.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Run `docker compose run --rm tooling corpus` with eng/acceptance/contract.json temporarily renamed: exit code 1 with only an exception trace on stderr - byte-identical exit status to a genuine snapshot-mismatch failure.
2. Run `tooling gates` (all) on a tree where corpus passes but performance throws (e.g., #329's commented global.json): the passing corpus measurements are never serialized, stderr shows only the performance exception, exit 1.
3. A consumer script recording "performance regression detected" from nonzero exit files an incorrect action item in both scenarios.
**Confidence**: High on behavior (all sites quoted; single serialize point verified); Medium on defect-vs-design classification - minimal exit taxonomies are common, but the partial-result loss in `all` and crash/failure conflation in a release gate have concrete operational cost.

### 337. Release-Evidence Publication Swaps Directories With Two Non-Crash-Safe Moves - Process Death Between Them Deletes PackageSource and Strands the Previous Bundle in an Unreferenced Hidden Backup Until Manual Recovery

After such a crash, every rerun fails at Resolve-Path ("path does not exist"), the orphaned .backup accumulates beside it, and nothing distinguishes recoverable state from corruption; the operator must notice and hand-move the hidden directory. 3. Rerun any dependent command (pack/pilots/package-consumers): fails at Resolve-Path with "PackageSource is not a directory"; grep confirms no script ever references *.backup to self-heal.
**Confidence**: High mechanics (both move sites, catch-only restoration, absence of recovery consumer, destination==source default all verified line-by-line); Low-Medium likelihood/severity (kill window is narrow, but the result bricks the release pipeline's input directory until manual recovery).

### 342. [RESOLVED 0f15534a4] Frontend Failure Minimizer Accepts Compile-Invalid Shrinks (CS0020) - Minimized Evidence Reports a Different Root Cause Than the Original Mismatch and True Semantic Minimization Stops Early

**Location**: `Tools\SharpProof.Fuzz\FuzzRunner.cs` (Lines 268-284: minimization predicate accepts ANY FuzzOracleStatus.Mismatch; failure Detail taken from the minimized result); enabling sites `Tools\SharpProof.Fuzz\FrontendFuzzing.cs` (compile-failure classified as Mismatch at Lines 1006-1014; raw integer rendering `(0L)`; generation-side zero-divisor guard at Lines 851-856 that the shrinker never re-applies; shrinker leaf candidates Integer(0)/Integer(1) and child replacement/binary rebuild paths).
**Description**: The generator carefully prevents rendering constant `/ 0L` because a literal zero divisor is compile error CS0020, and the pinned fixture cements that ANY non-compiling generated program is scored Mismatch. The structural shrinker can manufacture exactly that shape: for a failing tree containing e.g. Divide(Left(), Conditional(condition, Integer(0), Integer(2))) (legal to generate - the guard skips divisors whose constant value is unknown), GetCandidates offers the Integer(0) leaf as a type-compatible replacement for the Conditional child. The rewritten program renders ((left) / (0L)), fails emission with CS0020, and CompareBatch returns Mismatch("Generated C# did not compile: CS0020 ..."). Because the FuzzRunner predicate accepts any Mismatch, minimization adopts the candidate, restarts smaller, and finally records Minimized = the non-compiling one-liner with Detail = the CS0020 text. Two consequences: (a) nightly failure evidence attributes the mismatch to a trivial compile artifact instead of the original semantic divergence, sending debuggers down a dead end; (b) minimization terminates on a 3-node tree long before reaching the minimal SEMANTIC repro, destroying the diagnostic value of the retained Original/Minimized pair. The sibling IR shrinkers are immune because their predicates re-run semantics-aware oracles where abstention != mismatch, proving the frontend predicate is the odd one out. Distinct from #254/#280/#282/#194/#253 (classifier laundering, dead dimensions, seed aliasing, tautological comparisons): here comparisons are genuine but the MINIMIZER launders the failure class; no existing entry covers shrinker/predicate interplay.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Build a case exactly as the generator can produce: Binary(Divide, Left(), Conditional(Condition(), Integer(0), Integer(2))) where some genuine lowering divergence makes Compare return Mismatch.
2. Run CSharpStructuralShrinker.Minimize(case, candidate => oracle.Compare(candidate).Status == Mismatch) - the FuzzRunner predicate verbatim.
3. Observe the candidate replacing the Conditional child with Integer(0): source renders ((left) / (0L)), emit fails CS0020, predicate passes, minimization adopts it.
4. Inspect the resulting FuzzFailure: Minimized is the CS0020 one-liner and Detail is the compile error, not the original interpreter/runtime divergence.
**Confidence**: High (render form, generator/shrinker guard asymmetry, Mismatch classification of compile failures pinned by tests, predicate shape all statically verified).
