### 348. [RESOLVED fa58c7533] Interface-Targeted [SharpProofSuppress]/[SharpProofTrusted] Are Advertised by AttributeUsage, Actively Validated, Yet Consulted by Zero Consumers - Legal Suppression/Trust Declarations Silently Do Nothing

**Location**: `SharpProof.Attributes\SharpProofSuppressAttribute.cs` and `SharpProofTrustedAttribute.cs` (AttributeTargets includes Interface); sole scope authority consumed everywhere `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\SharpProofControlAttributePolicy.cs` Lines 120-137 (EnumerateScopes: method -> associated property -> containing-type chain -> assembly - NO interface walk); validation still reaches interfaces via ValidateDeclaredScope registered for SymbolKind.NamedType (SharpProofAnalyzerEngine.cs Lines ~132-139); collector mirror ClaimManifestBuilder TrustedAttributes iterates the same EnumerateScopes; selection-side mirror ContractSelectionInventory walks types+assembly only.
**Description**: Both control attributes declare AttributeTargets.Interface, so applying them to an interface compiles cleanly. ValidateDeclaredScope treats the interface as an in-scope NamedType: a blank reason produces SP0024 and a lookalike type produces SP0047 - the machinery actively polices these instances, proving they are considered live input. But every EFFECT consumer derives scopes from EnumerateScopes(method), which never consults implemented interfaces. Consequently an interface-scoped [SharpProofSuppress("reason")] never suppresses analysis of implementing methods, and interface-scoped [SharpProofTrusted] never selects anything analyzer-side nor emits TrustedBoundary assumption rows collector-side. One nuance: a default interface member declared INSIDE the interface does see the attribute (its ContainingType is the interface) - so the dead case is precisely "interface as scope for implementors," the natural reading of the advertised target. Users migrating a class-level boundary to the interface get identical syntax, identical validation behavior, and zero effect with no diagnostic. DISTINCTNESS vs #276: that occupies type/assembly-scoped trust reporting an ANALYZER-VS-COLLECTOR disagreement; here BOTH sides ignore Interface scopes while both sides validate them - removing the Interface target from AttributeUsage or wiring it into EnumerateScopes are fixes #276 does not describe. Distinct from #204 (effect attributes' Property target refused WITH explicit UnsupportedContract evidence): control attributes silently inert despite positive evidence of in-scope validation.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. In a strict/all project declare `public interface IPureGate { int Read(); }` with [SharpProofSuppress("reviewed precision gap")] on the interface, plus implementing class Gate with an impure Read.
2. Run the live analyzer: suppress-checking ORs scopes method->types->assembly only; the interface attribute is never consulted, so Read stays selected/analyzed exactly as if unannotated - contrast the identical attribute on class Gate, which suppresses reporting.
3. Compile with the collector enabled: CreateAssumptions yields zero TrustedBoundary rows for IPureGate-scoped trust; no SP0048 assumption evidence launcher-side.
4. Control proving the machinery considers the instance live: change the interface attribute reason to blank - SP0024 IS reported at the interface declaration, i.e., validated but never honored.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (EnumerateScopes confirmed the only scope source, containing no interface arm; verified at HEAD); Medium-High on defect classification.

### 350. Generated-Code Header Heuristic Requires Exact Whole-Token First-Comment Equality - Banner-Prefixed Auto-Generated Files Recognized as Generated by Compiler/IDE Convention Are Treated as Hand-Written, Leaking Selection Analysis and SP0024 Into Generator Output

**Location**: `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\AnalyzerGeneratedCodePolicy.cs` (Token table Lines 10-16: six exact strings like "<auto-generated/>"; fallback flow Lines 48-67 running whenever the SyntaxTreeOptionsProvider answer is Unknown; HasGeneratedHeader Lines 75-94 requiring the FIRST non-whitespace leading trivia to be a comment; IsExactGeneratedHeader Lines 96-109 demanding trimmed-body OrdinalIgnoreCase EQUALITY with one token); consumers SharpProofControlAttributePolicy.cs and every pipeline generated-code gate.
**Description**: The ecosystem convention (compiler warning suppression, IDE generated-code detection) recognizes generated files when the header comment begins-with/contains <auto-generated; real-world generators frequently emit a decorative separator banner BEFORE the marker block (the classic wsdl.exe/SvcUtil/xsd-era shape: a dashes banner line, then // <auto-generated> ...). For such trees the compiler treats them as generated, but SharpProof's first-trivia walk hits the banner comment, IsExactGeneratedHeader(banner) returns false, the path suffix doesn't match, so IsGenerated(tree) = false. Blast radius: the quietness policy INVERTS for these files - malformed [SharpProofSuppress]/[SharpProofTrusted] or contract attributes inside them get validated/reported, and any generator-emitted annotation becomes live selection input instead of being silenced. This composes with accepted #160's leak paths but has a different root cause: #160 is about which callbacks gate on IsGenerated; this is about the IsGenerated predicate itself misclassifying conventionally-generated trees. Distinct from #160/#161/#162 (registration timing, activation tiers, dedup keys): none concerns the header/path recognition heuristic whose strictness is the sole defect here.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Add Legacy.gened.cs - no matching path suffix - beginning with a dashes banner line, then // <auto-generated>, then // </auto-generated>, containing `[SharpProofSuppress("")] internal static void M() {}`.
2. Analyze under advisory/all: options provider returns Unknown, HasGeneratedPath false, HasGeneratedHeader false (first trivia is the banner) -> the tree is treated as hand-written.
3. Observe Error-severity SP0024 emitted from the file, although placing the identical content under an exact // <auto-generated> first comment silences it - proving the banner line, not the generated nature, decided the outcome.
4. Contrast: the C# compiler suppresses convention-based generated-file warnings for step 1's file, showing SharpProof diverges from platform behavior users rely on for "generated code is quiet".
**Confidence**: Medium (SharpProof-side strictness fully verified; the external-convention claim matches Roslyn/IDE documented behavior; impact requires third-party headers of the banner shape).

The entries below were produced by a second read-only hunter wave run later on the same day (20 parallel hunters across disjoint areas; 8 areas returned clean NO-FINDINGS audits whose ruled-out lists are not reproduced here). They are open findings against the current worktree at commit `6a62c315f`; the consolidation writer line-spot-checked the highest-risk citations before appending. Numbering continues the sequence above.

### 352. [RESOLVED 5b7156223] Per-Compilation InvocationEmissionPolicy Caches Preprocessor Decisions in a Plain Dictionary Mutated From Concurrent Analyzer Threads - Corruption Can Silently Elide Real [Conditional] Invocations From Effect Summaries

**Status**: Resolved by switching the compilation-scoped preprocessor-symbol cache to `ConcurrentDictionary.GetOrAdd`. A 64-tree concurrent conditional-invocation regression now completes without cache races and confirms the safe cache type.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Effects\InvocationEmissionPolicy.cs` (Field declaration Lines 11-12: `private readonly Dictionary<SyntaxTree, ImmutableHashSet<string>> _definedPreprocessorSymbols = [];`; unsynchronized read-modify-write Lines 42-51 `TryGetValue`+`Add`); shared ownership chain `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\AnalyzerSession.cs` (Lines 30, 101-111, 229: one `Lazy<EffectAnalysisSession>` per compilation handed to every analysis), `SharpProof.Effects\EffectAnalysisSession.cs` (Lines 26, 53, 246-249 session-owned instance), `SharpProof.Effects\OperationEffectScanner.cs` (Line ~596 invocation path); concurrency opt-in `SharpProof.Analyzer\SharpProofAnalyzer.cs` (Line 31 `context.EnableConcurrentExecution()`).
**Description**: `IsElided` caches per-tree effective preprocessor symbol sets in a plain `Dictionary<,>` guarded by nothing. The instance is compilation-scoped state shared by all analyzer actions (Roslyn runs registered actions concurrently once `EnableConcurrentExecution()` is set), so two concurrently analyzed methods each containing a `[Conditional]`-target invocation enter `IsElided` simultaneously; simultaneous writers/readers on a non-concurrent `Dictionary` can corrupt buckets, hang bucket walks, throw (`IndexOutOfRangeException`/`ArgumentException`), or serve a wrong cached set. Every sibling cache obeys the stated discipline - `AnalyzerSession` declares eleven `ConcurrentDictionary` fields (Lines 40-64) and `EffectAnalysisSession` guards `_nodes` with `_gate` (Lines 32, 77) - making this lone field an oversight rather than policy, and contradicting the class doc "compilation-scoped deterministic may-effect analysis" (EffectAnalysisSession.cs Lines 20-23). Worst corruption mode is semantic: a spurious `elided=true` makes the effects scanner drop an invocation the C# compiler actually emits (under-approximating may-effects toward unsound purity certifications); the common modes are nondeterministic mid-analysis exceptions/hangs instead of typed abstentions. Distinct from #302 (a lock held too long elsewhere - this is a missing lock), #4 (Lazy synchronization, rejected), #113/#304 (no swallowing involved).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Compile a project whose source defines several public methods that each call a `[Conditional("SHARPPROOF_CONTRACTS")]` static helper (symbol left undefined so `IsElided` takes the cache-miss path).
2. Run `SharpProofAnalyzer` (which calls `EnableConcurrentExecution()`) over a solution forcing parallel action execution across those methods (many files / IDE full-solution analysis).
3. Observe intermittent analyzer exceptions/hangs originating in `Dictionary'2.Add/TryGetValue` inside `InvocationEmissionPolicy.IsElided`, with reruns nondeterministic while single-threaded execution passes; worst case, divergent effect summaries between runs for identical input.
**Confidence**: High (unsynchronized mutable shared state and its concurrency reachability chain quoted verbatim; only the corruption's manifest form is nondeterministic).

### 353. [RESOLVED d1671dc34] A Duplicate Reference to the Trusted Attributes Assembly Silently Disables Every Contract and Effect Feature While Bypassing the SP0050 Surfacing Built for Exactly This Failure Class

**Status**: Resolved by preserving the selected trusted reference assembly when aliases are present and validating every duplicate reference payload. Identical aliased package references remain admitted; source-shadowed, mismatched, or unreadable copies remain rejected. `DuplicateAliasedPackageReferencesRemainAdmitted` and the source-shadow regression pass.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Frontend\ContractApiIdentityResolver.cs` (`HasTrustedAttributesPayload` Lines 188-202: `if (matches.Length != 1 || matches[0] is not PortableExecutableReference { FilePath: { Length: > 0 } path }) { return false; }`, counting reference ENTRIES not distinct assemblies; `UnreadableContractApiReason` assigned only in the `File.Open` failure arm Lines 204-208); sole consumer `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\SharpProofAnalyzerEngine.cs` (Lines 61-74, gated exclusively on `UnreadableContractApiReason`).
**Description**: When SharpProof.Attributes appears more than once among `Compilation.References` - legal compilation state when the duplicates carry distinct aliases - `HasTrustedAttributesPayload` returns false, the constructor sets `Contract = null` (Lines 40-47), and every `ResolveAttributeCore` resolution returns null: closed contracts (`NotNull`/`Positive`/`InRange`), clauses, and all effect attributes become completely inert. The failure is indistinguishable from "nothing to analyze": `UnreadableContractApiReason` stays null because it is set only when opening the file throws, so the SP0050 surfacing that exists precisely to prevent silent disablement (per its own description: a referenced-but-broken contract API must be surfaced, never silent) never fires, although the environment fault (duplicate reference) is detectably analogous to the unreadable case. `IsRejectedClauseMethod` still flags literal Requires/Ensures/Assume call sites, but attribute-only consumers get zero diagnostics - pure silent coverage loss. Distinct from #311 (evidence-authority accessibility split), #298 (companion supersession), #348 (interface-targeted control attributes inert): different site and mechanism.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Create a project referencing the shipped SharpProof.Attributes.dll normally AND adding a second `MetadataReference` to the identical PE file under a distinct alias, so `Compilation.References` contains two entries resolving to the assembly symbol.
2. Add attribute-only usage, e.g. `[SharpProof.Attributes.EnforcePure] static int Get() => 42;` with no `Contract.Requires/Ensures/Assume` anywhere.
3. Run the analyzer: `HasTrustedAttributesPayload` fails on `matches.Length == 2`, no SP0050 or any contract diagnostic is emitted, and EnforcePure selection silently does nothing; removing either reference restores full analysis.
**Confidence**: Medium (gate and silent-null path verified verbatim; trigger requires a duplicated/aliased reference layout, which is legal and reachable in test harnesses and alias-based facades but uncommon in default MSBuild graphs).

### 354. [RESOLVED 82614bc1e] Foreach Iteration and Catch Exception Locals Are Seeded With Permanent EffectRegionSet.Empty - Writes Through Reference-Typed Elements Erase Every Mutation They Perform

**Status**: Resolved by conservatively assigning runtime-bound foreach aliases from their collection and catch exception locals to `Unknown`. The regression covers both reference mutation paths and passes in the Effects suite.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Effects\ConversionOwnershipClassifier.cs` (Seeding Lines 121-127: every `IVariableDeclaratorOperation` inserted with `EffectRegionSet.Empty`; enrichment switch Lines 310-346 covering ONLY declarator initializers, simple assignments to locals/parameters, ref-like compound/increment targets, and ref-field rebinding - no foreach-control-variable or catch-variable source exists; `ClassifyLocal` Lines 576-589 returns the seeded set via `TryGetValue`, falling back to conservative `Unknown` only for UNSEEDED locals); consumer `SharpProof.Effects\OperationEffectScanner.cs` (Lines 322-326 `ScanField`: `region = _conversionOwnership.ClassifyRegion(field.Instance)` feeding `Write(region)`/`Read(region)`).
**Description**: A `foreach` iteration variable receives its value from the enumerator (`Current`) with no assignment operation anywhere in the IOperation tree, and a catch exception variable is runtime-bound likewise - both stay `Empty` forever because enrichment never fires for them. Without the seeding, `ClassifyLocal` would fail closed to `Unknown`; the seeding converts the safe default into the unsafe one. Downstream, a write `b.F = 42` through reference-typed iteration variable `b` computes `region = ClassifyRegion(local b)` = Empty and emits `Write(Empty)`, which projects to no write contract fact at all; the same erasure applies to receiver-writes via methods called on `b`. Runtime reality: every object reachable from the iterated collection is mutated, so `[EnforcePure]`/no-write contracts get certified for caller-visible mutation (worst class). Value-type loops mask the hole (copy semantics), which is why existing fixtures using `int` loops pass. Same root cause covers `catch (E ex) { ex.Field = 1; }`. Distinct from #295 (closure/display-class capture invisible - different symbol population), #155 (ref-local aliases to fields), #154 (primary-ctor receivers): the mechanism is the declarator seeding plus the missing enrichment arms for runtime-assigned locals.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Compile: `public sealed class Box { public int F; } public static void M(List<Box> list) { foreach (var b in list) { b.F = 42; } }`.
2. Analyze `M` via `new EffectAnalysisSession(compilation).Analyze(...)`.
3. Observe the summary records no write regions attributable to `b` (writes through the iteration variable vanish), i.e., certified write-free although runtime mutates every listed `Box`.
4. Control: replace the loop with `foreach (var b in list) { b = new Box(); }`-style direct assignment shapes or seed via explicit assignment (`var b = list[0];`) and the write IS recorded - isolating the missing runtime-assignment enrichment as the cause.
**Confidence**: High (seeding, absent enrichment arms, `ClassifyLocal` fallback polarity, and `ScanField` consumption all verified in source; existing tests use value-type loop variables, masking the defect).

**Status**: `82614bc1e` maps foreach declaration variables back to their collection ownership and marks catch exception declarations `Unknown`, with regression coverage for reference-typed loop and catch mutations. Catch-variable erasure is fixed; foreach summaries may still include an unrelated unknown enumerator boundary, so this item remains partial rather than claiming precision beyond the conservative guarantee.

### 355. [RESOLVED 4244178f1] ScanDeconstruction Emits a Bare MayDiverge When the Deconstruct Cannot Complete Normally - the Callee Is Never Resolved, Dropping Its Pre-Throw Writes and Allocations

**Status**: Resolved by resolving deconstruction callees before propagating their effects, with regression coverage for failed deconstruction and pre-throw mutations.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Effects\OperationEffectScanner.Expressions.cs` (Lines 57-72 `ScanDeconstruction`: `return value.Then(new EffectStep(completes ? EffectSummaryOperations.Unsupported() : EffectSummaryOperations.MayDiverge(), completes)).Summary;`); completion source `SharpProof.Effects\OperationCompletionEvaluator.cs` (Lines 768-835 `CanCompleteDeconstruction`/`DeconstructionPhasesMayComplete` return false for always-throwing `Deconstruct`); intended pattern `SharpProof.Effects\OperationEffectScanner.cs` (Lines 809-813 `ScanThrow` deliberately joins pre-throw construction effects because caught exceptions still let them persist).
**Description**: When the source `Deconstruct` cannot complete normally, the summary is just the RHS scan plus `MayDiverge()` - reads/writes/allocation all Empty/None, throws empty, completeness Complete - and the scanner never resolves the `Deconstruct` invocation through the call resolver in EITHER branch (no callee summary, no `sourceCalls` entry). At runtime a user `Deconstruct` may mutate observable state BEFORE throwing (`Global.State = 1; throw new InvalidOperationException();`), and a caller-side catch swallows the exception so the mutation persists; yet no write fact exists anywhere in the method summary, enabling false-pure certification. The codebase's own sibling treatment proves intent: `ScanThrow` joins exception-construction effects precisely so pre-divergence work survives a caught throw; the deconstruction arm lacks the equivalent join. Test gap: `DeconstructionFailure` passes only because its asserted static write lives in the CATCH BLOCK, not inside the throwing `Deconstruct`. Distinct from the completion-arm family (#211 ITryOperation fixed, #261 loop fixed, #294 switch-expression): those miscompute whether LATER operations run; this arm never resolves the callee's own effects at all.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Compile: `public sealed class P { public void Deconstruct(out int a, out int b) { Global.State = 42; throw new InvalidOperationException(); } }` and `[EnforcePure] public static int M(P p) { try { var (a, b) = p; } catch (InvalidOperationException) { } return Global.State; }`.
2. Analyze `M`: `MethodCanCompleteNormally(Deconstruct)` is false, so `ScanDeconstruction` emits `value.Then(MayDiverge, false)` without resolving the callee.
3. Observe the summary contains no `Writes` entry for `Global.State` (and no allocation facts), although `M` observably performs the write before the swallowed exception - certified pure while the write persists.
**Confidence**: High (mechanism traced through `ScanDeconstruction` -> `CanCompleteInvocation` -> `MethodCanCompleteNormally`; the MayDiverge-only summary verifiably omits the never-resolved `Deconstruct` call site).

### 356. [RESOLVED 5c7e70ec8] ScanCallStep Early-Returns on a Proven-Null Receiver Before Scanning Arguments - Argument Effects Vanish and the Reported Escaping Exception Contradicts the Reachability Layer's Own Ordering

**Status**: Resolved by scanning invocation arguments before applying the null-receiver dispatch boundary, preserving argument effects and exceptions.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Effects\OperationEffectScanner.cs` (Lines 654-664: receiver `EffectStep` built and `if (!result.CompletesNormally) { return result; }` executed BEFORE the argument loop at Lines 666-673); contradiction `SharpProof.Effects\ExceptionHandlerReachability.cs` (Lines 221-239: `prerequisitesComplete &= invocation.Arguments.All(...)` evaluated BEFORE adding the potential-null-receiver fact); correct setter-path precedent `ScanWriteTargetEvaluation` (argument/RHS scanned before the store; pinned by the `ThrowingRhsBeforeNullSetter` fixture).
**Description**: When abstract flow proves the receiver null, the receiver step does not complete and `ScanCallStep` returns before scanning ANY argument. But C# evaluates arguments before the dispatch-time null check: in `c.M(Fx())` with `c` definitely null, `Fx()` runs first - its allocations, reads/writes, and its own exception are what the program exhibits, not a bare NRE. The repo's own handler-reachability layer models exactly this order for the same operations (arguments first, null-receiver fact second), which is why `NullReceiverAfterThrowingArgument` judges the NRE catch unreachable - the two layers disagree on evaluation order. The effect summary, however, retains only `Throw(NRE)` with zero argument facts. For `[EnforcePure]`/`[ZeroAllocations]` members, any effectful argument to a call on a provably-null receiver is certified allocation/write-free, and the reported escaping exception type is wrong whenever the argument throws first. Distinct from #288 (event accessor retention, fixed), #292 (expression-position mutations), and all listed completion arms: specifically the argument-scan suppression on the invocation path.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Compile: `class C { public object Touch(object o) => o; }` plus `[ZeroAllocations] public static void M(C c) { c.Touch(new object()); }` with flow arranging `c` provably null at the call.
2. Analyze `M` with `new EffectAnalysisSession(compilation).Analyze(...)`.
3. Observe `Summary.Allocation == None` and `Throws == {NullReferenceException}` - no managed allocation for the argument even though it constructs (and if the argument threw `InvalidOperationException`, THAT would escape at runtime, not NRE).
4. Contrast the setter path: the same shape through a property setter counts the RHS exception (pinned fixture), proving the invocation arm is the odd one out.
**Confidence**: High (direct code path quoted; internal inconsistency with `ExceptionHandlerReachability` ordering and tested setter-path behavior decisive).

### 357. [RESOLVED 0a8089fc8] MethodCanCompleteNormally Conjoins Statement Facts Sequentially, so a Conditional Early Return Followed by a Never-Completing Tail Reports "Never Returns" and Deletes All Post-Call Effects Into False-Pure Verdicts

**Status**: Resolved by deriving completion from CFG exits rather than conjunction over sequential statement facts, retaining effects on paths with a normal exit.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Effects\ManagedAbstractFlow.cs` (`DefiniteOperationFacts.MethodCanCompleteNormally` Lines 1937-1971; `SequenceMayCompleteNormally` Lines 2319-2330 - pure CONJUNCTION `if (!MayCompleteNormals(operation)) return false;` over statements; return/conditional cases Lines 1987-1994; infinite-loop rule Lines 2070-2073; documented permissive contract Lines 1931-1935 and 1973-1978); consumers `SharpProof.Effects\OperationCompletionEvaluator.cs` (Lines 576-598, esp. 595-597), `SharpProof.Effects\OperationEffectScanner.cs` (Lines 689-692, 983-995 sequence break), `SharpProof.Effects\EffectMethodNodeBuilder.cs` (Lines 341-351 CFG successor skip), verdict projection `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\EffectContractDiagnostics.cs` (Lines 150-191, 236-254: facet checks ignore Termination).
**Description**: `SequenceMayCompleteNormally` ANDs per-statement completion facts instead of reasoning about paths: statement 1 of `if (bail) return;` completes normally (control may skip the branch), but statement 2 `while (true) { }` does not, so the conjunction reports the METHOD never completes normally - although the `bail==true` path returns normally, exactly the "reachable normal exit" fact the XML doc promises and the Lines 1973-1978 contract demands stay permissive. Consumers treat the CALL as never-returning: `ScanCallStep` marks the step non-completing, `EffectStep.Then` drops subsequent siblings' summaries, `ScanSequence` breaks, and the CFG builder never schedules successor blocks - so static writes, capabilities, and contract-relevant operations after the call are certified away. Because facet checks ignore termination, the polluted summary (empty writes/throws) still ESTABLISHES no-write/DoesNotThrow contracts as Proven while the suppressed code really executes. Secondary triggers: tail `throw` (verdict stays non-Proven but witnesses/evidence lost) and constructor member initializers (EffectMethodNodeBuilder Lines 51-57 skip the entire body when initializer completion is wrongly false). Distinct from #211/#261/#294 (operation-kind predicates: try/loop/switch-expression arms) - this is the SEQUENCE COMPOSITION rule feeding the same consumer chains; coexistence follows the ledger's own #261 precedent.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Write: `static int _state; static void Guard(bool bail) { if (bail) return; while (true) { } }` and an annotated caller `static void User(bool flag) { Guard(flag); _state = 1; }` under a no-write/DoesNotThrow effect contract.
2. Analyze `User`: `MethodCanCompleteNormally(Guard)` = conjunction(true, false) = false -> the call step does not complete -> `_state = 1` skipped and its CFG block unscheduled.
3. Observe User's summary carries only the divergence marker with EMPTY write/throw facets -> contract established -> outcome Proven.
4. Execute `User(bail: true)`: `Guard` returns immediately and `_state = 1` runs - a real state write occurred under a Proven certification.
**Confidence**: High (wrong-false computation, all four suppression points, and the termination-blind verdict projection read and quoted at HEAD; only end-to-end execution was not run).

### 358. [RESOLVED 0a8089fc8] The Mutual-Recursion Cycle Guard Returns Definite "Never Completes Normally" for Terminating Mutual Recursion, Suppressing Caller Effects Through the Same Consumer Chain as the Completion-Fact Family

**Status**: Resolved with the CFG-exit completion calculation and mutual-recursion regression coverage; cyclic calls now remain conservative without erasing caller effects on normal exits.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Effects\ManagedAbstractFlow.cs` (Cycle guard Lines 1946-1948: `if (!_activeMethods.Add(normalized)) { return false; }` inside `MethodCanCompleteNormally`; comparer note Lines 1834-1836; consumer `InvocationMayCompleteNormally` Lines 2295-2297); same consumers as the sequence-conjunction entry (OperationCompletionEvaluator.cs 595-597; OperationEffectScanner.cs 689-699, 983-995; EffectMethodNodeBuilder.cs 341-351); contrast benign guard use in `ExceptionHandlerReachability.GetCallableExceptions` (Lines 2555-2558); recursion-summary pollution limited to callee summaries (`EffectAnalysisSession.cs` Lines 376-383).
**Description**: On re-entry during cycle detection the completion fact returns DEFINITE false. For terminating mutual recursion whose only fall-off-end path traverses the cycle - `A(int i) { if (i > 100) return; B(i + 1); }` and `B(int i) => A(i);` - evaluating B enters A, A's statement invokes B, re-entry fires, and false propagates to BOTH, although `B(200)` demonstrably returns normally (contradicting the Lines 1931-1935 doc). The sibling walker's identical guard is benign there because throw-kind collection is a monotone union over each cycle member's single full walk; the completion fact computed here is order-sensitive, so the same guard is unsound. Callers treat the callee as never-returning and delete all subsequent steps (sequence break, successor-block skip), yielding empty write/throw facets that establish purity/DoesNotThrow contracts as Proven while post-call code executes. The `FindRecursiveMethods` machinery only joins `UnknownBoundary(DirectCall|Recursion)` into the CALLEES' summaries - it does not repair the caller-local scan suppression. Distinct from the sequence-conjunction arm (different faulty line and trigger shape) and from all operation-kind completion arms.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Write: `static int _state; static void A(int i) { if (i > 100) return; B(i + 1); } static void B(int i) => A(i);` plus annotated `static void User() { B(200); _state = 1; }` under a no-write contract.
2. Analyze `User`: active={B} -> A -> active={A,B} -> A's `B(i+1)` re-enters -> guard returns false -> B=false -> call step marked non-completing -> `_state = 1` dropped.
3. Observe empty facets establish the contract (Proven); execute `User()`: `A(200)` returns immediately and the write runs.
**Confidence**: Medium-High (mechanism and every cited line verified at HEAD; residual uncertainty only whether some downstream stage re-checks recursive callees in a way that would taint User's completeness - the DirectCall|Recursion boundary lands on A/B summaries, not User's local scan, and facet checks ignore uncertainty markers).

### 359. [RESOLVED c381f9bf6] ResolveStaticFieldTypeInitialization Inverts "Initializer Provably Never Completes" Into EffectSummary.Empty - Definitely-Throwing Static Initializers Certify [DoesNotThrow] Claims Proven While Every Execution Throws TypeInitializationException

**Status**: Resolved by keeping throwing static initialization conservative instead of converting a definite failure into an empty effect summary.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Effects\EffectAnalysisSession.cs` (Branch Lines 292-296: `if (!mayInitialize || isSourceType && StaticInitializationCannotComplete(normalizedTarget)) { return EffectSummary.Empty; }`; helper `StaticInitializationCannotComplete` Lines 301-339 iterating initializers through `DefiniteOperationFacts.MayCompleteNormally` and `type.StaticConstructors.Any(... !facts.MethodCanCompleteNormally(constructor))`; healthy-path contrast Lines 297-298 returning `UnknownBoundary(UnmodeledCall)`); sole consumer `SharpProof.Effects\OperationEffectScanner.cs` (Lines 333-335 joining the result into static field reads); `[DoesNotThrow]` establishment `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\EffectContractDiagnostics.cs` (Lines 170-178, 236: established iff `summary.Throws.IsEmpty`); contradicting spec `SEMANTICS.md` (L132-137 escaping-exception containment; L236-239 implicit exceptions make `[DoesNotThrow]` unknown; L253-256 unmodeled static-initializer boundaries must be Unknown); repo fixtures proving the fact machinery recognizes these bombs: `StaticBomb`/`BeforeFieldInitBomb` (EffectAnalysisTests.cs Lines 5166-5183).
**Description**: When static initialization will provably run at the access site AND definitely throw, the correct responses are a throw boundary (or at minimum the `UnknownBoundary` every other branch returns). Instead the branch returns `EffectSummary.Empty` - Reads/Writes empty, Throws empty, Terminates, Complete - so a selected `[DoesNotThrow]` claim evaluates `established = summary.Throws.IsEmpty = true` and reaches Proven although every execution throws `TypeInitializationException` from the access site. The polarity inversion is visible in-place: the SAME function returns `UnknownBoundary` when initialization MAY complete, and `Empty` (strictly stronger silence) when it definitely cannot - failing closed exactly backwards. The sibling implementation of the identical fact, `OperationCompletionEvaluator.StaticInitializationMayComplete` (Lines 919-967), uses it conservatively, and no `TypeInitializationException` producer exists anywhere in summary construction (grep: only FrameworkTypeMetadataNames and catch-reachability mention it). Trigger needs no exotic shape: an explicit `static Bomb() => throw new ApplicationException();` cctor, or a beforefieldinit readonly field initialized by an always-throwing in-source method. Distinct from #294 variant B: there the FACT is miscomputed via `MayCompleteSwitchExpression`'s definite-false (guarded-total-switch trigger); here facts compute CORRECTLY for ordinary throwing cctors and the RESPONSE mapping itself is inverted - fixing #294 leaves this fully live.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Create: `internal static class Bomb { static Bomb() => throw new ApplicationException(); public static readonly int Value = 42; }` and `[SharpProof.Attributes.DoesNotThrow] public static int Get() => Bomb.Value;`.
2. Run `new EffectAnalysisSession(compilation).Analyze(Api.Get)`: expected per SEMANTICS L253-256 an Unknown boundary; actual summary is `{Reads: Static(), Throws: empty, Termination: Terminates}` because `StaticInitializationCannotComplete(Bomb)` fires (cctor body is `IThrowOperation`) and Lines 292-296 return Empty.
3. Observe the `[DoesNotThrow]` claim evaluate to Proven (Throws.IsEmpty); execute `Get()`: TypeInitializationException escapes on every call.
4. Variant without explicit cctor: `static readonly int Value = Make(); static int Make() => throw new ApplicationException();` - `MayCompleteNormally` recurses into `Make` and yields the same Empty outcome.
**Confidence**: High (branch text, fact helpers, single consumer, absent exception producer, establishment predicate, and contradicting normative doc all verified at HEAD; CLR static-initialization semantics standard).

### 360. [RESOLVED 7e71f62a7] Rank-1 Array Equality Lowers to an Abstention-Free Exact IR Equality That the Interpreter Resolves by IrValue Wrapper Identity, so Aliased Sequence Inputs Get Concrete Verdicts That Depend on Host Wrapper Sharing Rather Than C# Semantics

**Status**: Resolved by excluding array types from the frontend's exact built-in equality catalog and making direct IR sequence equality abstain instead of using `IrValue` wrapper identity. Added frontend and interpreter regressions; ordinary reference equality remains supported.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Ir\IrInterpreter.cs` (Line 354: `(IrValueKind.Sequence, IrValueKind.Sequence) => ReferenceEquals(left, right)` - comparing the WRAPPER objects; decisive contrast Lines 352-353: the Reference arm descends to payload identity `ReferenceEquals(left.Reference, right.Reference)`); wrapper model `SharpProof.Ir\IrModel.generated.cs` (Lines 107-129 sealed heap class); non-interning production `SharpProof.Ir\IrFactory.cs` (Lines 292-312 `CreateSequenceValue` news a fresh wrapper per call - contrast interned Boolean/Integer/String Lines 314-349); frontend admission `SharpProof.Frontend\CSharpScalarSemantics.generated.cs` (Lines 248-252 `SupportsBuiltInEquality` admits every non-delegate reference type incl. array types; Lines 102-103 map Equals/NotEquals to IR Equal/NotEqual) with emission `SharpProof.Frontend\RoslynOperationLowerer.cs` (Lines 656-774, no abstention for array operands); replay hosts binding per-variable fresh wrappers `SharpProof.Worker\CallableCounterexampleReplayer.cs` (Lines 30-47) and fuzz materializers `Tools\SharpProof.Fuzz\FrontendFuzzing.cs` (Lines 1312, 1483-1499), `SharpProof.Testing\WellSortedIrGenerator.cs` (Line 102).
**Description**: `[EnforcePure] static bool Same(int[] a, int[] b) => a == b;` passes the language gate (arrays admitted) and lowers to an EXACT `Binary(Equal, varA, varB)` term with zero abstentions - the frontend promises C# reference-equality semantics. At execution the Sequence arm compares the identities of two `IrValue` wrapper OBJECTS, not the modeled arrays; because `CreateSequenceValue` allocates an uninterned wrapper per call, the answer is decided by how a host shares wrappers across bindings, not program semantics. Every host that decodes bindings per-variable - the wire-model decode feeding worker counterexample replay and evidence-authority replay, plus all fuzz/differential harnesses - gives aliased parameters (`Same(x, x)`) distinct wrappers, evaluates FALSE where C# mandates TRUE, flipping provability/refutation for clauses containing array equality (worst class: wrong-verdict capability). Mitigating scope noted honestly: the shipped response-evidence authority currently fail-closes before sequences (materializes only bool/int values), narrowing today's surface, but cached-refutation replay and the public differential oracles remain exposed, and the interpreter API is public embedding surface. Distinct from #343 (generator null-pinning coverage), #158/#292/#293 (flow-capture/mutation/catalog axes): nobody touches `EvaluateEquality` or sequence wrapper identity.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Build a minimal IR program whose body returns `Binary(Equal, Variable(a), Variable(b))` for two distinct sequence-typed vars; execute via `IrProgramInterpreter.Execute` binding BOTH keys with separate `factory.CreateSequenceValue(type, ...)` results (fresh wrappers): `ReturnValue.Boolean == false`.
2. Re-run passing the SAME `IrValue` instance for both keys: result flips to true - the verdict is a function of host wrapper-sharing, not the program.
3. Compile-and-run the corresponding C# `Same(arr, arr)` (returns true) to show the divergence for the aliased input the lowering claimed to honor exactly.
4. Confirm no abstention: `RoslynOperationLowerer` on `a == b` with `int[]` params reports `FrontendSubsetDecision.Exact` with zero unsupported markers.
**Confidence**: High (every hop - gate admission, operator mapping, emission, wrapper allocation, interpreter arm - read directly; the Reference-vs-Sequence arm asymmetry on adjacent lines is dispositive).

### 361. [RESOLVED TBD] Vacuity-Derived Proven Postconditions Gamble Their Proof Core on Backend Unsat-Core Composition While the Evidence Authority Hard-Mandates a Literal body:normal-completion Citation - Mismatch Discards Fully Completed Runs as Failed/MalformedResult

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker\CallableVerifier.cs` (Probe path Lines 139-182: vacuity decided by satisfiability probing with `normalCompletionProofCore = CallableProofCore.Create(proven, assumptionLabels)` taken VERBATIM from the backend outcome at Lines 166-168; literal-false path hardcodes the label at Lines 140-143; merge into every Proven ensure row Lines 251-274); label producer `SharpProof.Worker\PostconditionObligationBuilder.cs` (Lines 87-98: `AddNormalCompletionAssumption` skips registering the labeled assumption entirely when its predicate duplicates an earlier non-user assumption); enforcing consumer `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact\CompilerResponseEvidenceAuthority.cs` (Lines 309-314: rejects any Proven+NoModeledNormalReturn postcondition whose ProofCore lacks the literal `"body:normal-completion"`); destructive fallback `SharpProof.Worker\SharpProofWorker.cs` (Lines 353-366: self-validation failure replaces the ENTIRE response with Failed/MalformedResult, all claims Unknown/InfrastructureFailure, CacheStatus=Rejected).
**Description**: The literal-false completion path synthesizes the mandated label, proving the authors knew the citation must exist; the PROBE path instead bets that the backend's unsat core happens to blame the completion assumption. Nothing obliges that: when body evidence is jointly unsatisfiable for reasons among spec/summary/domain assumptions (entry feasibility stays Feasible - it checks only requires+entry domains), the minimal core omits the completion disjunction; and the dedup suppression makes the mandated label STRUCTURALLY unreachable whenever the completion predicate duplicates an earlier assumption. On mismatch the worker's own validation fails (`response.vacuity_authority`) and the fallback destroys the run wholesale - including already-Refuted rows - converting a solvable callable into a false run-wide infrastructure verdict. Fail direction is fail-closed (no wrong Proven escapes), but the producer/consumer contract mismatch turns evidence-shape luck into total verdict loss. Distinct from #301/#226 (cache stamping), #304/#266 (replayer catch arms), #306 (launcher rewrite), #323 (summary-evidence ordering): this is inside the vacuity evidence path itself.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Build a sealed compiler-manifest artifact (programmatic builders from `SharpProof.Worker.Test\CompilerManifestArtifactTests.cs`) for one callable with requires `p > 0`, a body whose summary relations are mutually contradictory on shared inputs (Decode validates structure/seals, not semantic consistency), and one Ensures `result == c`.
2. Run `SharpProofWorker.VerifyAsync` with a real `IrSmtBackend`: entry feasibility Feasible; the completion probe returns Proven whose unsat core cites only the contradictory summary/spec labels; all ensure queries UNSAT -> rows Proven + NoModeledNormalReturn.
3. Observe `record.ProofCore` lacks `"body:normal-completion"`; validation reports `response.vacuity_authority`; VerifyAsync returns Failed/MalformedResult with every claim Unknown/InfrastructureFailure instead of Complete with vacuous-Proven rows.
4. Deterministic variant: make one body assumption term-identical to the completion disjunction so the labeled assumption is suppressed before probing - the mandated label then cannot exist in any core.
**Confidence**: Medium (producer/consumer contradiction and unconditional fallback verified verbatim; reachability requires a body-evidence contradiction independent of completion - a shape this repo's own threat model treats as in-scope for hostile/tampered artifacts).
**Status**: The completion probe now requires its backend core to cite `body:normal-completion`; a core that omits the dedicated evidence label is mapped to `MalformedBackendResult` before any vacuity-marked claim is emitted. Regression coverage exercises a duplicated completion predicate and preserves the literal and reachable completion cases.

### 364. Private I/O Paths Accept Pre-Existing Non-Regular Files - --result /dev/null Unlinks the Device Node and Renames a Regular File Over It While the Run Reports Success

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher\Program.cs` (Staging + `DeleteIfExists` Lines 92-96; generic delete helper Lines 926-932 using `File.Exists`/`File.Delete` with no file-type check; `ValidateDistinctPaths` Lines 1045-1108 applying `RequireLocalPath` ONLY to publication paths at Lines 1059-1062); canonicalization type-checks ANCESTORS only `SharpProof.Host\LinuxPathIdentity.cs` (Lines 96-118 `Canonicalize`: final component unchecked); blind rename-over-inode `SharpProof.Ir\AtomicFile.cs` (Lines 144-187 `Prepare`/`Publish` via `File.Replace`/`File.Move`); worker writes through the same rename `SharpProof.Worker\Program.cs` (Lines 283-286); deliberate contrast on the publication side `LinuxPathIdentity.cs` (Lines 1215-1233 `EnsureRegularPublicationPath`, Lines 684-714 `DeleteIfUnprotected` demanding regular files).
**Description**: `--request/--result/--compiler-manifest/--cache-directory` receive no regular-file/type validation anywhere: `Canonicalize` validates only that ancestors are directories, distinctness checks compare path strings/stat identity, and nothing refuses character devices/FIFOs. Execution then deletes the existing node (`File.Exists` is true for `/dev/null`) and the worker's staged-write rename recreates the path as a REGULAR FILE containing the result JSON - the container's `/dev/null` is silently destroyed while the launcher exits 0 with a genuine green verdict; every subsequent process writing to /dev/null fills a growing regular file (disk exhaustion, bizarre downstream failures). Unprivileged variant: any user-owned FIFO/block device passed as `--result` is clobbered identically. The codebase's own publication layer explicitly demands regular outputs ("publication members must be regular files"), so private-path acceptance is a gap, not a choice. Distinct from #308 (relative cache-directory anchors), #316/#290 (temp sweeping/test globs): none covers inode-type validation of the private path pipeline.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. In the canonical container, run a verification with the result redirected at the device: `dotnet build -p:SharpProofVerify=true -p:SharpProofVerifyResultFile=/dev/null`.
2. Build completes with exit 0 (the launcher reads back its own JSON from the path).
3. `stat -c '%F %s' /dev/null` -> `regular file` with size > 0 (was `character special file`, size 0); `echo x >/dev/null` now appends to disk.
4. Unprivileged cross-check: `mkfifo /tmp/f` and pass it via the same property - exit 0 and `/tmp/f` is now a regular file.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (no type gate exists anywhere on the pipeline; POSIX unlink/rename semantics standard); impact-weighted Medium overall because triggering requires an operator-supplied pathological path.

### 366. Canonicalize Validates a Reconstructed Single-Slash Walk but Returns Path.GetFullPath Verbatim - the POSIX Leading-'//' Spelling Produces Two Identities for One File, Splitting Publication-Marker Ownership

**Location**: `SharpProof.Host\LinuxPathIdentity.cs` (`Canonicalize` Lines 84-135: rejects dot segments, walks reconstructed `"/"+segments` prefixes with lstat symlink rejection, but `return fullPath;` returns `Path.GetFullPath(path)` UNMODIFIED - and .NET on Linux preserves exactly two leading slashes per the POSIX implementation-defined rule); lexical consumers: `AcquirePublicationLocks` (Lines 587-595), `ValidatePublicationTopology`/`IsStrictPathAncestor` (Lines 760-795, `StringComparison.Ordinal`), `PublicationMetadataPath` (Lines 805-821, SHA-256 over the UTF-8 canonical STRING), `BindPublicationSet` adoption keyed on that hash (Lines 867-896), `IsSameOrDescendant`/stat fallback only for already-existing files (Lines 660-675, 1308-1318); entry of user-supplied spellings `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher\Program.cs` (Lines 1139-1149 `FullPath`/`OptionalFullPath`).
**Description**: `"/w/out"` and `"//w/out"` denote the same kernel object, pass validation identically (the walk checks single-slash prefixes), yet escape as two DIFFERENT identity strings. Every sameness mechanism downstream is lexical on that string, so: (a) within one invocation, a publication set spelled with mixed slash counts passes distinctness/topology gates (nonexistent destinations have no stat identity to fall back on) and `BindPublicationSet` commits TWO live ownership markers over one destination; (b) across invocations, respelling an unchanged output makes `InvalidatePreviousPublication` find no marker under the new hash, skip invalidation, and then abort with "refuses to adopt a pre-existing publication destination without an exact ownership marker" (exit 3) on a tree SharpProof itself published seconds earlier - stranding permanent orphaned `.set` markers and flip-flopping between spellings. Availability/identity-integrity defect (fail-closed direction, not soundness-breaking). Distinct from #272 (validation-vs-use TOCTOU - no concurrency needed here), #316/#242 (crash-orphaned/torn markers - these are fully written markers duplicated by ordinary operation), #243/#313 (unrelated gates).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Verify the primitive: print `Path.GetFullPath("//tmp/x/result.json")` on Linux - the leading `//` survives while `Canonicalize` accepts the input.
2. Publish normally once with `--publish-result /tmp/pub-demo/result.json`; note the marker under `/tmp/pub-demo/.sharpproof-publication/<hex>.set`.
3. Rerun the identical command spelled `--publish-result //tmp/pub-demo/result.json`: no marker matches the new hash; the launch fails with the adoption-refusal error despite the file being SharpProof's own prior output; cleanup now requires manually deleting the output and the stranded marker.
4. Single-run variant: supply `--publish-request /tmp/pub-demo/req.json --publish-result //tmp/pub-demo/req.json`-style overlapping spellings; distinctness/topology pass and two `<hash>.set` markers claim one destination.
**Confidence**: Medium (walk-vs-return asymmetry and all lexical consumers verified; the one external premise - .NET preserving exactly two leading slashes on Unix - is documented POSIX behavior and self-verifiable per step 1).

**Location**: `SharpProof.Verifier\buildTransitive\SharpProof.Verifier.targets` (Generic verifier-failure error Lines 285-286 `<Error Text="SharpProof verifier failed with exit code $(_SharpProofVerifierExitCode)." />` with NO `Code=`; runtime-closure error Lines 37-38); `SharpProof.BuildTasks\RunVerifier.cs` (output-limit `Log.LogError` Lines 362-365); `SharpProof.BuildTasks\InvalidatePublishedResult.cs` (all six conflict-rule `Log.LogError` calls Lines 206-230); `SharpProof.BuildTasks\ValidatePublishedVerificationResult.cs` (catch-all Lines 133-141); `SharpProof.BuildTasks\ResetPublishedVerification.cs` (Line 60 `LogErrorFromException`). ### 368. [RESOLVED c9ac48b1c] Cache-Alias Screening Omits Compiler-Owned Outputs Entirely - SharpProofVerifyCacheDirectory May Point At bin/<cfg>/<tfm>, Passing Pre-Flight While Every Sibling Configuration Is Rejected, and dotnet clean Never Removes the Entries

**Status**: Resolved by rejecting cache directories that overlap compiler-owned output paths, with build-task regression coverage for the alias.

**Location**: `SharpProof.BuildTasks\InvalidatePublishedResult.cs` (`aliasesCache` predicate Lines 183-194 screening against `publicationPaths ∪ publicationMarkerPaths ∪ inputPaths` ∪ worker tree ONLY; `aliasesCompilerOutput` Lines 195-204 symmetrically never consulting the cache path; `compilerOutputPaths` computed at Line 118 but unused by `aliasesCache`); stated invariant Line 224 ("SharpProof output, input, cache, and worker paths must be distinct."); forwarding `SharpProof.Verifier\buildTransitive\SharpProof.Verifier.targets` (Lines 265-266 `--cache-directory` verbatim).
**Description**: The distinctness rule family is enforced for cache-vs-publication/input/worker but silently NOT for cache-vs-compiler-owned territory - the configuration users are most likely to reach for (`$(TargetDir)`, `$(IntermediateOutputPath)`). Pre-flight accepts it, the worker materializes hashed cache entries directly inside bin/obj, and `dotnet clean` never removes them (Clean deletes tracked FileWrites/known outputs only; the reset task handles publications, not cache). Result: durable pollution of compiler-output trees that glob-based packaging/zip tooling can ingest, plus asymmetric UX - pointing the cache AT result.json is rejected with the targeted error while pointing it at the DIRECTORY CONTAINING result.json is accepted. Distinct from #319 (early invalidation pass running before the cache property exists - orthogonal: even the fully-initialized pass lacks the compiler-output arm) and #220 (staging accounting): no entry covers the predicate contents.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Verified consumer project: `dotnet build /p:SharpProofVerify=true "/p:SharpProofVerifyCacheDirectory=$(pwd)/bin/Debug/net8.0"`.
2. Build succeeds; `ls bin/Debug/net8.0` now contains cache-entry blobs alongside App.dll (contrast: the same option aimed at the result.json FILE fails pre-flight with the distinctness error).
3. `dotnet clean /p:SharpProofVerify=true` -> blobs remain; repeated builds accumulate them.
**Confidence**: High on the screening omission (predicate code exhaustively quoted; no other site intersects cache with compiler outputs); framed at pollution/asymmetry tier pending maintainer intent.

### 369. [PARTIALLY RESOLVED fa58c7533] Explicit Interface Implementations and Static Constructors Are Analyzed In-Process but Marked Unsupported in the Manifest - Strict Builds Kill Analyzer-Blessed Members While Advisory Builds Silently Lose All Coverage for Them

**Location**: `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\ClaimManifestBuilder.cs` (Lines 93-106: `supported` requires `target.MethodKind is (Ordinary or Constructor or PropertyGet or PropertySet or EventAdd or EventRemove)`); admitting counterparts `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\LanguageSubsetGate.cs` (Lines 140-148 `SupportsCallable` whitelists `StaticConstructor` and `ExplicitInterfaceImplementation`) and `SharpProof.Contracts\ContractBinder.cs` (Lines 74-82 same extended set); enforcement `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\CompilerCallableLowerer.cs` (Lines 50-56 `!target.IsVerifierSupported -> Fail(target, WorkerClaimReason.UnsupportedCallable)`); strict consequence `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher\Program.cs` (Lines 480-493, 519 incomplete callables -> exit 6 under require-proven).
**Description**: Three layers disagree on one axis: the analyzer deliberately supports EII and static constructors (someone whitelisted both in `SupportsCallable` and `BindCore`), so an explicit interface implementation carrying `[EnforcePure]` is selected, classified Supported, analyzed clean and silent - yet the collector emits `supported=false`, preparation fails `UnsupportedCallable`, every claim becomes Unknown, and callable coverage is Incomplete. Under `require-proven` the launcher exits 6 with the aggregate SP0047 "selected analysis is incomplete" - killing builds the in-process analyzer blessed with zero diagnostics; under advisory, all verification coverage for those members silently disappears. No false Proven (fail-closed), but a guaranteed analyzer-vs-verifier divergence with an opaque strict-mode presentation. Distinct from #341 (DOCUMENTATION enumerating fewer kinds than the gate - this is the collector's whitelist drifting from two code layers that agree), #183 (string-op classification flag), #298/#348 (trusted-attribute axes).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Strict-profile project (`SharpProofProfile=strict` => verify policy require-proven) referencing SharpProof.Attributes; add `public interface IFoo { int Compute(int x); }` and `public sealed class Foo : IFoo { [SharpProof.EnforcePure] int IFoo.Compute(int x) => x * 2; }`.
2. Build in-container: analyzer emits nothing (body supported, purity proven); collector marks the callable unsupported; worker returns Unknown/Incomplete for the claim; require-proven fails the build (exit 6/SP0047) with no analyzer-side counterpart diagnostic.
3. Switch to advisory profile: same member silently loses all worker coverage - no diagnostic either way, isolating the MethodKind whitelist as the discriminator.
**Confidence**: High for explicit interface implementations; static-constructor admission remains intentionally fail-closed because constructor initialization and replay semantics need a separate design. Commit `fa58c7533` aligns the collector for explicit interface implementations and adds manifest coverage; it does not broaden static-constructor support.

### 370. The Collector-Only HasStaticStateMutation Gate Voids Effect Evidence for Every Selected Member of a Type Whose Static Constructor Contains Any Assignment - Analyzer Proves Pure, Verifier Fails the Strict Build Opaquely

**Location**: `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\ClaimManifestBuilder.cs` (Conjunct at Line 105: `!(analyzerEffectsSelected && HasStaticStateMutation(target))` inside `supported`; predicate Lines 186-207 scanning ALL statements of explicitly declared static constructors for any assignment/increment; unavailable-routing Lines 463-472 `MarkUnavailable(evidence, WorkerClaimReason.UnsupportedContract)`); no analyzer counterpart `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\AnalyzerFeaturePipeline.cs` (Lines 192-360; the only `StaticConstructors` uses in Analyzer.Core are member-initializer reachability at scattered lines).
**Description**: When any effect attribute selects a method, the collector additionally demands that the containing type's declared static constructors contain NO assignment whatsoever - any field, any statement. If one exists (`Threshold = 0;`), `supported=false` routes EVERY effect evaluation of EVERY member of that type through unavailable evidence (Outcome Unknown, Certainty Unavailable, witness/replay wiped), while the analyzer pipeline applies no such gate and records Proven normally. Cross-check-triangle divergence: under require-proven the launcher's incomplete check fails the build with the generic aggregate message naming neither the static constructor nor the reason - an opaque kill for code the analyzer certified; under advisory, effect verification silently vanishes for those callables. The gate is over-broad even as a soundness measure (a benign cctor write poisons unrelated statics forever). Fail direction safe (never false Proven). 2. Build in-container: expected per analyzer - `Square` selected, Supported, purity Proven, silent; actual - `HasStaticStateMutation` sees the cctor write, `supported=false`, the EnforcePure claim becomes Unknown(UnsupportedContract), coverage Incomplete, require-proven exits 6/SP0047 with no explanation referencing the cctor.

### 371. [SharpProofSuppress] Silences the In-Process Analyzer but Is Never Consulted by the Collector - Suppression Meaning Flips When Moving Advisory->Strict With Zero Surfacing

**Location**: Analyzer side `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\SharpProofControlAttributePolicy.cs` (Lines 5-17, 158-188 `ValidateAndShouldSuppress` returning suppress for scoped well-formed attributes) and `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\AnalyzerFeaturePipeline.cs` (Lines 135, 154, 281: `selection.IsSuppressed` -> record and SKIP all analysis); collector side `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\ClaimManifestBuilder.cs` (resolves `ContractSelectionInventory.Suppress` at Line 35 but mirrors ONLY `Trusted` into assumptions at Lines 474-487; zero other references to suppression - grep finds only the unrelated `ReportDiagnostic.Suppress` enum passthrough at `CompilerWireMappings.generated.cs` Line 84); documented intent `README.md` Line 274 / `docs/public-api.md` Line 76 ("changes reporting only").
**Description**: A well-formed `[SharpProofSuppress("reason")]` on method/property/type/assembly makes the analyzer record Suppressed and skip - no analysis, no diagnostics. The collector never consults the suppress bit: suppressed callables still receive postcondition claims, assumption rows, and fully evaluated/sealed effect evidence, and the worker verifies them. In advisory mode the documented "reporting only" contract holds; under `require-proven` the SAME annotation's effective meaning silently flips - a suppressed callable whose claims cannot be proven yields Unknown/Incomplete, launcher exit 5/6, and a hard build failure with no diagnostic acknowledging the suppression that previously silenced the identical fact. Users relying on suppressions during advisory triage hit this deterministically on profile flip, and nothing at the collector boundary explains it. Fail-open is avoided (verification is NOT skipped), so the damage is a profile-dependent semantics change without surfacing. Distinct from #348 (INTERFACE-targeted control attributes validated but inert everywhere - different axis: here class/method scoping WORKS analyzer-side and diverges collector-side).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Advisory-profile project: `internal static class Gate { [SharpProof.EnforcePure] [SharpProof.Suppress("triage: precision gap")] internal static int Impure(int x) { Console.WriteLine(""); return x; } }` - analyzer silent, build green.
2. Flip only `SharpProofProfile=strict`; rebuild: analyzer still silent, but the manifest now carries the EnforcePure claim; the worker marks it Unknown/Refuted and the build fails via launcher exit 5/6 with no reference to the suppression.
3. Grep confirmation: no consumer of `ContractSelectionInventory.Suppress` exists outside the analyzer.
**Confidence**: Medium (behavioral divergence verified by exhaustive grep and quoted code; open question is design intent, but the undocumented profile-dependent meaning flip is factual either way).

### 374. [RESOLVED 2b0d08fc2] The SAT Model-Decode Path Leaks Every Z3 AST Wrapper It Creates - Per-Code-Unit Evaluate Results Are Never Disposed Against the Backend's Own Tested Ownership Invariant

**Location**: `SharpProof.Smt\IrSmtBackend.cs` (`DecodeString` Lines 456-506: Line 462 `model.Evaluate(nullTag, true)` matched but never disposed; Line 473 leaks BOTH the undisposed `_context.MkLength(sequence)` and its Evaluate result; Lines 485-495 dispose `indexExpression`/`element` but NOT the `model.Evaluate(element, true)` result at Line 487 - up to the 1,000,000-code-unit decode cap of leaked wrappers per long-string counterexample, per SAT query); disciplined contrast `CreateSatisfiable` Lines 252-259 (`using var` for analogous `model.Evaluate` results); stated invariant `SharpProof.Smt\Z3ExpressionOwner.cs` Lines 3-5; policy statement `AccountResources` comment Lines 176-181 (finalizer-reliance explicitly named a defect class; remediation lineage commit `283067dfe` covered query CONSTRUCTION, missing decode); pinning test `QueryExpressionOwnerDisposesPinnedZ3ExpressionsWithoutManagedGc` (`SharpProof.Smt.Test\IrSmtBackendTests.cs` Line 562).
**Description**: Every Microsoft.Z3 `Expr` wrapper holds a native reference requiring deterministic `Dispose()` (dec_ref). The decode path allocates and abandons them: for a SAT model whose string value is N code units, roughly N+2 native-referencing wrappers escape per query and are reclaimed only by finalization. `IrSmtBackend` is explicitly long-lived ("outlives hundreds of queries per lane"), so verification lanes accumulate nondeterministic native memory growth and finalizer pressure proportional to decoded counterexample size - resource-tier degradation, not verdict corruption (decode failures remain fail-closed MalformedResult). The sibling call sites prove the omission unintended, and the project pins the ownership invariant with a dedicated test for the construction side only. Distinct from #299 (statistics accounting), #151 (UTF-16 payload/tag semantics - the decoding LOGIC is correct), #300 (model key validation): this is AST-wrapper lifecycle on the decode half.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Reference `SharpProof.Smt` + Microsoft.Z3 4.12.2 (native lib resolved as in `ContainerNativeLibrarySetup.cs`).
2. Build a query whose unique SAT model is a long string: goal `Length(s) > 900_000` over a string variable, wrapped in a `VerificationQuery`.
3. `await backend.CheckAsync(query, CancellationToken.None)` -> Satisfiable; force GC twice and snapshot private bytes / finalizer queue; repeat N times without collecting - footprint grows ~O(string length) per query, reclaimed only at finalization.
4. Contrast: replicate the loop body capturing the `Evaluate` results in `using` and the growth disappears, isolating the missing disposal.
**Confidence**: High (three undisposed sites directly visible against an explicitly documented and tested invariant in the same project; only severity - eventual finalizer reclaim - carries residual uncertainty).

### 377. The OSS Corpus Phase Discards Every Analyzer Diagnostic Outside Instrumented Method Spans - Whole-File Regressions Are Invisible to the Snapshot Gate Unlike the Synthetic Phase

**Location**: `SharpProof.Gates\Corpus\OpenSourceCorpusRunner.cs` (Lines 147-155: observation diagnostics filtered by `ReferenceEquals(diagnostic.Location.SourceTree, target.Tree) && target.Span.Contains(diagnostic.Location.SourceSpan)` before canonicalization - anything outside an instrumented method's FullSpan is dropped with no counter, failure, or trace); contrasting synthetic phase `SharpProof.Gates\Corpus\CorpusGate.cs` (Lines 358-367: `Observe` canonicalizes the FULL diagnostic set, so stray emissions fail snapshot comparison); verdict plumbing Lines 140-157 (`Matches`/failure logic consuming only windowed observations); surrounding fail-closed discipline: instrumentation-count mismatch abort (Lines 94-99), missing outcomes abort (136-140), stale/duplicate snapshot entries rejected (498-508, 159-164).
**Description**: For the 25+ pinned open-source files, only emissions landing INSIDE instrumented method spans participate in snapshot comparison; a regression that makes the analyzer emit diagnostics ANYWHERE ELSE in those compilations - wrong-scope reporting, attribute/target leakage onto non-instrumented members, emissions displaced by future instrumentation changes - alters nothing the gate checks, and `CorpusGateResult` carries no total-emission field that would expose the delta. The synthetic phase proves the intended posture (whole-set canonicalization, unaccountable emissions fail `Matches`); the OSS half silently violates it, so the differential oracle can certify a build whose diagnostic stream differs from the blessed snapshot across entire files. In a gate that otherwise throws on every unaccountable state, discarding analyzer output without accounting is a measurement-validity hole. Distinct from #194/#253/#254 (differential-oracle tautologies elsewhere), #333 (timing fixtures), #346 (samples CI orphan): the corpus attribution window is previously unreported.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Perturb a probe build of the analyzer so it additionally emits a diagnostic on the first NON-instrumented method declaration of each OSS corpus file.
2. Run `dotnet run --project SharpProof.Gates -- corpus`: every instrumented window still contains exactly its blessed diagnostics; ratchet counts unchanged; `Passed = true`, exit 0.
3. Inspect the emitted JSON: no field counts total analyzer diagnostics for the OSS compilation (DiagnosticCount sums only windowed observation.Diagnostics) - the regression contributed zero gate-visible signal.
4. Control: make the same perturbation fire inside an instrumented span - the snapshot comparison fails immediately, isolating the window filter as the blindness.
**Confidence**: Medium (drop-on-the-floor path and synthetic/OSS asymmetry verbatim at HEAD; open question whether strict whole-file accounting was an explicit design goal versus accepted per-method scope).

The entries below were produced by a third read-only hunter wave run later on the same day (20 agents total: 19 read-only hunters over disjoint areas plus this consolidation writer; the clean areas returning NO-FINDINGS audits do not reproduce their ruled-out lists here). They are open findings against the current worktree at commit `801fd9d0a` (the hunters' baseline; the consolidation writer re-verified every primary citation against the tree as of append-time HEAD `c9ac48b1c` immediately before writing). Numbering continues the sequence above.

### 378. [RESOLVED 8bc4ceb7e] Accessor and List-Pattern Call Sites Skip the Definitely-Executable-Prefix Check - False SP0027 When a Prior Statement in the Same Block Definitely Throws

**Status**: Resolved by applying the same definitely-executable direct-block-prefix check used for ordinary calls to accessor and list-pattern call sites, while retaining their receiver/argument replayability rules. Regression coverage now proves that a definitely non-completing prior statement suppresses both accessor and list-pattern refutations; the focused analyzer suite passes 3/3.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\RequiresCallSiteDiscovery.cs` (ternary Lines 194-203 routing `IsAccessorCall(...)` || `operation is IListPatternOperation` to `HasReplayableAccessorEvaluation` only, bypassing `HasReplayablePrefix`; prefix test Lines 448-468; operand-only accessor gate Lines 480-490; flow-state gate Lines 168-176), `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\RequiresCallSiteAnalyzer.cs` (CanReplay gate Lines 281-284; concrete-replay gate Lines 392-403 - neither consults prior statements); knowledge that priors throw exists and is used for ordinary calls `SharpProof.Effects\ManagedAbstractFlow.cs` (Lines 1871-1874 Divide/Remainder excluded from CompletesNormally; Lines 296-301 flow domain still records states past such statements).
**Description**: Replayability for ordinary call sites requires (a) reachable block, (b) caller ownership, and (c) every prior top-level statement completing normally (`HasReplayablePrefix`, RequiresCallSiteDiscovery.cs Lines 448-468). Accessor (PropertyGet/Set, EventAdd/Remove) and list-pattern implicit calls are exempted from (c) entirely - their CanReplay depends only on receiver/arguments completing (Lines 480-490). Because the managed-flow domain does not model definite expression-level faults (division by zero, null-receiver member access, unconditionally-throwing callees, type-initializer bombs), such a prior leaves the block reachable and stateful, so the candidate replays concretely and SP0027 fires for a getter/add/indexer invocation that provably never executes - a fabricated refutation, the worst failure mode for a soundness-first refutation diagnostic. The repo's own pins prove the split: the ordinary-call path suppresses exactly these priors (`NonCompletingCallPrefixCannotProduceARefutation`, RequiresAndControlTests.cs Lines 1281-1358: ThrowingPriorStatement/NullReceiverPriorStatement/TypeInitializerPriorStatement -> empty), while the accessor path is pinned only with no priors (`PropertyAndEventAccessorsCheckRequiresExactlyOnce`, Lines 120-159). Distinct from #297 (Contracts-side placement scanner - different layer), #357/#358/#294 (Effects-side completion-FACT computations - here the facts compute correctly and are simply never consulted on these branches): different site, mechanism, and trigger.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Compile with contract features enabled: `public sealed class Holder { public int Value { get { Contract.Requires(false); return 0; } } }` plus `public static class Fixture { public static void DividesFirst() { var zero = 0; _ = 1 / zero; _ = new Holder().Value; } }`.
2. Run the analyzer with SP0027 enabled: SP0027 "Call to 'Value' violates precondition 'false'" is reported at `_ = new Holder().Value;`.
3. At runtime `DividesFirst` always throws DivideByZeroException at `_ = 1 / zero;`; the getter (and its Requires) never execute - the refutation is fabricated.
4. Control A: delete the divide statement and SP0027 becomes legitimate (matches pinned PropertyAndEventAccessorsCheckRequiresExactlyOnce). Control B: replace the accessor with an ordinary call `Positive(0, -1)` after the same divide - no diagnostic (pinned NonCompletingCallPrefixCannotProduceARefutation), isolating the accessor branch as the cause. Event `+=`/`-=` handlers and `is [...]` list patterns hit the identical bypass via the same ternary.
**Confidence**: High - every hop verified line-by-line against the worktree, including the exact ternary parse, the operand-only concrete gate, and the two pinned contrasting behaviors.

### 379. [RESOLVED 2a7059383] A Concrete Refutation Erases the Method-Level "Selected Analysis Incomplete" Signal - Mixed Refuted+Unknown Outcomes Silently Drop SP0047 for Unreplayable Call Sites

**Status**: Resolved by carrying an explicit unknown-call-site flag alongside the rank-preserving semantic outcome. A mixed refuted/unreplayable regression now retains the SP0027 refutation, emits SP0047, and records the method outcome as Refuted.

**Location**: equality gate `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\AnalyzerFeaturePipeline.cs` Line 345 (`requiresOutcome == AnalyzerSemanticOutcome.Unknown` exactly, within Lines 333-356); aggregation fold `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\RequiresCallSiteAnalyzer.cs` Lines 223-229 (max-rank Combine over call sites); rank order `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\AnalyzerSemanticOutcome.cs` Lines 3-27 (Refuted=5 > Unknown=4); per-site Unknown origin `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\RequiresCallSiteAnalyzer.cs` Lines 281-284.
**Description**: RequiresCallSiteAnalyzer.Analyze folds all call-site outcomes with max-rank Combine; the pipeline reports SP0047 RequiresCallSiteAnalysisUnknown only when the folded outcome equals Unknown exactly. Any single concretely-refuted site (Refuted) therefore masks any number of unreplayable/unknown sites in the same selected method: SP0027 fires for the refuted site while every other call site's preconditions go permanently unchecked with zero completeness diagnostics - defeating the product's own fail-visible guarantee for selected members, pinned by SelectedUnknownRequiresAnalysisReportsOneIncompleteDiagnostic (`SharpProof.Analyzer.Test\RequiresReplaySoundnessTests.cs` Lines 471+, e.g. KnownBranch Lines 483-488: a selected method whose outcome is Unknown must produce exactly one SP0047). Fail direction: silent verification-coverage loss - never a false Proven, but the guaranteed completeness signal is lost by composition. Distinct from #298 (authoring errors hidden behind companion substitution in the Contracts resolver - different layer and mechanism), #170/#233/#297 (different sites): no entry covers outcome aggregation or the SP0047 equality gate.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Compile: `public static void M(bool flag) { Contract.Requires(flag); Positive(-1); if (flag) { Positive(-2); } }` where `Positive(int)` contains `Contract.Requires(value > 0);` (source clause selects the method, matching the pinned KnownBranch shape).
2. Run with SP0027+SP0047 enabled: SP0027 fires for `Positive(-1)`; the `Positive(-2)` site yields Unknown (non-direct statement, CanReplay=false per RequiresCallSiteAnalyzer.cs Lines 281-284), the fold is Refuted, so AnalyzerFeaturePipeline.cs Line 345 is false and NO SP0047 is emitted - the unchecked site is invisible.
3. Delete only `Positive(-1);`: the identical remaining site now produces SP0047 RequiresCallSiteAnalysisUnknown (pinned KnownBranch behavior), isolating refutation-induced masking.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (equality gate, max-rank combine, and per-site Unknown path quoted verbatim; the contrasting pure-Unknown behavior is test-pinned); Medium-High overall since severity is diagnostic-fidelity rather than verdict corruption.

### 380. [RESOLVED 4add2cc16] Per-Query Z3 SeqSort Wrapper Escapes Ownership in QueryEncoder Constructor

**Status**: Resolved by extending the query disposal owner to track `Sort` wrappers and registering each encoder's sequence sort. The SMT ownership regression confirms the native sort wrapper is released without relying on managed finalization.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Smt\IrSmtBackend.cs` (field declaration Line 284; assignment `_stringSort = (SeqSort)_context.MkSeqSort(_context.IntSort);` at Line 295 inside the constructor spanning Lines 286-343, ahead of the UnsupportedIrEncodingException early exits at Lines 304-313; sole uses at Lines 330, 544, 700 - grep confirms no release site anywhere in the assembly); disposal impossibility `SharpProof.Smt\Z3ExpressionOwner.cs` (invariant comment Lines 3-5; `List<Expr>` only, Line 8; `Own<T> where T : Expr` constraint, Lines 13-14); invariant restated in the AccountResources comment IrSmtBackend.cs Lines 178-180; ownership pinning test QueryExpressionOwnerDisposesPinnedZ3ExpressionsWithoutManagedGc `SharpProof.Smt.Test\IrSmtBackendTests.cs` Line 562 with version pin 4.12.2 at Lines 564-566.
**Description**: Every CheckCore constructs a QueryEncoder whose constructor executes `_stringSort = (SeqSort)_context.MkSeqSort(_context.IntSort);` (Line 295). In Microsoft.Z3 4.12.2 (the pinned version) `Context.MkSeqSort(Sort)` returns a fresh SeqSort wrapper whose Z3Object(Context, IntPtr) constructor calls IncRef - every managed wrapper takes its own native reference regardless of how the pointer was obtained (verified against the pinned binding sources; note `_context.IntSort` itself is cached by the binding and does not leak). That reference can only be released by the wrapper's own Dispose/finalizer, but `_stringSort` is a plain field on QueryEncoder, which implements no IDisposable; Z3ExpressionOwner accepts only Expr; and nothing else in the file disposes it. So each and every query - UNSAT proofs, SAT models, Unknown/rlimit-exhausted outcomes, even UnsupportedIrEncodingException early exits, since Line 295 precedes type validation - leaks one native-referencing wrapper until GC finalization. This is precisely the failure class the repository itself codifies as a defect for this component ("relying on the finalizer would make a long-lived backend's native footprint nondeterministic", Z3ExpressionOwner.cs Lines 3-5; repeated in the AccountResources comment at IrSmtBackend.cs Lines 178-180), and the construction-side invariant is enforced by a dedicated test for the Expr population while the sort escapes it structurally. Blast radius: resource-tier only - nondeterministic native refcount retention and finalizer pressure proportional to query count on a backend explicitly documented as "outlives hundreds of queries per lane"; sorts are interned by Z3 so node memory does not grow and verdicts are unaffected (fail-safe direction). DISTINCTNESS: #374 ([RESOLVED 2b0d08fc2]) covers the DECODE half (DecodeString undisposed model.Evaluate results and MkLength/MkNth intermediates - Expr wrappers, SAT-with-string-model trigger only, intermediates abandoned despite a usable using pattern nearby). This finding is the CONSTRUCTION half at a different site, a different wrapper kind (SeqSort, which the owner cannot even hold), a different trigger (every CheckCore call including pure UNSAT proofs - untouched by the decode remediation), and a structurally different mechanism (no disposal pathway exists at all, versus abandoned intermediates). Not #299 (statistics accounting block), not #151 (payload semantics), not #271 (library identity).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Reference SharpProof.Smt with Microsoft.Z3 4.12.2 and install the native resolver as in ContainerNativeLibrarySetup.cs.
2. Run N sequential `await backend.CheckAsync(query, CancellationToken.None)` calls on one long-lived IrSmtBackend using trivial UNSAT queries (e.g., goal factory.Boolean(true) - no string variables, no SAT model), keeping the backend alive.
3. Snapshot the finalizer queue / forced-GC-surviving Microsoft.Z3.SeqSort instances between batches: count grows by exactly one per CheckAsync call, reclaimed only at finalization; instrumenting MkSeqSort shows one owning wrapper per query with no matching Dispose.
4. Contrast: wrapping the Line-295 result in a disposable holder (or registering it with the owner) eliminates the growth, isolating the missing release.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (wrapper-owning constructor verified against the pinned binding's own source; no release site exists anywhere in the assembly; trigger is unconditional per query); severity explicitly Low (finalizer eventually reclaims interned sort references; no wrong-verdict capability), mirroring the resource-tier framing #374 received.

### 381. [RESOLVED dc330a153] Launcher-Synthesized No-Result Response Is Born Protocol-Invalid (worker.no_result vs the run_projection Table) - Every Worker Death Is Forced Through the Destructive Rewrite, Which Then Publishes Fabricated MalformedResult Evidence

**Status**: Resolved by aligning the `worker.no_result` projection with the launcher's `InfrastructureFailure` result. The package integration and abnormal-exit regressions now validate the typed failure without the repair rewrite.
