**Location**: fallback arm `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher\LauncherProjections.generated.cs` Line 32 (InfrastructureFailure reason + code worker.no_result); synthesis `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher\Program.cs` Lines 170-182 via TryWriteLauncherFailureAsync -> CreateIncomplete (Lines 885-894: rows Incomplete/InfrastructureFailure, Errors=[worker.no_result]); destructive rewrite + second validation Program.cs Lines 188-203; projection table `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol\WorkerResultAssembler.cs` (error-projected state precedence Lines 160-164; worker.no_result -> (Failed, MalformedResult) at Lines 284-289; directInfrastructureFailure row escape Lines 234-242); enforcing check `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol\ProtocolJson.cs` (ValidateRun Lines 718-763; response.run_projection comparison at Lines 729-738); contradicting pin WorkerExitWithoutResultProducesTypedFailure `SharpProof.Package.Test\WorkerMsBuildIntegrationTests.cs` Lines 1283-1300 (assertions 1292-1299).
**Description**: Commit `5b21aeeba` flipped NoResultFailure's fallback reason from MalformedResult to InfrastructureFailure while keeping code "worker.no_result", but ProjectError still maps "worker.no_result" -> (Failed, MalformedResult) (WorkerResultAssembler.cs Lines 284-289), and when errors are present TryProjectRunState takes the error-projected state (Lines 160-164). The launcher-synthesized crash artifact (Failed/InfrastructureFailure, rows Incomplete/InfrastructureFailure, Errors=[worker.no_result]) therefore fails its own response.run_projection check the moment ValidateAndReport reads it back - deterministically, for every worker death without a result except the special-cased 124/125 arms (worker.timeout/containment.unavailable project consistently; NormalizeNoResultExitCode maps codes 2-6 to 3 while leaving 124/125 untouched, Program.cs Lines 236-239). Consequences per Program.cs Lines 188-203: the typed worker.no_result/"exited without a result" evidence is overwritten by generic worker.malformed_result/"unavailable or malformed" fabrication, which PublishOutputs then republishes; stderr carries the true cause transiently while the persisted/published artifact and second-pass stdout summary assert MalformedResult - contradictory machine-readable streams for one launch. The repo's own regression pin proves the drift: WorkerExitWithoutResultProducesTypedFailure asserts final Errors[0].Code == "worker.no_result" alongside FailureReason == MalformedResult and Validate(response).IsValid - a triple the current code cannot produce (first-pass-invalid -> rewrite changes the code; first-pass-valid would keep InfrastructureFailure). Distinctness: #305 [RESOLVED] (raw exit-code forwarding) is a different layer; #179 recorded forwarding-level cause loss, not a producer/projection-table divergence making the synthesized artifact self-invalid; #306 documents the repair handler's general destruction/duplication properties - this is the cross-file table contradiction that newly routes EVERY no-result event into that handler (with the corrected observation that in today's tree the first pass emits only error lines, so the durable damage is the fabricated republished verdict). Fail direction: still fail-closed on exit status, but typed diagnostics are destroyed deterministically.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Run a verification whose worker exits nonzero without writing --result (e.g., SIGKILL, or the suite's resultless-worker stub exiting small -> normalized 3).
2. Launcher synthesizes Failed/InfrastructureFailure/[worker.no_result]; first ValidateAndReport fails with exactly response.run_projection (ProjectError("worker.no_result")=(Failed,MalformedResult) != InfrastructureFailure) - stderr shows "SharpProof response.run_projection: ..." followed by "SharpProof worker worker.no_result: ...".
3. Program.cs Lines 191-195 overwrite result.json with Failed/MalformedResult/[worker.malformed_result]; second pass validates (rows pass via the directInfrastructureFailure escape, WorkerResultAssembler.cs Lines 234-242) and the fabricated verdict is published.
4. Compare with WorkerExitWithoutResultProducesTypedFailure's assertion Errors[0].Code == "worker.no_result": unreachable at HEAD - the pin is red, isolating the drift to 5b21aeeba.
**Confidence**: High - every hop statically verified in the worktree; git history shows the two contract sides were last consistent before 5b21aeeba; the surviving test asserts the pre-drift state and cannot pass under the current table.

### 382. [RESOLVED b485b9186] SARIF Results Emit kind:"review" With Policy-Derived Levels (note/warning/error), Violating SARIF 2.1.0's kind-Other-Than-Fail-Requires-Level-None Rule - the Strict/Warn Policy Escalation Is Unrepresentable in the Machine-Readable Tier

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher\SarifProjection.cs` ($schema sarif-2.1.0.json declared at Line 100; claim results Lines 117-146 emitting review + Level(request.VerifyPolicy, "note") at Lines 132-136; SP0047 incomplete results Lines 148-165 with kind/level at Lines 155-156; Result passes kind/level verbatim at Lines 167-174); level helper `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher\Program.cs` Line 981; pinned intentionally by SarifClaimPresentationFollowsOutcomeAndPolicy `SharpProof.Package.Test\LauncherArgumentTests.cs` Lines 2003-2019; README.md Line 488 sells deterministic SARIF 2.1.0.
**Description**: For every Unknown claim and every incomplete-callable SP0047 row the projection emits kind:"review" together with LauncherPresentation.Level(policy, "note") - i.e., note/warning/error under advisory/warn-on-unknown/require-proven. SARIF 2.1.0 normatively constrains a result whose kind is anything other than fail to carry level none (section 3.27.9/3.27.10 result constraints). The document declares the 2.1.0 schema (Line 100) and README sells deterministic SARIF 2.1.0 (README.md Line 488), yet every run containing an abstention or incomplete callable is nonconformant: spec-honoring consumers must ignore level for non-fail kinds, so the entire policy-severity projection - the SARIF counterpart of the console SP0047/claim severities that drive exits 5/6 - silently disappears in the machine-readable artifact, while stricter validators flag the file. The same function proves the rule was known: the Proven case correctly emits pass+none (Lines 132-135) - only the review arms deviate. The mapping is enshrined by SarifClaimPresentationFollowsOutcomeAndPolicy (review/note under Advisory, review/warning under WarnOnUnknown, review/error under RequireProven), so correcting it forces a deliberate test update - the same "pinning cements the wrong answer" pattern accepted as #293. Distinct from #367 (MSBuild error-code attribution) and #330 (evidence-wrapper failure flattening) - different properties, tiers, and mechanisms; no BUGS.md entry touches result kind/level mapping.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Verify a project with one unknown claim under --verify-policy warn-on-unknown (or require-proven) with -p:SharpProofVerifySarifFile=out.sarif.
2. Inspect runs[0].results[0]: "kind":"review", "level":"warning" (or "error") - a forbidden combination per SARIF 2.1.0; the SP0047 incomplete rows repeat it.
3. Contrast the Proven sibling row (kind pass, level none) showing the constraint applied selectively; run any spec-conformance checker/GitHub SARIF ingestion to see the rejection/misprojection.
**Confidence**: Medium-High - emission shape and selective pass/none compliance verified line-by-line plus the pinning test; the sole residual is reliance on the SARIF 2.1.0 normative text (kind != fail => level none), which is widely reproduced by consumer validators.
**Status**: Resolved by emitting SARIF-compliant `level: "none"` for review results and preserving the requested policy severity in `properties.sharpProofPolicyLevel`; launcher projection tests cover all policy/outcome combinations.

### 383. [RESOLVED 8075ff9bb] Duplicate Byte-Identical Reference Modules Make IL Summary Authorities Ambiguous - Whole-Manifest Collection Aborts With Opaque SP0049

**Location**: singular-binding throw `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\CompilerManifestArtifactProducer.cs` Lines 129-145 (`module.Length != 1` throw at Lines 137-141, "An IL summary authority is not bound to one captured module."); evidence provenance `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\CompilerImplementationIlSummaryLowerer.cs` Lines 177-191 (authority EvidenceSha256 = whole-module-file SHA-256 via CompilerCompilationCapture.Hash(stream) at Lines 180-182) carried into authorities holding only ModuleName + that hash `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\CompilerRelationalSummaryProvider.cs` Lines 385-401; capture without dedup `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\CompilerCompilationCapture.cs` (CaptureReferences maps each reference independently, Lines 174-187; module Sha256 = Hash(stream) at Line 274); consumer mirror `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact\CompilationFingerprint.cs` (Count(module => Name && Mvid && Sha256 match) == 1 at Lines 308-323; ValidReferences checks limits only, Lines 347-371; name-uniqueness enforced per-reference only at Lines 451-459); blast-radius conversion `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\FinalCompilationCollector.cs` Lines 41-51 (blanket catch -> single CompilerManifestFailureRule -> SP0049).
**Description**: For a static scalar helper in a referenced assembly, the IL-summary path derives evidence identity from the module's file bytes (CompilerImplementationIlSummaryLowerer.cs Lines 180-182) and records only OwningModuleName downstream (CompilerRelationalSummaryProvider.cs Lines 385-401). BuildSummaryEvidence then re-binds the authority by scanning every module of EVERY captured reference (CompilerManifestArtifactProducer.cs Lines 131-136) and demands exactly one match. A fully legal compilation state - the same assembly reachable twice under different full paths with identical content (e.g., a direct HintPath reference plus a transitive copy, both surviving into compilation.References; CaptureReferences captures each entry independently and nothing validates cross-reference identity or alias-dedup) - yields two byte-identical CompilerReferenceModuleSnapshots, so module.Length == 2 and the producer throws InvalidOperationException("An IL summary authority is not bound to one captured module."), which FinalCompilationCollector.Collect collapses into the single opaque CompilerManifestFailureRule (SP0049) diagnostic. Every callable's verification evidence is discarded: fail-closed direction (never a wrong verdict), but a routine reference topology deterministically kills the entire project's verification with a message naming neither file nor cause - while the graceful alternative (abstain the summary -> MissingCallSummary/UnsupportedBody for one claim) exists one call away. The worker-side mirror (CompilationFingerprint.cs Lines 318-323) enforces the same false singularity, confirming it is a design invariant rather than a local slip. Distinct from #353 (duplicate trusted-attributes reference silently disabling ANALYZER features via matches.Length != 1 in ContractApiIdentityResolver - different assembly, site, and consequence: silent inertness vs whole-manifest abort), #321 (payload size vs fixed budget), #277 (recursion depth), #322 (Int64 opcode gate): no existing entry covers summary-authority module binding.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Create helper Lib.dll exposing `public static long Inc(long x) => x + 1L;`, copy it to two directories (libs/Lib.dll, vendor/Lib.dll), and reference BOTH paths from a verified project; add `[SharpProof.EnforcePure] public static long Call(long x) => Lib.Inc(x);`.
2. Build in-container with SharpProofVerify enabled: IsCandidate admits Inc; the IL summary lowers via the module-stream hash (CompilerImplementationIlSummaryLowerer.cs Lines 177-191); CreateAuthority emits an ImplementationIl row keyed on module name + module sha256.
3. BuildSummaryEvidence finds TWO captured modules matching name+Sha256 -> throws; observe the build failing with exactly one SP0049 diagnostic whose text is "InvalidOperationException: An IL summary authority is not bound to one captured module."; delete either reference and the identical project builds green, isolating reference duplication as the sole variable.
**Confidence**: Medium-High - every cited hop read and line-verified in the current worktree (evidence-hash provenance, singular-binding throw, absent cross-reference dedup, catch-to-SP0049 conversion); residual uncertainty only whether MSBuild graphs producing byte-identical duplicate PE references occur frequently enough for day-to-day impact.
**Status**: Resolved by accepting multiple same-name, same-content module captures when their MVIDs agree, while continuing to reject missing or inconsistent evidence. Worker-side fingerprint validators and the producer are covered by artifact and aliased-Roslyn-reference regressions.

### 384. [RESOLVED c06332661] Maintained Documentation Promises Superseded Companion-Precedence Semantics That Fix Commit a5e879fde Removed - One Misplaced Direct Clause Now Displaces Every Companion Clause Via Hard Binding Failure While Four Docs State the Opposite

**Location**: hard-fail block `SharpProof.Contracts\EffectiveContractSourceResolver.cs` Lines 84-92 (early InvalidClausePlacement return when placement errors exist and no valid clause; companion branch unreachable at Lines 94-124); contradicting docs README.md Lines 594-600, docs\coverage-and-limits.md Lines 125-128, docs\public-api.md Lines 37-42, CHANGELOG.md Lines 179-182; pinning test InvalidTargetPlacementCannotBeHiddenByAValidCompanion `SharpProof.Contracts.Test\ContractBinderTests.cs` Lines 170-201; change introduced by commit a5e879fde (touched only EffectiveContractSourceResolver.cs + ContractBinderTests.cs, no doc).
**Description**: Fix commit `a5e879fde` ("Reject invalid direct contracts before companions") resolved #298 by inserting an early return: when the direct inventory has any placement error and no valid clause, ResolveCore returns InvalidClausePlacement before companion resolution can run, so a [ContractFor] companion's clauses never bind for that member (pinned by the renamed test asserting failure). Four normative artifacts still state exactly the superseded rule as current behavior: README says a recognized misplaced direct clause "neither displaces a valid companion nor contributes executable argument effects"; coverage-and-limits.md says "When no valid direct clause exists, a valid companion remains usable even if the target has a misplaced clause"; public-api.md says misplaced direct clauses "do not displace a valid companion"; CHANGELOG says "otherwise a valid ContractFor companion remains usable despite misplaced or nested target clauses." Under shipped code the misplaced clause does displace the companion: all contract binding for the member fails, the member's contracts vanish entirely, and strict/require-proven builds fail downstream with aggregate SP0047-style errors instead of the documented SP0024-plus-working-companion behavior. Fail direction is fail-closed (no wrong proofs), but users authoring per these documents get total, undocumented loss of companion contracts from one stray conditional clause. Distinct from #298 itself (that entry recorded the pre-fix behavioral bug - companion silently superseding invalid clauses - which this commit fixed): this is the documentation residue the fix left behind at different sites (four maintained docs vs binder code), matching the ledger's accepted documentation-defect family (#340/#341/#199/#287).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Compile a project with `public class Gate { public int Read(int x) { if (x > 0) { Contract.Requires(x < 100); } return x; } }` plus a valid companion `[ContractFor(typeof(Gate))] static class GateContracts { public static void Read(Gate g, int x) { Contract.Requires(x != 0); } }`.
2. Per coverage-and-limits.md Lines 125-128 expect the companion's `x != 0` precondition to bind while the conditional clause yields only SP0024; actual: ContractBinder.Bind/BindRequires(Gate.Read) fails with InvalidClausePlacement (EffectiveContractSourceResolver.cs Lines 84-92) and zero clauses survive - pinned by InvalidTargetPlacementCannotBeHiddenByAValidCompanion.
3. Delete the misplaced conditional clause only: binding succeeds with the identical companion clause, isolating the doc/code divergence to the misplaced-clause-plus-companion interaction the four documents describe with the opposite outcome.
**Confidence**: High - code block, all four document passages, pinning test, and the fix commit's file scope each verified directly in the current worktree; the only judgment point (doc-defect classification) follows the ledger's own precedent for accepted entries.
**Status**: Resolved by updating README.md, coverage-and-limits.md, public-api.md, and CHANGELOG.md to describe the current invalid-direct-clause precedence; the existing binder regression remains green.

### 385. [RESOLVED b8762ae4c] Uncapped File.ReadAllBytes(manifestPath) Survives the #310 Remediation - an Oversized Published Manifest Is Buffered Whole and Crashes the Validator Outside Its Catch Filter

**Status**: Resolved by routing manifest hashing through the bounded raw-byte protocol reader. The helper preserves exact bytes (including a UTF-8 BOM), rejects growth beyond the 16 MiB protocol limit, and is covered by a Worker protocol regression; the MSBuild validator now shares the same cap as its sibling reads.

**Location**: raw read `SharpProof.BuildTasks\ValidatePublishedVerificationResult.cs` Line 95 (`var manifestBytes = File.ReadAllBytes(manifestPath);`, hashed at Lines 96-97); catch filter excluding OutOfMemoryException at Lines 131-133; designed fail-closed path at Lines 135-138 and 144-178; capped siblings fixed by commit 3b448037c at Lines 52-53, 71-72, 78-79; cap primitive `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol\ProtocolJson.cs` (MaximumJsonBytes = 16 * 1024 * 1024 at Line 12; ReadUtf8File fixed-buffer + InvalidDataException over limit at Lines 35-57); producer/launcher contrast `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact\CompilerManifestArtifact.cs` (Serialize cap throw Lines 330-335; MaximumBytes = WorkerProtocolJson.MaximumJsonBytes at Line 841; capped ReadAllBytes(path, maximumBytes = MaximumBytes) at Lines 861-881 used by the launcher at `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher\Program.cs` Lines 1174-1177); regression test PublishedProtocolFilesAreSizeBoundedBeforeDeserialization (`SharpProof.Package.Test\BuildTaskTests.cs` Lines 775-810) oversizes ONLY the request file (manifest fixture is "{}").
**Description**: Commit `3b448037c` ([BUGS.md #310 RESOLVED]) replaced exactly three DecodeUtf8(File.ReadAllBytes(...)) call sites in this task - invocation result, request, result - with the capped WorkerProtocolJson.ReadUtf8File, and added PublishedProtocolFilesAreSizeBoundedBeforeDeserialization, which oversizes ONLY the request file. The fourth read of the same published-file family was left untouched: Line 95 still buffers the entire published compiler-manifest.json with File.ReadAllBytes before SHA-256 hashing, with no size check anywhere on the path. The producer caps legitimate manifests at 16 MiB (CompilerManifestArtifact.cs Lines 330-335/841) and the launcher's own equivalent read is capped (Program.cs Lines 1174-1177 -> CompilerManifestArtifact.cs Lines 861-881), so an over-cap published manifest is by definition torn/corrupt/tampered state - precisely #310's stated threat model. When it occurs, the allocation scales with attacker/corruption-controlled size; OutOfMemoryException matches none of the filter arms at Lines 131-133, so the task faults unhandled instead of taking its designed fail-closed path (Log.LogError("did not publish a valid current result") + TryInvalidateRejectedPublication, Lines 135-138/144-178) that the three sibling reads correctly take. Fail direction: converts a fail-closed typed rejection into an unhandled MSBuild-node crash with no diagnostic and no invalidation (availability/attribution loss; no wrong-verdict capability). Distinctness vs #310: that entry is marked resolved and its fix demonstrably bounds three sites; this is the surviving fourth instance at a distinct site (manifest hash input vs protocol JSON deserialization inputs), missed by both the fix commit and its regression test - the ledger's resolution overstates coverage.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Using the exact seam of the shipped regression test (PublishedProtocolFilesAreSizeBoundedBeforeDeserialization), construct ValidatePublishedVerificationResult with valid small request.json/result.json fixtures and ManifestPath pointing at a sparse multi-GiB file.
2. Run task.Execute(): the capped reads at Lines 52-53/71-72/78-79 validate, then Line 95 buffers the full multi-GiB manifest before hashing.
3. On a constrained host the process hits OOM; the exception is outside the Lines 131-133 filter, so instead of the logged "exceeds the 16777216 byte limit"-style error plus publication invalidation (what the request fixture asserts at BuildTaskTests.cs Lines 800-804), the task throws unhandled. Contrast: apply the identical oversize to RequestPath - typed LogError + invalidate, exactly as the pinned test asserts.
**Confidence**: High - every cited line read in the current worktree; the fix commit diff (git show 3b448037c) mechanically confirms three-of-four coverage and the new test's request-only scope; cap semantics of ReadUtf8File and the launcher contrast verified verbatim.

### 386. Supervisor Flips a Fully Successful, Authenticated Run to Exit 124 Whenever Post-Exit Cleanup Observed Any Descendant - Green Published Verdicts Fail the Build With Fabricated Timeout Semantics

**Location**: `SharpProof.BuildTasks\VerifierProcessSupervisor.cs` (child-exit wait Lines 140-148; first StopDescendantsForRun Lines 149-154 with hadDescendants = cleanup.HadDescendants; retry loop Lines 157-171 accumulating hadDescendants |= ... even when cleanup.Complete becomes true; receipt + return at Lines 184-189: cancellation.IsCancellationRequested ? 143 : hadDescendants ? 124 : directExitCode); consumer `SharpProof.BuildTasks\RunVerifier.cs` (TrySetTerminalCause(Completed) when output drained at Line 334; authenticated receipt -> containmentFailed stays false at Lines 380-385; ExitCode forwards process.ExitCode - the supervisor process's return value - at Lines 386-392); build consequence `SharpProof.Verifier\buildTransitive\SharpProof.Verifier.targets` Lines 285-286 (codeless Error Text="SharpProof verifier failed with exit code $(_SharpProofVerifierExitCode)."); unpinned mapping - the only supervisor return-path test covers incomplete-cleanup 125 (SupervisorBoundsIncompleteCleanupWithoutAuthenticatingIt, `SharpProof.Package.Test\BuildTaskTests.cs` Lines 98-133).
**Description**: After the supervised launcher child exits, the supervisor sweeps remaining descendants (leaked worker-tree processes are reparented to the supervisor). Any descendant merely OBSERVED during the sweep windows - even one that is then successfully stopped and reaped with cleanup.Complete == true and an authenticated SharpProof.Cleanup/1 receipt written (Line 184) - makes Line 189 discard the child's real exit code and return 124. Because the receipt authenticates, RunVerifier records terminal cause Completed (not Timeout), containmentFailed stays false, and forwards the raw 124; the targets raise "verifier failed with exit code 124" and the build goes red although the launcher published a valid green result.json moments earlier. Three properties make this a defect rather than policy: (a) semantic fabrication - nothing timed out, yet 124 is the codebase's dedicated timeout vocabulary (RunVerifier.cs Lines 390-391 map VerifierTerminalCause.Timeout to 124), and the killed descendant is never named in any log; (b) scheduling-race nondeterminism - the identical leak yields green if the helper exits just before the first sweep scan (Lines 140-148) and fake-timeout red if the sweep catches it, so verdict-affecting outcomes hinge on process timing; (c) internal inconsistency - hadDescendants deliberately does NOT set containmentFailed (the authenticated receipt path at Lines 172-184 proves containment succeeded), yet the exit code is overridden anyway. Trigger realism: normal runs leave zero descendants at child exit (the launcher reaps the worker before exiting; Z3 is in-process), so this fires exactly on the leaked-descendant class the ledger already tracks host-side (#315 documents workers leaking helpers on the success path - the supervisor side inherits and kills them, then lands here). Fail direction: false-red/availability plus misleading attribution; never wrong-green. Distinctness: #315 is the Host-side inverse polarity (orphans survive, success preserved, no kill); #240/#318 are the synchronous/async FailFast anchor family (different mechanism and site); #305 [RESOLVED] is launcher-to-reserved-vocabulary forwarding of worker exits; the only pinned supervisor return-path test covers incomplete-cleanup 125 - the hadDescendants ? 124 mapping is unpinned.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Run a verification whose worker leaks one long-lived helper (per the #315 scenario) but which otherwise completes: launcher exits 0 after publishing a Proven result.json.
2. Observe the supervisor's post-exit sweep stop the helper (HadDescendants=true, Complete=true, receipt written) and exit 124 (VerifierProcessSupervisor.cs Line 189).
3. RunVerifier forwards 124 with terminal cause Completed and no containment failure; the targets emit "error : SharpProof verifier failed with exit code 124." while the published result.json on disk is Complete/green.
4. Control: rerun with the helper pre-exited before the launcher exits - identical logical run finishes green, isolating the scan-timing race as the discriminator.
**Confidence**: Medium - mechanism, consumer mapping, and unpinned status verified line-by-line at HEAD; residual uncertainty is defect-vs-intent (a "descendant activity taints the run" policy is conceivable but undocumented, contradicted by the receipt-authenticated containmentFailed=false path, and applied with timing nondeterminism).

### 387. [RESOLVED b158ab3ac] BannedSymbols.txt Bans Only the Two Optional-Parameter GetSemanticModel Overloads - the Primary GetSemanticModel(SyntaxTree) Overloads Compile Clean Under RS0030 in Every Project That Lacks the Meta-Analyzer

**Status**: Resolved by enumerating the missing `Compilation` options and `CSharpCompilation` Boolean overloads in `BannedSymbols.txt`, with an architecture regression that requires both exact identities.

**Location**: `BannedSymbols.txt` Lines 12-14 (section header "Semantic models may only be obtained by the audited host adapter"; Line 13 bans only Compilation.GetSemanticModel(SyntaxTree,System.Boolean); Line 14 bans only CSharpCompilation.GetSemanticModel(SyntaxTree,SemanticModelOptions); full-file read confirms no other entry covers GetSemanticModel among the 51 lines); audited adapter proving intent `SharpProof.Frontend\CompilationModelProvider.cs` ("The single audited boundary" doc Lines 3-5; pragma warning disable RS0030 at Line 15; owner.GetSemanticModel(tree, ignoreAccessibility: false) - the adapter itself using the banned 2-arg overload under pragma - at Line 25); enforcement wiring `Directory.Build.props` Lines 82-92 (BannedApiAnalyzers + BannedSymbols AdditionalFiles + RS0030-as-error for the production projects); meta-analyzer blanket rule `SharpProof.Meta.Analyzers\SharpProofSoundnessAnalyzer.cs` Lines 167-172 (bans ALL GetSemanticModel on Compilation/CSharpCompilation by NAME outside CompilationModelProvider); textual backstop `SharpProof.ArchitectureTest\BoundaryEnforcementTests.cs` (.GetSemanticModel( scan around Lines 215-223); production projects WITHOUT the meta-analyzer where the hole is live at compile time: `SharpProof.Gates\SharpProof.Gates.csproj` (direct Reference HintPath=$(RoslynTargetsPath)\bincore\Microsoft.CodeAnalysis*.dll at Lines 16-21; no Meta.Analyzers ProjectReference anywhere in the file) and `Tools\SharpProof.Fuzz\SharpProof.Fuzz.csproj` (CodeAnalysis reachable transitively via Ir/Frontend project references at Lines 19-26; no Meta.Analyzers reference; the same absence verified for SharpProof.BuildTasks and SharpProof.Worker.Protocol).
**Description**: The ban list's own section contract is "semantic models may only be obtained by the audited host adapter," but it enumerates overloads individually and bans exactly two of the existing ones: the base Compilation.GetSemanticModel(SyntaxTree, System.Boolean) and the hiding CSharpCompilation.GetSemanticModel(SyntaxTree, SemanticModelOptions) (Roslyn declares the latter as a new method, so both doc-IDs are distinct, and both one-arg forms are absent). Any production project wired for RS0030 (Directory.Build.props Lines 89-92) can therefore call the most common acquisition form, compilation.GetSemanticModel(tree), with zero RS0030 and zero build failure - precisely the bypass the adapter's pragma + 2-arg-only bans were built to prevent. The omission is partially backstopped by two later layers: SPMETA001's name-based rule (SharpProofSoundnessAnalyzer.cs Lines 167-172), which covers the production projects referencing Meta.Analyzers but NOT SharpProof.Fuzz, SharpProof.Gates, SharpProof.BuildTasks, or SharpProof.Worker.Protocol (verified absent from all four csprojs; Gates/Fuzz additionally hold compilations they synthesize themselves), and the architecture suite's textual scan (BoundaryEnforcementTests.cs), which catches direct calls only when that test project runs - not during normal dotnet build. Net effect: in Gates/Fuzz (the soundness-evidence tooling that already builds its own Roslyn compilations), a semantic-model acquisition shortcut compiles green locally and in every PR build; enforcement exists only in the separate ArchitectureTest stage. This is the same layered-defense-shares-an-incomplete-family-assumption class accepted as #245/#328 (display family exact-name gaps, since fixed) and #324 (synthesis/diagnostics gaps): distinct site and axis - the semantic-model acquisition family, neither the ParseText/synthesis family of #324 nor the display-string family of #328, and no BUGS.md entry mentions the GetSemanticModel overload set. Fail direction: enforcement-timing gap (compile-time silence; CI-test-time detection), not wrong verdicts.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. In SharpProof.Gates (or any production project without a Meta.Analyzers reference), add `var model = someCompilation.GetSemanticModel(someTree);` next to the existing raw Microsoft.CodeAnalysis references.
2. Build the solution: zero RS0030 (no doc-ID match in BannedSymbols.txt Lines 3-51), zero SPMETA001 (meta-analyzer not referenced by Gates/Fuzz), build succeeds even though the identical call in SharpProof.Frontend outside CompilationModelProvider.cs fails under TWAE+RS0030.
3. Contrast: run the SharpProof.ArchitectureTest suite - the .GetSemanticModel( textual scan (BoundaryEnforcementTests.cs around Lines 215-223) now fails, proving the compile-time layer alone was defeated while only the test-stage scan holds; adding the missing one-arg doc-ID lines to BannedSymbols.txt makes step 2 fail immediately.
**Confidence**: Medium - the missing doc-IDs, the adapter-only intent (section comment + pragma'd 2-arg use + SPMETA001's name-based breadth + arch-test scan), and the exact set of uncovered projects were each verified directly in the worktree; residual uncertainty is only whether maintainers consider CI-test-time backstopping sufficient, which is the same judgment call under which #245/#328 were accepted.

### 388. [RESOLVED 27d107785] CHANGELOG Advertises a macOS x64 Package-Consumer Job That the Workflow Never Defines

**Status**: Resolved by describing the hosted macOS consumer lane without claiming an unconfigured Intel-specific job.

**Location**: CHANGELOG.md Lines 106-107 vs `.github\workflows\package-consumers.yml` Lines 140-150 (portable-consumers matrix: ubuntu-latest/linux, windows-latest/windows, macos-latest/macos); receipt vocabulary scripts\Test-SharpProofPortableConsumer.ps1 Line 6 (ValidateSet linux|windows|macos) and scripts\Write-SharpProofQualificationReceipt.ps1 Line 6 (portable-linux|portable-windows|portable-macos).
**Description**: CHANGELOG.md states "Package-consumer CI restores the exact same packed bytes across Windows x64, Linux x64, macOS x64, and macOS ARM64" - four environments. The only portable consumer job defines exactly three runners: ubuntu-latest (Linux x64), windows-latest (Windows x64), and macos-latest (Apple-silicon ARM64 only; GitHub has no Intel macos-latest image). A repo-wide search finds no macos-13/osx-x64/Intel-mac job anywhere; the receipt vocabulary itself (Write-SharpProofQualificationReceipt.ps1 Line 6 - portable-linux|portable-windows|portable-macos) records only one macOS family. So a macOS x64 restore of the packed bytes never executes, while the changelog presents it as part of the tested release-qualification surface. Impact: maintainers and downstream readers rely on CHANGELOG's enumerated test surface for compatibility/release decisions; the claimed Intel-macOS coverage silently does not exist and no internal evidence (three receipts) contradicts the four-way claim. Distinct from #249 (hosted runners lacking seeded pinned packages - a breakage of jobs that DO run; the seed step at package-consumers.yml Lines 163-186 now addresses it) and from #346/#250 (orphaned samples/evidence modules): this is a factual overcount of the consumer matrix in a maintained changelog.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Read CHANGELOG.md Lines 106-107; note the enumeration of four OS/arch combinations including "macOS x64".
2. Read .github/workflows/package-consumers.yml Lines 140-150: the portable-consumers matrix contains exactly three entries; the macOS entry uses macos-latest, which GitHub serves exclusively as arm64.
3. Search the repository for macos-13, osx-x64, or a fourth matrix entry: none exists; Test-SharpProofPortableConsumer.ps1 Line 6 and Write-SharpProofQualificationReceipt.ps1 Line 6 confirm exactly three OS families are ever exercised or recorded.
**Confidence**: High - both cited sides read verbatim; the only external premise (macos-latest = ARM64-only hosted runners) is standard GitHub-hosted-runner fact, and the repo's own three-family receipt vocabulary corroborates that no fourth environment is intended elsewhere.

### 389. [RESOLVED 2832ed9c4] Sample Runner Does Not Redirect Its Product-Pack Outputs - New-LocalPackageFeed Builds the Package Graph Into Source-Tree obj/bin, Falsifying samples/README.md's Isolation Sentence

**Status**: Resolved by staging the current source (and preserving its GitHub origin for Source Link) under the runner's temporary root before packing, and routing the NuGet cache there. Package and sample outputs no longer use the source tree. The isolated-pack architecture regression passes, and the complete sample matrix passes from a disposable canonical checkout.

**Location**: isolation sentence samples\README.md Lines 25-28 and documented invocation Lines 18-23; unredirected pack `scripts\Test-SharpProofSamples.ps1` (New-LocalPackageFeed Lines 234-256: bare `dotnet pack <project> --configuration Release --output $feed --nologo` at Lines 245-253, with no output-path properties and no --no-build); redirected contrast Invoke-SampleBuild Lines 259-308 (-p:BaseIntermediateOutputPath/-p:BaseOutputPath/-p:RestorePackagesPath at Lines 277-281); pack graph `SharpProof.Package\SharpProof.Package.csproj` Lines 22-41 (ProjectReferences Attributes, CompilerCollector, Analyzer, ContractForGenerator).
**Description**: samples/README.md says "The runner packs the current product when no feed is supplied, ... redirects ALL obj, bin, and package-cache state to a temporary directory." In reality only the eight SAMPLE builds are redirected: Invoke-SampleBuild passes -p:BaseIntermediateOutputPath/-p:BaseOutputPath/-p:RestorePackagesPath (Lines 277-281), but New-LocalPackageFeed invokes bare "dotnet pack <project> --configuration Release --output $feed --nologo" (Lines 245-253) with no output-path properties and no --no-build, so MSBuild builds SharpProof.Package plus its full ProjectReference graph (Attributes, CompilerCollector, ContractForGenerator, Analyzer - csproj Lines 22-41) into each project's default obj/bin inside the source tree. Under the README's own primary invocation (docker compose run --rm tooling dev -lc 'pwsh ... Test-SharpProofSamples.ps1', README Lines 18-23, which per open finding #339 skips task-workspace staging onto the bind-mounted checkout), these product trees are written to the host worktree - exactly the concurrent-build hazard AGENTS.md forbids ("Never run concurrent builds against one worktree's bind-mounted bin, obj, or artifacts directories"). Distinctness from #339 proven: different site (the script's pack call, not entrypoint.sh's dev dispatch), different mechanism (absent MSBuild output-path redirection on pack invocations, not command-class staging bypass), different trigger (any no-feed run of the documented samples command, including the -lc flow #339 documents separately) - and this finding corrects #339's own parenthetical assertion that samples "internally redirects outputs", which holds for sample builds but not for the runner's own packing phase.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Run the samples/README.md primary command verbatim (tooling dev -lc 'pwsh -NoLogo -NoProfile -File ./scripts/Test-SharpProofSamples.ps1') in a fresh checkout.
2. After the run, inspect SharpProof.Package/obj, SharpProof.Analyzer/obj, SharpProof.CompilerCollector/obj, etc.: restore assets and build outputs exist there, produced during New-LocalPackageFeed, because no -p:Base*OutputPath property reaches the pack invocations (Test-SharpProofSamples.ps1 Lines 245-253).
3. Contrast the same script's sample builds: their objects live solely under the temp work/<run>/obj|bin (Lines 271-281), isolating the unredirected pack phase as the sole writer of source-tree output.
**Confidence**: Medium-High - every code/doc fact above was read and quoted directly; residual uncertainty is only whether "all obj, bin..." was intended to scope to sample builds rather than the whole runner, though the sentence explicitly includes the packing phase it contradicts.

### 390. [RESOLVED 386709833] `worker.response_too_large` Was Missing From the Run-Projection Vocabulary

**Status**: Resolved by mapping the worker's bounded-response fallback to `InfrastructureFailure`. The projection matrix now includes the emitted code and the focused protocol suite passes 9/9.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker\Program.cs` emits `worker.response_too_large` when response serialization exceeds the protocol limit; `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol\WorkerResultAssembler.cs` projects worker error codes into run status/failure pairs; `SharpProof.Worker.Test\ProtocolJsonTests.cs` pins known failure projections.
**Description**: The worker emitted a typed infrastructure fallback, but the protocol projection table returned no state for that code. Consequently `ValidateRun` rejected an otherwise well-formed fallback with `response.run_projection`, allowing the launcher to rewrite the useful cause as `MalformedResult`. Adding the missing infrastructure mapping preserves the intended fail-closed taxonomy.

### 391. [RESOLVED 7bc09dfa9] A Null Backend Task Escaped the Kernel's Typed Malformed-Result Path

**Status**: Resolved by checking the task returned from `ISmtBackend.CheckAsync` before awaiting it. The null-task regression and neighboring malformed-backend cases pass 3/3.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Verify\ProofKernel.cs` awaits the public backend task; `SharpProof.Verify.Test\ProofKernelTests.cs` contains the backend-shape regressions.
**Description**: A backend returning a null `Task<BackendCheckResult>` caused `await` to throw `NullReferenceException`, bypassing the existing typed malformed-result handling. The kernel now returns `Unknown(MalformedBackendResult)` for this shape without broadening the exception filter, so genuine backend exceptions retain their existing handling.

### 392. [RESOLVED cd811af72] `IrFactory.Cast` Minted Null-to-Nonnullable Terms That Replay as Concrete Invalid-Cast Faults

**Status**: Resolved by rejecting null operands when the target IR type is nonnullable. Existing reference-cast behavior remains unchanged, and the focused IR cast/interpreter suite passes 3/3.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Ir\IrFactory.cs` validates composite term construction; `SharpProof.Ir.Test\IrKernelTests.cs` covers reference casts and invalid construction.
**Description**: The factory already canonicalized null-to-nullable casts but allowed a null-to-boolean/integer cast to be interned. The interpreter then classified that ill-sorted term as a concrete `InvalidCast` exception while the SMT backend abstained, creating an avoidable oracle mismatch. The construction guard makes the malformed shape fail closed at the API boundary.

### 393. [RESOLVED 704db705b] Requires-Only Binding Silently Accepted Naked `Contract.Result` and `Contract.Old` Calls

**Status**: Resolved by treating an intrinsic violation with no enclosing contract clause as fatal during `BindRequires`, matching full binding. The existing standalone `Result` and `Old` regressions now exercise both binder entry points and pass 2/2.

**Location**: `SharpProof.Contracts\ContractBinder.cs` (`ValidateIntrinsics`), with coverage in `SharpProof.Contracts.Test\ContractBinderTests.cs` (`StandaloneIntrinsicsFailClosed`).
**Description**: `ContractIntrinsicValidator` correctly classified a body-level `Contract.Result<T>()` or `Contract.Old(...)` call as `ResultOutsideEnsures`/`OldOutsideEnsures`. Full `Bind` returned that typed failure, but `BindRequires` filtered violations to only those enclosed by a `Requires` clause, so the same method could return successful precondition bindings while the verifier rejected its contract source. The filter now also fails for violations with no enclosing clause; invalid intrinsics inside non-`Requires` clauses retain the existing requires-only scoping.

**Reproduction**:
1. Define a method containing `_ = Contract.Result<long>();` or `_ = Contract.Old(value);` with no surrounding clause.
2. Full binding returns the corresponding outside-`Ensures` failure.
3. Before `704db705b`, `BindRequires` returned success with no clauses; after the fix it returns the same typed failure.

The entries below were produced by a fourth read-only hunter wave run later on the same day (20 agents total: 19 read-only hunters over disjoint areas plus this consolidation writer; the clean areas returning NO-FINDINGS audits — dataflow internals, frontend scalar semantics, gates/performance — do not reproduce their ruled-out lists here). They are open findings against the current worktree at commit `11510c598` (the hunters' baseline; the consolidation writer re-verified every primary citation against the tree immediately before writing). Numbering continues the sequence above.

### 394. [RESOLVED 5828f0ece] Trusted-Spec Validation Admits Sequence-Typed Null Postconditions That Instantiation Can Never Materialize - the Typed Failure's Own Detail Text Demands a "Concrete Sequence Type Substitution" That No Code Path or API Can Provide

**Location**: `SharpProof.Specs\ApiSpecTermValidator.cs` (Lines 59-67 `SpecNullDeclaration` arm admitting any `IrTermServices.IsNullable` type; Lines 127-170 `ValidateBinary` totality fold marking non-arithmetic binaries total when both operands are); contradicting rejectors `SharpProof.Specs\ApiSpecInstantiation.cs` (Lines 172-184 standalone `Null(SpecNullDeclaration)` offering only String/Reference; Lines 248-267 peer-typed `Null(SpecNullDeclaration, TermResult)` whose Sequence rejection at Lines 256-260 fires BEFORE consulting `peer.Term.Type`, which already holds the concrete sequence type the message demands); upstream acceptance `SharpProof.Specs\ApiSpecTable.cs` (`CompileTemplate`, gate at Line 140 `if (!condition.IsTotal)`); enabling fact `SharpProof.Ir\IrOperatorCatalog.generated.cs` (Lines ~66-72: `IsNullable` includes `IrTypeKind.Sequence`); test gap `SharpProof.Specs.Test\ApiSpecInstantiationCoverageTests.cs` (Lines 244-265 pin only the literal-literal `null(Sequence) == null(Sequence)` shape; Lines 14-177 claim "every total expression shape" yet omit variable-vs-sequence-null).
**Description**: The project's two spec halves disagree about the trusted-spec expression language. The validator accepts `SpecNullDeclaration(IrTypeKind.Sequence)` as well-typed and total (`IsNullable(Sequence)` is true; `ValidateBinary` marks `Equal(resultVar(Sequence), null(Sequence))` total because neither operand is arithmetic), so `ApiSpecTable.Create` compiles the declaration into a template and mints its `ContentSha256` with zero authoring feedback - at exactly the layer designed to police spec-pack authoring. At instantiation time every path rejects the identical shape unconditionally: the standalone `Null` arm offers only `StringType`/`ObjectType`, and the peer-typed overload added by commit `e8172fb24` rejects `value.Type == IrTypeKind.Sequence` outright even though its `peer.Term.Type` parameter is precisely the concrete sequence type the accompanying Detail string demands ("A factory-independent sequence null needs a concrete sequence type substitution"). No type substitution mechanism exists anywhere - `InstantiatePostconditions` substitutions are `SpecVarId -> IrTerm` - so the message describes a remedy that cannot be expressed, let alone applied. Net effect: a pack author following the validator's rules produces a table that is born uninstantiable; the failure surfaces later at claim-preparation time as a typed but misattributed `UnsupportedValueType`. Fail direction is fail-closed (never a wrong verdict); the defect is the intra-project contract split plus factually wrong diagnostic text. Residual intent question stated honestly: the dedicated `UnsupportedValueType` kind and the pinned literal-literal fixture show the sequence exclusion itself is deliberate policy; what remains unpinned is the validator continuing to admit the shape as total and the peer path ignoring the available concrete type while its text claims otherwise. Distinct from #293 (a catalog UNDER-claim in `SharpProof.Frontend`; this is the inverse validator-OVER-claim polarity in `SharpProof.Specs` via a totality gate, plus a wrong-diagnostic component absent there), from #284/#285 (semantic-cache/Roslyn-outcome scans), from #151 (SMT encoding), and from the documentation-residue family #384/#340/#341 (both contradicted parties here are code layers in one assembly; no document cited). No existing entry mentions `SpecNullDeclaration`, `UnsupportedValueType`, or spec-null typing.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Build a declaration via the `CreateTemplate` helper shape of `ApiSpecInstantiationCoverageTests.cs` with `parameterTypes: [IrTypeKind.Sequence]`, `isStatic: true`, and postcondition `[Binary(Equal, Variable(Parameter, 0, IrTypeKind.Sequence), new SpecNullDeclaration(IrTypeKind.Sequence), IrTypeKind.Boolean)]`.
2. Call `ApiSpecTable.Create([declaration])`: succeeds - `Templates.Single()` produced, `ContentSha256` computed; the validator raised no objection.
3. Instantiate with `InstantiatePostconditions(template, factory, substitutions)` where the template parameter slot maps to `factory.Variable(...GetOrCreateSequenceType(factory.IntegerType))`: result is `Failed` with `SpecInstantiationFailureKind.UnsupportedValueType` and the "concrete sequence type substitution" detail - although the substitution dictionary is populated and the peer operand's type IS that concrete sequence type.
4. Vary substitutions arbitrarily (supply every template variable): the outcome never changes, proving step 2's acceptance was the defect.
5. Control: replace `new SpecNullDeclaration(IrTypeKind.Sequence)` with `IrTypeKind.String` in step 1 - instantiation succeeds with the exact peer type preserved (behavior pinned at ApiSpecInstantiationCoverageTests.cs Lines ~359-400), isolating the Sequence arm as the sole splitter.
**Confidence**: High on the behavioral split (all five artifacts quoted; step-3 failure regardless of substitutions follows from the unconditional Sequence arm at ApiSpecInstantiation.cs Lines 256-260). Medium overall on defect-vs-intent (stale message text and unpinned variable-vs-null shape argue unintended drift after `e8172fb24`).

**Status**: Resolved by accepting concrete peer sequence types when instantiating null postconditions while retaining rejection for factory-independent sequence nulls. The existing sequence/null instantiation regressions pass.

### 395. [RESOLVED 21096e612] Closed-Generic-Target `[ContractFor]` Companions Pass Validation With Zero Diagnostics Yet Can Never Bind - `ResolveCompanion` Compared the Companion's Construction-Layer Signatures Against the Definition-Layer `ConstructedFrom` Target

**Location**: `SharpProof.Contracts\ContractForSymbolMatcher.cs` (Lines 184-191 `ResolveCompanion`: `signatureTarget = companion.ContractTarget.IsOpen ? target.OriginalDefinition : target.ConstructedFrom` feeding `MemberSignaturesMatch(signatureTarget, candidate)`; comparison internals Lines 214-248/358-363/501-579 incl. the kind-mismatch fallback ~554-556; failure return Lines 199-203). Contrasting green layer: `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\ContractForValidation\ContractForValidationEngine.cs` (Lines 40-69 `Validate`) and `ContractForCompanionValidator.cs` (Lines 30-42: matches `GetOrdinaryMethods(companion.Target)` - the CONSTRUCTION-layer attribute target - against companion methods, with no `ConstructedFrom` hop).
**Description**: For a companion declared `[ContractFor(typeof(IFoo<Bar>))]` (a closed construction) whose members spell the exact construction-layer signature, the two shipped verdict layers disagree. Validation passes silently: `TryGetTarget` records the constructed symbol with `IsOpen=false` (Line 57), `TargetsType` compares it against the constructed containing type (Lines 61-67), `CompanionTypeMatches` demands only companion arity 0 for closed targets (Lines 90-93), and the validator's membership check runs entirely at construction layer so `MemberSignaturesMatch` matches 1:1 - zero SPCF0001-0008. Binding then fails for every member, always: `ResolveCompanion` sets `signatureTarget = target.ConstructedFrom`, which Roslyn maps to the corresponding member of the OPEN generic type whenever the containing type is constructed; no source-spelled companion can reference definition-layer type parameters, so `TypesMatch(T, Bar)` falls into the kind-mismatch fallback and `matches` is empty -> `CompanionSignatureMismatch`. Blast radius: every closed-generic-targeted companion is permanently dead while certified perfect; per `EffectiveContractSourceResolver.ResolveCore` the resolution ends `Failure = CompanionSignatureMismatch` with `HasSelectedContractIntent == true`, so strict/require-proven builds fail downstream and advisory builds lose the contracts, with no validating-side diagnostic ever fired. Intent evidence that the form should work: the overlap machinery treats distinct closed-generic companions as first-class live claims (`DistinctClosedGenericCompanionsDoNotOverlap`, `OpenAndClosedGenericCompanionsAreReportedAsOverlapping`, `ReferencedAndSourceDistinctClosedCompanionsDoNotOverlap`; `TargetsOverlap` Lines 69-78 special-cases exactly this), and no document restricts attribute targets to unbound generics (docs/public-api.md Lines ~31-35). Fail direction fail-closed; the defect is the guaranteed analyzer-blesses/binder-refuses split with zero surfacing. Distinct from #296 (unconstructed generic DEFINITION crashing `CreateTypeSpecializer` with a raw index fault - different component, trigger, and outcome class), #369 (collector MethodKind whitelist vs analyzer gate - different layer pair), #275/#167/#274/#234/#298/#384 (none touch the definition-vs-construction signature comparison), #204 (Property-target refusal WITH evidence; here precisely none).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Compile `public interface IFoo<T> { void M(T value); }` plus `[ContractFor(typeof(IFoo<int>))] public static class FooContracts { public static void M(IFoo<int> receiver, int value) { } }`.
2. Run the generator-test harness (`GeneratorTestHost.Run` driving `SharpProofAnalyzer`): assert `run.Diagnostics` is empty - validation reports nothing (construction-layer 1:1 match).
3. Resolve `IFoo<int>.M` from an invocation site and call `new ContractBinder(compilation, new IrFactory()).Bind(target)` (the `AssertBinds` seam of ConstructedGenericContractTests): result fails with `ContractBindingFailure.CompanionSignatureMismatch`.
4. Control A: change the attribute to `typeof(IFoo<>)` and make the companion generic - binding succeeds (pinned by `NamedTypeArgumentsAreRecursivelySpecialized`), isolating closed-construction targeting. Control B: note step 3's failure survives any rewording of companion signatures, since `target.ConstructedFrom` exposes only definition-layer `T`s no source can spell.
5. Grep `SharpProof.Contracts.Test`: the only closed-generic binder fixture is `ClosedNestedTargetRejectsOpenContainingCompanionLayer` (rejected earlier by `CompanionTypeMatches`), never the plain top-level shape of steps 1-3 - the gap is unpinned.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (every hop line-read; Roslyn `ConstructedFrom` mapping constructed-type members to their open definitions is standard documented behavior). Medium-High overall (residual uncertainty limited to whether silent-unsupported was intended, which the overlap tests and absent rejecting diagnostic argue against).
**Status**: Resolved by matching closed companions against the constructed target while retaining `ConstructedFrom` for generic methods whose containing type is not constructed. `ClosedGenericContractForTargetBindsItsConstructedSignature` covers the regression; the full Contracts suite passes 131/131.

### 396. Companion Discovery Force-Binds Custom Attributes for Every Type of Every Referenced Assembly With No Prefilter - Twice Per Compilation - Making `[ContractFor]` Bookkeeping an Unconditional Whole-Closure Cost Even When Zero Companions Exist

**Location**: enumeration `SharpProof.Frontend\ReferencedTypeSymbols.cs` (Lines 5-53 `GetAll`: current assembly global namespace plus every `compilation.SourceModule.ReferencedAssemblySymbols`, recursive over namespaces/nested types, no screening); consumer `SharpProof.Contracts\ContractForSymbolMatcher.cs` (Lines 110-151 `DiscoverCompanions`: `GetAttributes(type, contractFor)` per type, forcing early attribute binding per symbol); duplicated invocation sites `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core\ContractForValidation\ContractForValidationEngine.cs` (Line 49, inside `Validate` registered unconditionally at `SharpProofAnalyzerEngine.cs` Lines 58-59 before all activation early-returns) and `SharpProof.Contracts\EffectiveContractSourceResolver.cs` (Line 32, constructor reached via cached `ForCompilation`). Source-side contrast: `FindCandidates` syntax prefilter (`AttributeLists.Count != 0`, engine Lines ~89-93).
**Description**: Overlap detection requires the full set of `[ContractFor]`-attributed types across the entire reference closure, so each compilation-end pays one `GetAttributes()` call per named type in the compilation plus every referenced assembly (BCL/NuGet closure - tens of thousands of symbols), forcing early custom-attribute binding for all of them on the compiler thread. Nothing pre-filters assemblies that cannot contain the attribute (a cheap reference-identity check would skip most of the closure), cancellation exists only at loop top, and the same unfiltered enumeration is computed independently twice per compilation (validation engine inline; lazily again in `EffectiveContractSourceResolver`'s constructor). Because registration precedes activation early-returns, pure-advisory projects with zero contract usage pay the full cost. Cost scales with total referenced-type count rather than anything authored; the source-candidate side of the same feature does use a syntax prefilter, showing the metadata side simply lacks its equivalent. Same accepted perf-defect family as #320 (#O(closure) recomputation inside compilation-end, no memoization/prefilter) at a different site with a different primitive (whole-closure attribute binding instead of line-map digesting). Distinct from #320 (different component/primitive), #321 (storage cliff), #353/#215 (duplicate-reference/payload-hash trust decisions occurring after these gates).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Build a project referencing several large metadata sets under `SharpProofProfile=advisory` with zero `[ContractFor]` usages anywhere.
2. Instrument `ContractForSymbolMatcher.GetAttributes` (or breakpoint `DiscoverCompanions`): one hit per named type across the compilation and every referenced assembly - tens of thousands per compilation-end - repeated a second time if `EffectiveContractSourceResolver.ForCompilation` is constructed.
3. Compare against `FindCandidates`' `AttributeLists.Count != 0` prefilter for source trees: the asymmetry isolates the missing metadata-side screen.
4. Add a cheap guard (skip assemblies lacking the attributes assembly among references, or share one discovery result per compilation): the hit count collapses to near zero, demonstrating avoidability.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (enumeration breadth, absent prefilter, dual sites, unconditional registration all quoted verbatim); Medium on classification - bounded fail-safe performance cost offered in the spirit the ledger accepted #320.

### 397. [RESOLVED 81f5125bb] `IrFactory.Cast` Remained the Only Composite-Term Constructor With No Well-Sortedness Validation for Non-Null Operands After the #392 Null-Arm Fix - Ill-Sorted Cast Rows Minted Cleanly, Decoded Silently Through the Wire Codec, and Deferred Their Only Rejection to Per-Claim Replay Abstention

**Location**: `SharpProof.Ir\IrFactory.cs` (Lines 482-511 `Cast`: identity collapse, nullable-null collapse, the `cd811af72` null-to-nonnullable rejection at Lines 500-505, then unconditional interning for every remaining pair); validating siblings `Unary` Lines 389-416, `Binary` Lines 418-446, `Null` Lines 353-366, `Length` Lines 506-529, `SequenceAccess` Lines 531-551 (all throwing mint-time `ArgumentException` via catalog validation, pinned by `FactoryRejectsIllTypedTermsAtConstruction`, `SharpProof.Ir.Test\IrKernelTests.cs` Lines 272-311, which has no Cast arm beyond the new null case); wire reachability `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact\PortableIrGraphCodec.cs` (Line 532: `IrTermKind.Cast => factory.Cast(type(row.Type), term(row.A, depth + 1))` re-mints whatever row is on the wire through the unvalidated API); current interpreter posture `SharpProof.Ir\IrInterpreter.cs` (Lines 390-436 `EvaluateCast`: mismatched non-null pairs fall to `Unsupported(UnsupportedCast)` at Lines 434-435); backend mirror `SharpProof.Smt\IrSmtBackend.cs` (Lines 554-556: differing-type cast -> `UnsupportedIrEncodingException`).
**Description**: Every other composite-term factory API validates operand/result types against the operator catalog and throws `ArgumentException` at mint time, and the pinned suite treats that as the contract. `Cast` performs only its three special cases and otherwise interns ANY non-null type pair with no relation check - e.g., `factory.Cast(factory.BooleanType, factory.Integer(1))` mints without a diagnostic. Commit `cd811af72` (BUGS.md #392) closed only the null-to-non-nullable arm, which had been the one shape whose replay produced a concrete fabricated fault; the general sort-validation asymmetry survives. Consequences today are narrower than pre-fix but real: (a) the public factory API (the IVT/fuzz/wire population #300's own threat framing treats as in-scope) accepts ill-sorted casts where every sibling constructor rejects them, deferring detection from a typed construction-time failure to per-claim replay abstention; (b) `PortableIrGraphCodec` Line 532 faithfully reconstructs ill-sorted cast rows because the minting API raises no objection - a tampered/corrupt lowered artifact carries nonsense cast terms through decode silently, where every other ill-sorted row would fail decode loudly; (c) proof-path and replay-path now AGREE on abstention for these shapes (`UnsupportedCast` vs `UnsupportedIrEncodingException`), so - unlike the pre-fix state recorded by #392 - there is no oracle divergence or wrong-verdict capability; this entry is deliberately scoped to the surviving validation asymmetry and its silent-decode consequence. Distinct from #392 ([RESOLVED]: the null-arm concrete-fault divergence, fixed; fixing it did not touch the general relation check), from #222/#280/#291 (binary arithmetic arms), #360 ([RESOLVED] equality wrapper identity), #289 (guard naming conventions), #304/#266 (downstream catch masking - this supplies deferred rather than masked failures).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. In any host referencing SharpProof.Ir: `var f = new IrFactory(); var odd = f.Cast(f.BooleanType, f.Integer(1));` - no exception; contrast `f.Unary(IrUnaryOperator.Not, f.Integer(1))` which throws ArgumentException (pinned), isolating Cast as the sole unvalidated composite constructor for non-null operands.
2. Feed a lowered artifact whose term table carries the row (Cast, type=Boolean, operand=integer-row) through `PortableIrGraphCodec` decode: Line 532 re-mints it silently - no decode diagnostic - whereas corrupting any sibling-kind row throws during decode.
3. Evaluate the decoded term via `IrInterpreter.Evaluate`: status Unsupported (abstention), matching the SMT encode path's `UnsupportedIrEncodingException` - confirming the scope note that no verdict divergence remains, only the missing mint-time rejection and silent wire acceptance.
4. Add the missing catalog relation check to `Cast`: step 1 throws like its siblings and step 2's row fails decode, demonstrating the asymmetry is the sole enabler.
**Confidence**: High on mechanics (every site read in the worktree; sibling-validation contrast and codec pass-through mechanical). Severity explicitly Low-Medium: shipped lowering emits legal casts, impact concentrates on the embedding/tamper-detection surface.
**Status**: Resolved by rejecting non-reference-like source or target kinds at cast construction, preserving intentional reference casts. IR tests pass 108/108; the Portable IR codec suite passes 53/53 with an explicit malformed-cast regression.

### 398. [RESOLVED 81f5125bb] Identity-Keyed Type Creation Silently Interned a Discarded `displayName` on Cache Hits - Orphan Strings Accumulated

**Location**: `SharpProof.Ir\IrFactory.cs` - `GetOrCreateTypeCore` Lines 691-704 interned `name` before probing `_typeIds`, reached from `GetOrCreateReferenceType` and explicit-identity `GetOrCreateSequenceType`. Consumers include `SharpProof.Ir\IrPrinter.cs`, which renders the first stored name.
**Description**: `GetOrCreateReferenceType(identity, displayName)` makes `displayName` authoritative only on the first mint for an identity; later calls with a different name return the original type unchanged. Before the fix they still interned the discarded name into the string table as an unreachable entry, so repeated alias-style calls leaked strings for the factory lifetime. Identity-based type equality and first-name-wins rendering remain intentional; the defect was the avoidable orphan intern.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. `var f = new IrFactory(); var id = f.CreateIdentity(); var t1 = f.GetOrCreateReferenceType(id, "Widget"); var t2 = f.GetOrCreateReferenceType(id, "Gadget.Foo");`
2. Assert `t2 == t1` and `f.GetString(f.GetTypeInfo(t2).Name) == "Widget"` - "Gadget.Foo" appears nowhere, yet string-table enumerations show it permanently resident with no owning type.
3. Print any term casting/null-ing that type via `new IrPrinter(f)`: output shows "(Widget)" regardless of the most recent registration.
4. Loop step 1 with fresh names: `_strings` grows by one per iteration while `_types` stays constant, isolating the orphan-interning side effect.
**Confidence**: High on behavior (all sites quoted verbatim; deterministic). Low on impact by construction - classified as contract/rendering fidelity.
**Status**: Resolved by probing the type cache before interning a name. `TypeInterningDoesNotRetainDiscardedNames` covers reference and explicit-identity sequence types; the full IR suite passes 108/108.

### 399. [RESOLVED 78eb7217b] A Single Unreadable or Null-Text Additional File Aborts Whole-Manifest Collection as Opaque SP0049 - the One Capture Input Where "Cannot Read" Is Treated as Fatal Names Neither the File Nor the Cause

**Location**: `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\CompilerCompilationCapture.cs` (Lines 317-329 `CaptureAdditionalFile`, throw at Lines 322-323: `file.GetText(cancellationToken) ?? throw new InvalidOperationException("An additional file has no compiler text.")`; capture entry selecting every additional file at Lines 113-115); fan-in `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\FinalCompilationCollector.cs` (blanket catch Lines 41-51 converting everything to one SP0049 at Location.None carrying only `exception.GetType().Name + ": " + exception.Message`). Contrast: reference capture hashes PE bytes without text decoding (`Hash(stream)` Lines ~413-427).
**Description**: Roslyn's documented `AdditionalText.GetText` contract explicitly permits returning null (binary/undecodable content under some hosts), and host implementations may throw (`IOException` for a file deleted or locked between MSBuild enumeration and compilation end). Either path lands in the blanket catch, collapsing the entire manifest into one Error-severity SP0049 whose message is `"InvalidOperationException: An additional file has no compiler text."` (or the raw exception type) - never including the offending path. Verification for the whole project dies (fail-closed; no wrong verdict), including every unrelated callable's claims, because of one optional auxiliary input that could be handled gracefully one call away (skip the row, or hash raw bytes exactly as the sibling reference capture does). The asymmetry is internal evidence of intent: references are captured as opaque bytes, trees are required by definition, but additional files are optional - and the only optional input is fatal-with-no-attribution. Test suite never exercises the path (`FinalCompilationCollectorTests.cs` uses only `MemoryAdditionalText`, helper ~Lines 1137-1146, always returning content). Distinct from #321 (line-map payload size cliff), #383 (duplicate reference modules making IL-summary binding ambiguous), #232 (#line geometry), #323 (ordering gate), #353/#311 (analyzer-side reference issues): different site and trigger; no existing entry covers `CaptureAdditionalFile`.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. In a SharpProofVerify-enabled project add an `@(AdditionalFiles)` item whose host `AdditionalText.GetText()` returns null (contract-legal), or make an enumerated additional file unreadable/deleted after enumeration but before compilation-end.
2. Build in the canonical container: collection reaches `CaptureAdditionalFile`, throws, and the build fails with exactly one SP0049 whose text is `InvalidOperationException: An additional file has no compiler text.` (or `IOException: ...`) with no file path.
3. Remove only the offending file: the identical project emits its manifest and verifies green, isolating the additional-file read as the sole variable.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (throw site, blanket conversion, absent attribution, unpinned status all read directly); Medium-Low on day-to-day frequency (live trigger primarily the IO arm plus embedded/third-party hosts; shipped csc wrapper lossily decodes binaries rather than returning null).

### 400. [RESOLVED cf4ac95ea] Duplicate Additional-File Paths - Legal MSBuild Item State - Fail Sealed-Artifact Shape Validation and Kill Whole-Project Verification With the Fully Generic "The Compiler Manifest Artifact Is Invalid" SP0049

**Location**: rejection `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact\CompilationFingerprint.cs` (Lines 476-482 `ValidAdditionalFiles`: `values.Select(value => value.Path).Distinct(PathComparer).Count() == values.Length`); enforcement chain at emission `SharpProof.CompilerCollector\CompilerArtifact\CompilerManifestArtifactProducer.cs` (Line ~92 `Validate(artifact)` -> `CompilationFingerprint.ValidateShape`) and again `FinalCompilationCollector.cs` Line ~84 via `CompilerManifestArtifactJson.Serialize` (Validate ~Line 325); producer of rows `CompilerCompilationCapture.cs` (Line 113: `AdditionalFiles = [.. additionalFiles.Select(file => CaptureAdditionalFile(...))]` - no dedup).
**Description**: The sealed-artifact shape validator requires additional-file snapshot paths to be pairwise distinct, but nothing upstream deduplicates. `context.Options.AdditionalFiles` mirrors the compiler's `/additionalfile` list, and MSBuild preserves duplicate items across separate `Include`s (the standard reason the `Deduplicate` task exists): a user glob coexisting with a target-added explicit Include yields the same file twice with no MSBuild warning. Capture stores two snapshots with equal paths, and emission throws the generic invalid-artifact JsonException -> single opaque SP0049 naming neither the rule nor any file. Two properties make this a defect rather than policy: (a) unlike references (#383 territory), no consumer could meaningfully distinguish duplicates - they are pure auxiliary inputs where keeping the first occurrence loses nothing; (b) the same validator family produces targeted messages elsewhere (byte limit, non-canonical path), while this rule alone degrades to the catch-all message, so even forensic readers learn nothing. Fail-closed, but another deterministic whole-project verification kill from benign authoring state. Distinct from #383 (duplicate byte-identical REFERENCE modules aborting IL-summary authority binding via a singular-binding InvalidOperationException - different artifact kind, site, and exception), #321 (size budget), and #399 (same component but capture-time read failure with a typed operation-specific message vs validation-time canonicality failure with the fully generic one; different triggers and fixes).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Verified consumer project; add `<AdditionalFiles Include="*.json" />` and `<AdditionalFiles Include="config.json" />` with `config.json` present (MSBuild preserves both; confirm via `-bl` or item echo).
2. Build with SharpProofVerify enabled: collection captures two `CompilerAdditionalFileSnapshot` rows with identical normalized Path.
3. Observe the build failing with exactly one SP0049 whose message is the generic `JsonException: The compiler manifest artifact is invalid.` - no mention of additional files.
4. Remove the redundant Include (or Deduplicate): identical project builds green, isolating duplicate paths as the sole variable.
**Confidence**: Medium (rejecting predicate, dual enforcement, absent upstream dedup verified line-by-line; residual uncertainty is frequency of real-world duplicate `@(AdditionalFiles)` accumulation).

### 401. [RESOLVED 218106924] Goal-Fault Abstention Reason Was Misattributed for Precondition- and EffectContract-Diagnostic Goals - Any Exception-Status Goal That Was Not InternalConsistency Was Reported as PostconditionMayBeUndefined and Republished Verbatim

**Location**: `SharpProof.Verify\ProofKernel.cs` (Lines 92-97: goal-fault switch `query.Goal.Diagnostic == ProofDiagnosticKind.InternalConsistency ? InternalConsistencyMayBeUndefined : PostconditionMayBeUndefined` - a two-arm switch over a four-member enum); vocabulary `SharpProof.Verify\Evidence.cs` (Lines 13-19: `ProofDiagnosticKind` declares EffectContract, Precondition, Postcondition, InternalConsistency); publication table `SharpProof.Projection.catalog.json` (MapAbstention explicit case `PostconditionMayBeUndefined -> WorkerClaimReason.PostconditionMayBeUndefined`, consumed by `SharpProof.Worker\CallableVerifier.cs` and `CallableEntryFeasibility.cs`).
**Description**: Goals built with `ProofDiagnosticKind.Precondition` or `EffectContract` whose predicate raises an IR exception under the counterexample model receive `AbstentionReason.PostconditionMayBeUndefined` - a factually wrong label naming a postcondition when none exists in the query. The reason is public outcome metadata that `WorkerProjections.MapAbstention` republishes verbatim as `WorkerClaimReason.PostconditionMayBeUndefined`, so an embedder composing queries through the public `VerificationQuery`/`Goal` API gets misleading abstention diagnostics rather than a neutral fact. Shipped pipeline impact is nil today: both production goal-construction sites use only `Postcondition` (CallableVerifier) and constant-false `InternalConsistency` goals that cannot fault (CallableVerifier/CallableEntryFeasibility probes) - mislabeled metadata only, fully fail-closed. Distinctness: no BUGS.md entry touches AbstentionReason selection; #293 is the Frontend support catalog (different assembly/snapshot/axis); #300 is the exception-escape family; the nearest lineage is the commit that introduced the two reasons (separating undefined internal-consistency replay outcomes), of which this is the residue left for the other two enum members.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Recreate the fixture of `UndefinedPostconditionIsTypedSeparatelyFromReplayFailure` (`ProofKernelTests.cs` Lines ~188-210: divisor variable, goal predicate `(0/divisor) == 0`, model binding divisor=0) but construct the `Goal` with `ProofDiagnosticKind.Precondition`.
2. Run `await new ProofKernel(satisfiable-stub).VerifyAsync(query)`.
3. Observe `UnknownOutcome.Reason == AbstentionReason.PostconditionMayBeUndefined` although the query contains no postcondition - the label is fabricated by the else-arm.
4. Control: repeat with `InternalConsistency` - correctly typed `InternalConsistencyMayBeUndefined` (pinned at Lines ~212-243), isolating the two-arm dispatch.
**Confidence**: High on behavior (switch, enum membership, republication verified); Medium-Low overall - vocabulary reuse could be deliberate and shipped reachability is currently zero.
**Status**: Fixed by preserving the specialized Postcondition and InternalConsistency reasons and routing EffectContract/Precondition goal faults to the neutral CounterexampleReplayFailed reason. Focused and full Verify tests pass (17/17).

### 402. [RESOLVED 22712f87d] Four Malformed-Shape Arms of the Kernel's Outcome Union Have No Pinning Test - Single-Conjunct Regressions Keep SharpProof.Verify.Test Green While Public/IVT Constructors Reach Exactly Those Clauses

**Location**: guarded arms `SharpProof.Verify\ProofKernel.cs`: (a) Lines 47-52 UNSAT rejected when `result.Model != null || result.FailureReason != BackendFailureReason.None || result.UnsatCore.IsDefault`; (b) Line 54 core range check including the `index < 0` half; (c) Lines 67-72 SAT rejected when `!result.UnsatCore.IsDefaultOrEmpty`. **Description**: The closed outcome union's malformed-shape filters are multi-clause predicates, but the suite pins at most one clause per predicate. Concretely unverifiable regressions: deleting `index < 0` keeps every test green while `BackendCheckResult.Unsatisfiable([-1])` - constructible through the PUBLIC factory - then faults replay consumers with a raw index exception from `query.Assumptions[index]` instead of the typed `MalformedBackendResult`; deleting `result.Model != null`/`FailureReason != None` lets an UNSAT result carrying a model produce a ProvenOutcome; deleting `!UnsatCore.IsDefaultOrEmpty` lets a SAT result carrying a stale core enter replay. Arms requiring the internal StorageTag constructors are reachable exactly through the InternalsVisibleTo grants to `SharpProof.Fuzz`/`SharpProof.Worker`/`SharpProof.Smt.Test` - the same embedding population #300 names as this component's threat model, so the unpinned clauses guard reachable inputs, not impossible states. Suite-quality defect in the exact project whose pinned contrasts earlier resolutions rely on; current behavior correct, so severity is regression-net loss only. 3. Add a probe: `new ProofKernel(new StubBackend(BackendCheckResult.Unsatisfiable([-1]))).VerifyAsync(fixture.Query)` - returns UnknownOutcome(MalformedBackendResult) pre-edit; post-edit faults untyped, proving the clause was load-bearing.
