**Status**: Resolved by adding Verify regressions for negative unsat-core indices, UNSAT results carrying a model or failure reason, and SAT results carrying an unsat core. The kernel continues to return typed `MalformedBackendResult` outcomes for every shape.

### 403. [RESOLVED 616f9e619] Pre-Binding Worker Failure Responses Are Born Protocol-Binding-Invalid (Placeholder inputHash/Manifest/RequestHash/Budgets) - the Launcher Handshake Destroys Every Typed Early-Failure Verdict Into MalformedResult Without Any Tampering

**Status**: Resolved by recognizing the worker's tightly constrained, protocol-valid pre-manifest failure shape and rebinding it to the authoritative request, input, manifest, budgets, and versions before reporting. Unknown or malformed responses still use the existing fail-closed `worker.malformed_result` path. A package integration regression covers invalid-request, unavailable/invalid manifest, backend, infrastructure, cancellation, and timeout responses; the focused launcher suite passes 4/4.

**Location**: producers `SharpProof.Worker\SharpProofWorker.cs` (InvalidRequest arm Line 49 passing `string.Empty` inputHash + `new WorkerBudgets()` + no requestHash; `Failed` closure Lines 91-94 defaulting `inputHash = ""`; `Interrupted(input == null)` Lines 96-114 stamping `EmptyInputHash`/`EmptyManifest()`, reached from the OperationCanceledException arm at Line 404 when the manifest was not yet loaded); `SharpProof.Worker\Program.cs` (Lines 64-73 `worker.response_too_large` fallback, Lines 106-109 `request.malformed`, Lines 134-141 `worker.canceled`, Lines 147-152 crash arm - ALL via the `Failure` helper Lines 156-162, which passes `EmptyInputHash`/`EmptyManifest()` and never a requestHash); placeholder defaults `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol\WorkerResultAssembler.cs` (`RequestHash = requestHash ?? EmptyInputHash`, `EmptyManifest()`); enforcing checks `ProtocolJson.cs` (Lines 334-344 `response.request_mismatch`/`response.input_mismatch` - placeholders are format-valid SHA-256 strings so the checks fire; manifest-equality and budgets checks alongside); destructive consumer `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher\Program.cs` (rewrite-on-invalid Lines 188-203, contrast `WriteLauncherFailureAsync` binding the REAL hashes/manifest).
**Description**: The protocol's request-aware validator demands every response bind to its inputs: InputHash == expectedInputHash, Manifest payload-equal to the authoritative manifest, RequestHash == expectedRequestHash, Summary.Budgets equal to the request's. The worker satisfies this on every path past manifest loading (post-load Interrupted(snapshot), normal completion). But every legitimate failure path firing BEFORE binding stamps placeholders: worker-side InvalidRequest, compiler_manifest.unavailable/invalid, input.unavailable, pre-manifest Canceled/TimedOut, and all Program.Main-level responses (request.malformed, backend.unavailable, worker.infrastructure, worker.canceled, worker.response_too_large). When such a well-formed typed response reaches the launcher (strict validation always runs when a result file exists), it accumulates `response.input_mismatch` + `response.manifest_mismatch` + `response.request_mismatch` (+ `response.budgets_mismatch` when the request carried non-default budgets), IsValid flips false, and the rewrite converts the true verdict - InvalidRequest, InputUnavailable, CompilerManifestMismatch, genuine cancel/timeout during load, InfrastructureFailure - into Failed/MalformedResult/worker.malformed_result, persisted and republished; stderr transiently shows the true codes while the artifacts contradict them. Observable damage: the entire early-failure taxonomy is unreachable in published evidence even under completely honest, untampered conditions. Fail direction closed but attribution destroyed wholesale. Distinct from #306 (the rewrite HANDLER's intrinsic destruction/conflation/duplication properties for arbitrary external triggers such as tampering or skew - this is the systematic non-tamper PRODUCER defect guaranteeing routing into it), #381 (launcher-SYNTHESIZED no-result artifact failing run_projection - different producer and violated check), #301 [RESOLVED] (premature Hit AFTER snapshot load producing cache_request_mismatch), #179 (cause loss when NO result exists), #309/#310/#385 (compaction/size mechanisms).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Statically: reproduce the pinned fixture of InvalidRequestStillProducesAWellFormedFailedResponse (valid request with QueryRlimit=0): VerifyAsync returns Failed/InvalidRequest with Errors=["budgets.rlimit"], InputHash=EmptySha256, Manifest=EmptyManifest().
2. Take a VALID sibling request, seal its manifest, and call public `WorkerProtocolJson.ValidateForRequest(step1Response, ComputeRequestHash(good), inputHash, sealedManifest, good, versions)` - exactly what ValidateAndReport executes: IsValid == false with response.request_mismatch + response.input_mismatch + response.manifest_mismatch (+ budgets_mismatch), although plain Validate(step1Response).IsValid is true (the pinned assertion).
3. Dynamically: run the launcher with publication enabled and truncate/rename the --compiler-manifest file in the window between staging and worker load: the worker exits 0 having written Failed/InputUnavailable with placeholder bindings; stderr shows the typed code followed by the mismatch lines, then the rewrite; the persisted/published result.json says MalformedResult and exit becomes 3.
4. Contrast: force cancellation AFTER manifest load (large manifest, immediate cancel): Interrupted(snapshot) binds real hashes/manifest and survives ValidateForRequest - isolating pre-binding paths as the defect.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (producer sites, validator clauses, rewrite consumer read line-by-line; launcher always strict-validates existing results). Residual uncertainty limited to practical frequency of timing-dependent triggers; the sharpest user-visible delta is artifact content/attribution rather than exit status for most subclasses.

### 404. [RESOLVED 8c10d3294] A Fully-Refuted Complete Run Whose Cache ADMISSION Fails Keeps CacheStatus = Miss, Which the Request-Aware Validator Declares Protocol-Invalid - Green Refutation Verdicts Are Rewritten to MalformedResult Whenever Admission Replay Diverges

**Location**: producer gate `SharpProof.Worker\VerificationCache.cs` (Lines 535-563 `IsCacheable`: storable shape PLUS `WorkerProtocolJson.Validate(response, expectedInputHash, expectedManifest).IsValid` PLUS `ReplayCachedClaims` Lines 565-612 re-executing every refuted claim via `TryCreateModel` (interval/model-shape constraints invisible on the wire), `EntryAssumptionsHold`, and `CallableCounterexampleReplayer.Replay`); stamping flow `SharpProof.Worker\SharpProofWorker.cs` (initial Miss at creation; Lines 369-400: only `Written`/`Unavailable` replace it when the IsCacheable block is entered - a false return skips the block leaving Miss); enforcing rule `ProtocolJson.cs` (Lines 430-459 `ValidateCacheForRequest`: `storableShape` conjunction and `WorkerCacheStatus.Miss => !storableShape` at Line 453, error `response.cache_request_mismatch`); worker-side blindness (Lines 380-385: cache rule runs only when `expectedRequest != null`, i.e., launcher-tier validation only).
**Description**: The request-aware validator encodes the biconditional "storable shape iff not Miss": a Complete, error-free, all-callables-Complete, all-claims-Refuted, all-postcondition-manifest response may carry Hit/Written/Rejected/Unavailable but NEVER Miss. The producer, however, stamps Written only if IsCacheable holds, and IsCacheable is strictly stronger than storableShape: it additionally re-executes each refuted claim and rebuilds models under wire-invisible constraints. Whenever any extra conjunct fails on an otherwise storable-shaped run - replay divergence, an absorbed CounterexampleReplayFailed-class fault (#304's arms), an interval-constrained variable whose model value fails TryCreateValue, an assumption-evaluation flip - control skips the write block and the response ships with Miss in exactly the shape the validator forbids. The launcher adds `response.cache_request_mismatch`, IsValid falls, and the rewrite converts the product's core scenario - an all-refuted verdict under advisory (cache enabled by default) - into Failed/MalformedResult. The predicates are maintained by hand in two assemblies with nothing tying them together, and the validator cannot in principle distinguish "Miss because genuinely not storable" from "Miss because admission replay failed". Distinct from #301 [RESOLVED] (opposite corner: Hit stamped when nothing served - Hit => storableShape violated; different status, decision point, and trigger), #226 (stale Unavailable diagnostic drift), #302/#177 (lock contention classified Unavailable; here healthy cache classified Miss), #309 (compaction Used bits). No ledger entry audits the Miss arm.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Static: build via `WorkerResultAssembler.Create(inputHash, sealedAllPostconditionManifest, Complete, None, allCompleteCallables, allRefutedClaims, budgets, WorkerCacheStatus.Miss, elapsed, requestHash, versions)` with consistent summary counts - byte-equivalent to the worker's IsCacheable-false output.
2. Call public ValidateForRequest with a cache-enabled advisory request: the errors contain `response.cache_request_mismatch` (Line 453 arm) although every non-cache invariant holds.
3. Show the producer reaches that state: in-process, make `CallableCounterexampleReplayer.Replay` (or EntryAssumptionsHold) return a non-None reason/false for one claim on the SECOND execution of an otherwise deterministic all-refuted run - IsCacheable goes false, Lines 369-400 skipped, assembled response keeps Miss while worker self-validation passes (internal overload omits the cache rule).
4. End-to-end: hand the bytes to the launcher path with matching authority: stderr shows response.cache_request_mismatch, the run is rewritten to worker.malformed_result, and the published artifact loses the all-refuted verdict.
**Confidence**: Medium (state combination, both predicate texts, and the self-validation blind spot verified line-by-line; residual uncertainty is frequency of admission-replay divergence given claims were already replayed once deterministically - interval/model-shape arms and #304-absorbed faults are the realistic sources).

### 405. [RESOLVED 59f57c4dc] Cache-Gate Assumption Evaluation Is the Worker's Only Unprotected Interpreter Call Site - Read-Side Faults Destroy Payload-Verified Entries as "Corrupt", Write-Side Faults Discard an Already-Validated Verdict Into Run-Wide InfrastructureFailure

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker\VerificationCache.cs` (Lines 691-714 `EntryAssumptionsHold`: bare `interpreter.Evaluate` at Lines 702-705, no try/catch of any kind; consumed by `ReplayCachedClaims` Line 600 and therefore `IsCacheable` Line 562; read-side deletion arms Lines 96-107 with catch filters Lines 116-128 - IOException/UnauthorizedAccessException, then ArgumentException/JsonException/InvalidDataException, NOT InvalidOperationException; `TryDeleteCorruptEntry` Lines ~478-490); write-side consumer `SharpProof.Worker\SharpProofWorker.cs` (Lines 369-374 `IsCacheable(...)` inside the try whose only catch is OperationCanceledException at Line 404); terminal conversion `SharpProof.Worker\Program.cs` (Lines 143-153 broad catch -> Failed/InfrastructureFailure/worker.infrastructure); contrasting protected siblings `CallableCounterexampleReplayer.cs` (symmetric ArgumentException AND InvalidOperationException arms around its own Evaluate) and `CallableVerificationPolicy.cs` (per-callable catch-all).
**Description**: `IrInterpreter.Evaluate` is called from exactly two places in SharpProof.Worker (grep-verified): inside the replayer, wrapped by blanket arms, and in `EntryAssumptionsHold` - which concretely interprets every Requires/Assume clause term of the CURRENT artifact under a model, i.e., the pipeline's only evaluation point for exactly the term shapes #266/#304 establish can fault (ill-formed UTF-16 payloads -> ArgumentException; IR value/factory state-machine violations -> InvalidOperationException). Two consequences, both mismatched to the fault's source: (a) READ PATH - an ArgumentException escaping through IsCacheable is caught by TryReadAsync's filter and routed to RestoreStaged + TryDeleteCorruptEntry: an entry that already passed schema-version, filename-hash, and SHA-256 PayloadHash verification is deleted because interpreting the CURRENT artifact's clauses faulted - healthy-entry destruction masquerading as corruption; worse, the adjacent filter omits InvalidOperationException, which escapes TryReadAsync entirely into Program.cs's generic handler - internally inconsistent with the file-adjacent replayer treating both classes symmetrically. (b) WRITE PATH - after a run has solved everything and passed protocol self-validation, `IsCacheable` re-executes the complete replay machinery including the bare Evaluate, with zero boundary until Program.cs replaces the ENTIRE response with Failed/InfrastructureFailure: one data-dependent interpreter fault on one clause destroys a fully computed verdict, with nothing attributing the cache gate as origin. Fail direction closed, maximum blast radius. Distinct from #304 (the REPLAYER'S internal blanket arms relabeling faults as CounterexampleReplayFailed, including their consumption at the Replayer.Replay call - it does not cover EntryAssumptionsHold, which never routes through the replayer, nor the write-site consequence; compositional: removing #304's InvalidOperationException arm pushes those exceptions into exactly the unprotected path documented here), #266 (the replayer's ArgumentException arm and its payload throw-source - different Evaluate site and consequences), #302/#177 (locking), #220/#134/#273 (staging/durability).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Static topology: grep SharpProof.Worker for `.Evaluate(` - exactly two hits, only the replayer's inside a try; VerifyAsync's working try catches only OperationCanceledException.
2. Inject a temporary fault into the worker test harness's interpreter - e.g., make `IrFactory.GetString` throw ArgumentException("Strings require well-formed UTF-16.") unconditionally, or wrap EvaluateBounded to throw InvalidOperationException once for any IrStringTerm.
3. Read-side variant: seed a valid replayable cache entry, rerun with the fault active: TryReadAsync reaches EntryAssumptionsHold; the ArgumentException variant deletes the payload-hash-verified file (cache directory empties) and reports a plain miss; the InvalidOperationException variant escapes TryReadAsync altogether.
4. Write-side variant: run a fresh all-refuted verification to completion with the fault active: the response passes Validate but IsCacheable throws; observe runStatus failed / infrastructureFailure / worker.infrastructure with NO cache write, although the identical unfaulted run completes Written.
5. Contrast control: apply the identical injection so it fires only inside the replayer's guarded region: the run stays Complete with the affected claim Unknown/CounterexampleReplayFailed - isolating EntryAssumptionsHold's missing boundary.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism and boundary topology (asymmetry against the replayer's own arms, VerifyTargetAsync's catch-all, and TryReadAsync's filters dispositive). Medium overall on present-day reachability (requires a fault class the ledger accepts as real but latent for this call shape).

### 406. The Worker's `--request`/`--result` Distinctness Guard Is Ordinal-String-Only - Symlink/Hardlink Aliases Bypass It While the Launcher's Own Path-Conflict Machinery Classifies the Identical Layout as a Conflict

**Location**: `SharpProof.Worker\Program.cs` (Lines 217-250 `TryParseArguments`: GetFullPath normalization at Lines 239-240; sole alias guard `!string.Equals(request, result, StringComparison.Ordinal)` at Line 249); pinning test `SharpProof.Worker.Test\WorkerProgramTests.cs` Lines 34-67 (`DirectInvocationRejectsRequestResultAliasBeforeStartBarrier` - identical STRING only); in-repo standard for the same decision `SharpProof.Host\LinuxPathIdentity.cs` (Line 650 `AreSameExistingFile` via stat device+inode, consumed by `PathsConflict` at Line 681) used by the launcher's ValidateDistinctPaths.
**Description**: The worker validates that --request and --result differ using only an ordinal comparison of two GetFullPath outputs. GetFullPath resolves dot segments and anchors but never symlink or link identity, so `/dir/request.json` and a hardlink/symlink `/dir/result.json` targeting it pass the guard while denoting one filesystem object - precisely the layout the launcher classifies as conflicting via the stat fallback, proving the project's own standard treats lexical equality as insufficient. The pinned regression cements only the exact-string case, so the bypass is unpinned. Consequences for direct invokers (usage documents direct invocation): the handshake proceeds past the intended barrier and `AtomicFile.WriteUtf8Async` renames staged bytes over the aliased name - through a symlink POSIX rename replaces the link entry with a regular file; across a hardlink the result name is re-pointed while the sibling retains the old inode. No verdict corruption and no cross-run damage in the launcher flow (launcher stages private files and screens aliases itself); the damage is that the documented hygiene invariant degrades to best-effort lexical matching at the process boundary where it is the ONLY defense. Distinct from #364 (non-regular file TYPES accepted on the private-path pipeline and clobbered - here both paths are ordinary regular files and the defect is missing inode-identity alias detection in argv parsing), #366 (Canonicalize returning GetFullPath verbatim splitting PUBLICATION marker ownership - no markers/publication machinery involved here), #308 (relative cache anchoring).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. In the canonical container create `/tmp/h11/a.json` and `ln -s a.json /tmp/h11/b.json` (or `ln` for a hardlink).
2. Invoke the worker directly with `verify --request /tmp/h11/a.json --result /tmp/h11/b.json --start-stdin --parent-pid $$`, stdin held open.
3. Observe the process proceeds past argument validation (reaching start-barrier behavior) instead of returning usage exit 2; contrast the SAME string for both flags, which returns 2 immediately (pinned test).
4. Static confirmation: `Path.GetFullPath` outputs differ while `stat -c '%d:%i'` shows equal device/inode - exactly the pair `AreSameExistingFile` treats as conflicting.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (guard composition, pin scope, launcher contrast read line-by-line); Low-Medium significance (direct invocation with aliased spellings required; worst outcome silent transformation of the aliased name).

### 407. Z3 Import Resolver Is Registered Before the Verified Native Handle Is Published - a Concurrent First P/Invoke Reads a Zero Handle and Falls Through to Ambient Default Probing That Never Consults the Container-Validated Payload

**Location**: `SharpProof.Host\ContainerNativeLibrary.cs` (Lines 13-48 `InstallZ3ResolverRequired`; decisive ordering Lines 28-36: `NativeLibrary.Load` -> `SetDllImportResolver(ResolveZ3Import)` at Line 32 -> `Volatile.Write(ref _z3Handle, handle)` at Line 36; resolver body Lines 50-66 returning `Volatile.Read(ref _z3Handle)` at Line 65). Installers: `SharpProof.Worker\SharpProofWorker.cs` Line 34, `Tools\SharpProof.Fuzz` fuzzing setups, both ContainerNativeLibrarySetup test helpers.
**Description**: The resolver is registered BEFORE the loaded handle is published. During that window `ResolveZ3Import` reads `_z3Handle == IntPtr.Zero` and returns it; per the DllImportResolver contract a Zero return means "callback did not resolve", so the runtime falls back to DEFAULT probing for "libz3" - the runtime-closure directory (which deliberately lacks libz3.so; the payload lives under the versioned native tree), then system directories and anything on the inherited, unscrubbed LD_LIBRARY_PATH. Two outcomes, both defeating the resolver's purpose: (a) `DllNotFoundException("libz3")` from a perfectly healthy container - absorbed by IsBackendUnavailable into a typed backend-unavailable verdict, i.e., spurious infrastructure attribution caused by the resolver's own ordering; or (b) silent mapping of an ambient, hash-unverified libz3 if one is probeable. The lock serializes installers but does nothing about this intra-install ordering versus a concurrent first P/Invoke from another thread. Blast radius: embedding/fuzz hosts issuing their first Z3 P/Invoke concurrently with install (shipped worker installs single-threaded before any query); fail direction mixed - usually fail-closed (a), worst-case trust-boundary bypass (b). Distinct from #271 (deferred security: STATIC identity gap - contract validates bytes separately from the library later loaded, i.e., WHICH bytes bind even when everything is ordered; here the internal ordering means resolution bypasses the validated payload entirely because no pinned handle exists yet to fall back FROM; fixing either does not fix the other). No other entry touches `ContainerNativeLibrary` resolution logic (#374/#380 are wrapper lifecycles).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Confirm ordering: Line 32 registers ResolveZ3Import; only Line 36 publishes _z3Handle; Line 65 returns Volatile.Read verbatim.
2. Contract check: a DllImportResolver returning IntPtr.Zero signals "not resolved", after which the runtime performs default probing for the literal name "libz3".
3. Dynamic: start thread B looping on `new Microsoft.Z3.Context()` BEFORE thread A calls InstallZ3ResolverRequired; place a stub libz3.so on LD_LIBRARY_PATH (inherited by spawned workers unscrubbed).
4. Observe intermittently either DllNotFoundException/typed backend-unavailable despite a valid contract+payload, or the ambient stub mapped (/proc/<pid>/maps shows the LD_LIBRARY_PATH copy); reordering Lines 32-36 (publish handle first) eliminates both.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (ordering, Zero-return fallback semantics, environment inheritance verified line-by-line); Low on present-day reachability (concurrent-warmup embedders only).

### 408. [RESOLVED 344e6b440] DeleteIfUnprotected Is the Sole "Must Be Regular Files" Site Throwing InvalidOperationException - It Escapes the Launcher's Continue-On-Failure Collection Filter, Aborting Mid-Sweep and Masking the Original Publication Failure Inside an AggregateException

**Location**: throw sites `SharpProof.Host\LinuxPathIdentity.cs` Lines 684-714 `DeleteIfUnprotected` (non-regular output Lines 695-699 and protected-alias Lines 708-709, both InvalidOperationException); sibling convention (IOException for the identical condition class): Lines 340-344, 372-377, 416-421, 494-497/519-523/526-531, 729-736, 1219-1223 ("publication members must be regular files"); sole production consumer `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher\Program.cs` Lines 802-831 `InvalidatePublication` (continue-on-failure sweep with `failure ??= exception`; per-member catch filter Lines 819-821 admitting exactly IOException/UnauthorizedAccessException/ArgumentException/Win32Exception); rollback wrapper Lines 662-680 wrapping into `AggregateException("SharpProof publication rollback failed.", ...)`. Type pinned by `InvalidAndNonRegularPublicationPathsFailClosed` (`LinuxPublicationSetTests.cs` Lines ~172-177 asserting InvalidOperationException).
**Description**: Every other publication-integrity check in LinuxPathIdentity reports a non-regular member via IOException; DeleteIfUnprotected alone uses InvalidOperationException for the same condition class. That divergence is not cosmetic because the API's one production caller implements a deliberate continue-on-failure sweep: iterate members, collect the FIRST failure, delete each remaining member and marker, throw once at the end - designed so one bad member does not prevent cleanup of the rest. Because InvalidOperationException matches none of the filter arms, the first non-regular member (e.g., a publication destination swapped for a directory mid-window) aborts the foreach immediately, leaving the REMAINING members' files and `.set` markers undeleted - a half-invalidated publication state the sweep was written to avoid - and the escaped exception later surfaces through TryRollbackPublication's AggregateException, burying the original publication failure beneath a secondary rollback diagnosis. Fail direction remains loud/red (no wrong-verdict capability), but cleanup completeness and attribution degrade exactly in the already-failing path where diagnosis matters most. Either the Host method should emit the sibling IOException or the launcher filter should admit InvalidOperationException; today the two halves disagree. Distinct from #364 (private I/O paths ACCEPTING non-regular files - opposite polarity at Canonicalize/staging, citing EnsureRegularPublicationPath as contrast; here the rejection exists but surfaces with a type defeating the consumer's design), #273 (durability of deletion - orthogonal), #306/#381 (launcher rewrite/projection tables). No entry touches DeleteIfUnprotected or InvalidatePublication's filter contents.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Static: compare LinuxPathIdentity.cs Lines 697/708 (InvalidOperationException) against Lines 342-343/526-531 (same message class, IOException); confirm Program.cs Lines 819-821 omit InvalidOperationException.
2. Unit-level pin: `Assert.Throws<InvalidOperationException>(() => LinuxPathIdentity.DeleteIfUnprotected(dir, []))` (already pinned) - a directory-shaped member throws the unfiltered type.
3. Integration shape: drive a publish whose rollback path triggers while one member.Path has been replaced by a directory: InvalidatePublication aborts at that member (later members' files and markers persist) and the surfaced error is AggregateException "SharpProof publication rollback failed." with the original exception demoted to an inner element.
4. Control: change the two throws to IOException (mirroring siblings): the same scenario sweeps every member and propagates the original publication exception unwrapped - isolating the taxonomy mismatch.
**Confidence**: Medium-High on mechanism (throw types, filter contents, loop-abort property, pinning test verified verbatim); Low-Medium on frequency (member changing kind mid-publication-window - rare but within the threat framing #242/#272 established).

**Status**: Resolved by using `IOException` for both regular-file and protected-alias rejections, so launcher invalidation continues its cleanup sweep; the Linux publication tests pass 35/35.

### 409. SPMETA010 Can Never Fire on the Only Production Semantic-Cache Write - Its Answer Grammar Recognizes a Fictional Vocabulary and Its Default Arm Flags Exactly the Answers the Domain Declares Cacheable

**Location**: `SharpProof.Meta.Analyzers\CacheSoundnessRules.cs` (Lines 385-394 `IsSemanticAnswerType`: namespace-root "SharpProof" AND name contains "Answer"/"Result"/"Outcome"; Lines 186-202 all recognition arms bottoming out in it; default arm Lines 200-201 flagging ANY non-constant value of such a type); sole production write `SharpProof.Worker\SharpProofWorker.cs` Lines 376-377 `cache.TryWriteAsync(response, snapshot.InputHash, manifest, projectBoundary.Token)` with `response` typed `WorkerVerifyResponse`; production vocabulary `WorkerRunStatus`/`WorkerRunFailureReason`/`WorkerClaimReason`/`WorkerCacheStatus` (no substring match); domain policy contradiction `SharpProof.Verify\Outcomes.cs` Lines 46-53 (`OutcomeCachePolicy.IsCacheable` declaring ProvenOutcome/RefutedOutcome THE cacheable answers). No member of the production answer vocabulary contains "Answer"/"Result"/"Outcome", so every arm (enum-member gated on the enum TYPE passing, object-creation, invocation-name, default) returns false for `WorkerVerifyResponse` and friends: the product's single semantic-cache write site can NEVER produce SPMETA010 even if the response were provably a canceled/malformed shape constructed inline. The rule fires only on synthetic shapes - the test suite calibrates it with a fictional enum whose NAME satisfies the substring grammar, so the regression net structurally cannot detect the mismatch (pinning-cements-wrong-calibration pattern accepted as #293). Polarity is also inverted relative to the domain: the default arm flags any non-constant reference typed *Outcome/*Result/*Answer in the SharpProof namespace, so `void N(ProvenOutcome p, ProofCache c) { c.Add("k", p); }` reports Error - flagging exactly the answer OutcomeCachePolicy.IsCacheable declares cacheable. Distinctness stated explicitly per vetting: #186 covered cache IDENTIFICATION via the ISemanticCache marker interface independent of names; #284 covered local/field/compound ALIAS tracking once a write is recognized - both present and pinned; neither touches the VALUE-side grammar (`IsSemanticAnswerType`'s substring test), the WorkerVerifyResponse blind spot, or the cacheability polarity inversion. Not #352 (thread-safety), not #301/#302/#226 (runtime cache behavior).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. In a Meta-Analyzers-attached project declare the marker interface, a `WorkerVerifyResponse`-shaped class and `WorkerRunStatus`-shaped enum under SharpProof-rooted namespaces, a `ProofCache : ISemanticCache` with `TryWriteAsync`, and analyze `void M(ProofCache c) => c.TryWriteAsync(new WorkerVerifyResponse(), "h");` - zero SPMETA010 regardless of any status facts.
2. Rename ONLY the parameter type to a synthetic `TimedOutAnswer`: SPMETA010 fires (object-creation arm) - isolating the type-name grammar as the discriminator.
3. Confirm the production path: grep `TryWriteAsync(` - the only ISemanticCache-typed call site is SharpProofWorker.cs Lines 376-377 with argument types none of which pass IsSemanticAnswerType.
4. Polarity control: analyze `void N(ProvenOutcome p, ProofCache c) => c.Add("k", p);` - SPMETA010 fires, contradicting OutcomeCachePolicy.IsCacheable(ProvenOutcome) == true.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (grammar evaluated symbol-by-symbol against real type names; sole-write-site claim grep-established). Impact framed honestly as latent defense-layer loss.

### 410. SPMETA010 Treats Factory-Delegate Arguments as Opaque Values - `GetOrAdd`/`AddOrUpdate`/`TryUpdate` Lambda Writes Escape While Their Inline Equivalents Are Flagged

**Location**: `SharpProof.Meta.Analyzers\CacheSoundnessRules.cs` (WriteMethods Lines 11-13 explicitly listing AddOrUpdate/GetOrAdd/TryUpdate; argument scan Lines 24-26 evaluating `IsNonCacheableSemanticAnswer(argument.Value, ...)` directly; default arm Lines 200-201 reached for `IAnonymousFunctionOperation` whose delegate TYPE fails `IsSemanticAnswerType`; no lambda-body descent anywhere - inner operations are visited only by unrelated object-creation/binary handlers, and the invocation handler requires a cache-typed instance).
**Description**: `WriteMethods` anticipates ConcurrentDictionary-style factory APIs, but AnalyzeWrite evaluates each ARGUMENT opaquely. A lambda argument arrives as IAnonymousFunctionOperation typed e.g. `Func<string, Answer>` - System namespace, no Answer/Result/Outcome substring - so the default arm returns false and the lambda BODY (which contains the very `Answer.Unknown` field reference or answer-typed object creation every non-delegate arm flags) is never examined. Thus `cache.GetOrAdd("key", static _ => Answer.Unknown)` - the canonical cache-population idiom whose whole purpose is producing and storing an answer - writes a non-cacheable answer silently, while the semantically identical `var a = Answer.Unknown; cache.Add("key", a)` (pinned alias case) and direct `cache.Add("key", Answer.Unknown)` report Error. Invocations inside the lambda (`_ => Bad()`) also escape: the invocation handler checks only whether the RECEIVER is cache-typed. The test suite covers aliases, branches, indexers, coalesce, compound, deconstruction, field targets, loops, and marker requirements but contains ZERO delegate/factory fixtures, so a future refactor moving inline writes behind factories is undetectable. Latent today (grep: all in-tree GetOrAdd/AddOrUpdate sites are ConcurrentDictionary memoizers, none on an ISemanticCache implementation). Distinct from #186/#284 (alias tracking - delegates never mentioned), from resolved #326 (SPMETA009's registration gap - same CLASS, different rule/detector/operation kind: this is the argument-traversal hole inside AnalyzeWrite, not missing operation routing), and from #409 (that finding concerns the vocabulary grammar; this survives even with a corrected grammar - a lambda returning a perfectly-named `Answer.Unknown` still escapes at the delegate boundary; disjoint mechanisms, both stand).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Using the test fixture vocabulary (`enum Answer { Unknown }`, `ProofCache : ISemanticCache` with `Add(string, Answer)` and `Answer GetOrAdd(string, Func<string, Answer>)`), analyze `void M(ProofCache c) { c.Add("k", Answer.Unknown); c.GetOrAdd("k", static _ => Answer.Unknown); }`.
2. Observe exactly ONE diagnostic (at Add); the GetOrAdd factory storing the identical value produces none.
3. Change the factory body to `static _ => Answer.Proven` and back - no diagnostic either way, proving the body is unexamined rather than judged safe.
4. Move the poison behind a local function (`Answer Bad() => Answer.Unknown; c.GetOrAdd("k", _ => Bad())`) - still silent.
5. Test-gap proof: no SPMETA010 fixture anywhere supplies a lambda-valued cache argument.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (arm-by-arm trace certain; WriteMethods membership proves anticipation); impact latent until a factory-style cache API is used, stated plainly.

### 411. SPMETA004 Polices Only Equality-Spelled `ir.`-Prefixed Literals Though Its Message Bans "Reason or Provenance Literals" Controlling Behavior Generally - StartsWith/Compare/Prefix Tables and Non-Constant Hosts All Escape While the Pipeline It Guards Operates on a Different Vocabulary

### 412. SharpProof.Gates Was Enrolled Into the RS0030-as-Error Banned-API Regime Over Three Live Compilation-Mutation Call Sites - the Release-Certifying Project Cannot Compile Under the Repository's Own Mandatory pr-gates Build, and No Architecture Test Scans Mutation-API Usage

Each site therefore produces error-promoted RS0030, `dotnet build SharpProof.sln -c Release` fails inside SharpProof.Gates, and consequently the mandatory pr-gates command (and Invoke-SharpProofGateEvidence.ps1's Rebuild of Gates) cannot pass; no pragma, NoWarn, suppression file, or editorconfig carve-out exempts them (verified: the repo-wide NoWarn lists contain only CA/RS2008-family codes; Gates.csproj sets TWAE=true itself). The architecture suite provides no backstop: its inventories pin the FILE's contents, and its usage scans cover display/speculative/semantic-model families, not mutation APIs. Distinct from #324 (APIs MISSING from the ban lists - here they ARE listed and used live), #387 (GetSemanticModel overload coverage inside the same file - different section/mechanism), #285/#325/#413 (inventory-membership defects - different artifact pair and consequence), #195 (dead code): none covers live violations of existing bans created by the enrollment.
**Reproduction Steps**:

### 421. [RESOLVED 6afecbdba] Git Failures Inside Process Substitutions Are Invisible to `set -euo pipefail` - the Clean-Exact-Commit Assertion Can Pass Without Scanning, and Deleted-File Sweeps Can Silently Skip

Three guards degrade silently: (a) if `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard -z` fails after the two preceding diff probes succeeded (transient index.lock appearance, IO error), the exact-commit assertion returns success WITHOUT having examined any untracked path - weakening the guarantee that acceptance/mutation/fuzz-nightly/pack/release commands certify a fully enumerated clean tree; (b) a failed for-each-ref yields a clone missing remote-tracking refs, shifting later coverage/baseline comparisons onto an impoverished ref namespace; (c) a failed `git diff --diff-filter=D` leaves worktree-deleted files present in the "exact source snapshot" that tar then certifies. Fail direction: vacuous pass of a security-relevant precondition plus snapshot divergence, both without diagnostic. Distinct from #258 (clone-ref MAPPING semantics of the same refs loop - which refs must exist for detached CI checkouts; orthogonal to failure visibility), #339 (dev dispatch order - entirely different mechanism). No entry covers process-substitution failure blindness.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Statically: in a scratch copy replace a substitution body with `(exit 7)` (e.g., `done < <(exit 7)` for the untracked scan) and invoke the enclosing function: the while loop is skipped and the function exits 0; no set -e/pipefail arm fires (bash async-subshell status discarded).
2. Dynamically for (a): start a `tooling acceptance` run and, in the window between the Line-80 diff probes and the Line-98 scan, create `${repo_root}/.git/index.lock`: the untracked scan produces nothing yet the run proceeds past assert_clean_exact_commit_source with untracked paths present.

**Confidence**: Medium overall (bash failure-visibility semantics certain; all three sites read; each trigger requires a mid-flow git fault after earlier git calls succeeded, so likelihood is low while severity rests on the certifying role of the weakened assertion).

The secondary error supersedes the flow: the original root-cause message never reaches the caller as the diagnosis, and the tmp+rename evidence block never runs, so no acceptance timing artifact is written at all - unlike failures after phase 1, which correctly overwrite stale evidence with failed-status records. Trap scoping makes the window real (script-scope traps apply to errors raised textually before the trap statement). The guard is self-limiting against recursion ($timingWritten flips true before the validator runs), but the damage stands: the earliest class of infrastructure failures yields neither evidence nor accurate diagnosis - the operator sees 'Acceptance outer timing interval is invalid.' for what is actually a contract-parse failure. Distinct from #333 (timing-FIXTURE coverage of the timeline authority in Test-SharpProofAcceptanceTimingFixtures.ps1 - a test-effectiveness gap whose repair would not change this runtime behavior; its fixtures always supply >=1 phase and never exercise the empty-phase arm) and #191 (backslash paths in the same file - different lines/mechanism). No entry covers trap/evidence interaction.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Temporarily append `,` to eng/acceptance/contract.json so Line 18's ConvertFrom-Json throws before Line 218 starts the restore phase.
2. Invoke `tooling acceptance`: the emitted error is 'Acceptance outer timing interval is invalid.' - not the JSON parse error - proving the trap's own validation threw over the original exception.
3. Observe artifacts/timings/: no new or moved acceptance-release.json for the run (the Set-Content/Move-Item block was never reached), unlike failures after phase 1 which produce failed-status evidence.
4. Control: introduce equivalent corruption AFTER Start-AcceptanceTimingPhase -Name 'restore': evidence IS written with status failed and the true message - isolating the empty-phase guard.
**Confidence**: High on mechanism (trap scoping confirmed; code path line-verified including RequireComplete computation and the Count<1 rejection); Low-Medium reachability (pre-first-phase infrastructure damage).
**Status**: Resolved by initializing the timing authority before contract parsing, allowing empty failed timelines, and adding a malformed-contract regression that verifies a failed timing receipt is still written.

### 423. [RESOLVED ddbb07305, 0dd33b1d9] Two Support Scripts Are Referenced by Nothing in the Repository - the Coverage-Authority Generator and the Dogfood Driver Are Unreachable Dead Weight Labeled "Required"

**Location**: `scripts\Get-SharpProofCoverageAuthority.ps1` (entire file; exports Get-CoverageAuthorityHash ~Line 240, universe digest assembly ~Line 326) and `scripts\Invoke-SharpProofDogfood.ps1` (entire file; drives /p:SharpProofDogfood=true builds, Lines 45-64). **Description**: Everything in the coverage chain consumes eng/coverage/baseline.json via Test-SharpCoverage-family scripts fed by the production-inventory authority; nothing consumes Get-SharpProofCoverageAuthority.ps1's universe hashing, and the MSBuild-side /p:SharpProofDogfood machinery (Directory.Build.targets Lines 8-34 validating the dogfood analyzer directory) has exactly one would-be driver - the orphaned script - so dogfooding can never be exercised by any automated or documented flow. Both files present themselves as load-bearing authority (one computes TCB-derived digests; the audit doc classifies them required) while edits to them are invisible to every gate: the established orphaned-evidence-tooling integrity-hazard class. Distinct from #250 (orphaned GitHubEvidenceArtifact validator MODULE - precedent, different file), #346 [RESOLVED bef564b6c] (samples runner orphaned from CI and subsequently WIRED - these two received no such wiring), #344/#282 (fuzz evidence publication/seed aliasing - different scripts). No existing entry mentions either file.
**Reproduction Steps**:

2. Repeat for Invoke-SharpProofDogfood/Dogfood: only Directory.Build.targets' property handling (unrelated to the script's existence), the audit-table row (Line 860), an architecture-test method NAME containing Dogfoods, and the script itself.
3. Hypothetical deletion check: no acceptance phase, container-command arm, or workflow references either script, so nothing fails - demonstrating they enforce nothing despite the audit doc calling them required.

**Status**: Resolved by removing both unreachable support scripts and the obsolete `SharpProofDogfood` MSBuild path; the usefulness audit records the replacement inventory/coverage path.

### 424. [RESOLVED b485b9186] Scheduled Soundness Workflows Define No Concurrency Control - Nightly/Weekly Runs Are Never Queued or Superseded While Every Other Workflow Demonstrates the Policy

**Location**: `.github\workflows\nightly.yml` (Lines 3-14: schedule cron "17 8 * * *" + workflow_dispatch, permissions, env - no concurrency key anywhere in the file) and `.github\workflows\weekly.yml` (same omission, cron "23 9 * * 1"). Contrasting siblings: ci.yml Lines 13-15, coverage.yml Lines 18-20, package-consumers.yml Lines 15-17, security.yml Lines 15-17 (all define cancel-in-progress groups), and package-consumers.yml Lines 319/362 where the publish jobs deliberately serialize with `group: sharpproof-publish-*` + `cancel-in-progress: false`.
**Description**: Neither scheduled workflow declares a concurrency group, so overlapping executions of the same heavy pipeline proceed in parallel rather than queuing or superseding: each run executes mutation sweeps, dependency audit, the full acceptance cycle, and (nightly) the fuzz campaign in its own 4-CPU container. A scheduled run still executing when a maintainer dispatches another - or when the next cron fires after a slow night - doubles runner-hours and GHA cache traffic for zero additional verification signal, since both runs verify byte-identical commits in the common case. The repository's own conventions prove omission rather than policy: every event-driven workflow defines a group, and the release-publish jobs were given explicit serialization precisely because unserialized duplicates were considered harmful. Correctness is unaffected (run-scoped COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME/artifact names prevent collisions); the damage is uncompensated waste. Distinctness: no existing entry concerns workflow-level run serialization; #331 (stale performance.json from early-throw paths), #346 (samples CI orphaning), #388 (CHANGELOG matrix overcount), #249 (runner feed seeding), #317 (MSBuild GUID churn) all concern different mechanisms.
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Open nightly.yml front matter (Lines 3-14): schedule/workflow_dispatch, permissions, env - no concurrency block anywhere in the file.
2. Compare ci.yml Lines 13-15 and package-consumers.yml Lines 360-362 establishing that the repository otherwise serializes duplicates.
3. Trigger workflow_dispatch on nightly.yml while a scheduled run is still executing: the dispatched run starts immediately in parallel (distinct run_id, so distinct COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME and artifact names - nothing fails; both consume runners simultaneously).
4. Confirm uncompensated duplication: both runs execute mutation/dependency-audit/acceptance/fuzz-nightly against the same commit, uploading equivalent evidence under different artifact names.
**Confidence**: High on facts (absence, sibling contrast, collision immunity all read directly); Medium at best on classification - some projects intentionally let scheduled flows overlap, so maintainer intent is the residual; reported for the objective internal inconsistency with the publish-job serialization.

**Status**: Resolved by adding workflow-level concurrency groups that queue duplicate scheduled or manually dispatched runs; an architecture regression covers both workflows.

### 425. [RESOLVED aaf0efe81] samples/README.md's Library Specification Row Advertised "locals" That No File in the Library Fixture Ever Declares

**Location**: `samples\README.md` Line 13 (Library row Demonstrates cell: "Multi-file decision logic with branches, multiple returns, locals, and `Old`") vs `samples\Library\VerifiedLibrary.cs` (Lines 5-10), `AccessPolicy.cs` (Lines 5-23), `QuotaPolicy.cs` (Lines 5-23) - the five annotated members contain only parameters, if-branches, multiple returns, and one parameter reassignment (`enabled = !enabled;`, QuotaPolicy.cs Lines 20-22); zero local-variable declarations of any form exist in the project (grep for var/out-var/typed locals returns only method parameters).
**Description**: samples/README.md Line 3 declares the sample projects executable package-consumer specifications, and the Library row is factually wrong on one of its four enumerated body constructs while the other three verify true (three files; branches; multiple returns in IsAuthorized/SelectApproval/SelectLimit; Contract.Old in Flip) - isolating "locals" as the sole falsehood. Blast radius: Library is the one sample whose worker verdicts are CI-enforced (exactly five Proven claims under strict via the package-consumer job), so a maintainer extending the fixture to actually exercise locals would believe the construct is already pinned by the executable spec, and a reader auditing coverage from the README over-credits what it pins. Documentation-only: the Expected-result column and runner assertions were cross-checked consistent (counts/outcomes/codes), so no runtime expectation is falsified. Git history shows original mismatch, not drift: the initial row already said "local use" while QuotaPolicy.cs was created without any local, and neither changed since. Distinct from #252 (docs advertising a nonexistent HOST-REJECTION SAMPLE - different surface and claim type: construct-category absent from an existing fixture vs an entire missing sample), #346/#389/#339 (CI orphaning/redirection/isolation - none touch the Demonstrates column), and squarely in the accepted maintained-doc-vs-artifact family of #388 (CHANGELOG enumerating environments that never run), #340/#341 (summaries stating surfaces the checked artifacts do not).
**Reproduction Steps**:
1. Read samples/README.md Line 13; note the Demonstrates cell lists "locals".
2. Read all three files under samples/Library/ in full; grep bodies for `\b(var |out var|int [a-z]|long [a-z]|bool [a-z])`: only parameter declarations match; the sole named mutable storage is the reassigned parameter `enabled`.
3. Contrast the neighboring claims of the same cell (branches, multiple returns, Old) which each verify true against the same files, isolating "locals" as the sole false enumeration.
4. Optional: run `tooling samples -PackageSource nupkgs` - green, because the runner asserts only counts/outcomes/codes, never the Demonstrates column: nothing would catch a fixture silently contradicting the README here.
**Confidence**: High (all three files and README read in full; history checked); severity explicitly Low (documentation accuracy of an executable-spec asset).
**Status**: Resolved by describing the fixture's actual parameter update instead of claiming an undeclared local variable.
